# ResLink vs us — the full gap report

*Compiled 2026-09-08 against working tree `3700f6b`. **Items 34–43 re-verified against `eb74da2`
on 2026-09-08 — see "Corrections" below before you trust a line number.***

**How this was built.** ResLink's design tool ships as a public Vite bundle at
`https://reslink.in/design/assets/index-BUje1ltR.js` (4.71 MB). No login was needed: the app
shell is served openly and only the data API sits behind phone-OTP auth. Their complete English
UI dictionary, their BOM engine, their cable-sizing tables and their three.js setup were read
directly from it. That evidence is in `docs/reslink-feature-inventory.md`. Their 11 YouTube
videos supplied the marketing claims (YouTube blocked the caption download, so the video notes
come from titles and the authors' own descriptions, not from spoken transcripts).

Our side was audited by 24 agents reading our real code, plus 13 more doing the feature-by-feature
diff, with every build gate re-run live. Raw totals before de-duplication: **187 gaps,
142 places we are ahead, 104 things we overcomplicated, 201 bugs.**

Every claim below carries a `file:line` or a quoted ResLink string. Anything unproven is marked
`[unverified]`.

---

### The 3D hyperrealism list

*2026-09-09. The owner narrowed scope to the 3D design studio, so these are worked in
the priority order the hyperrealism section sets out.*

| Item | State |
|---|---|
| **The IBL never worked** (part of 1/2) | ✅ `scene.environment` was a raw `CubeTexture` (mapping 301); PBR needs PMREM (306), so it contributed **nothing**. `<PmremEnvironment/>` converts it. Proof: glass `envMapIntensity` at 0.2, 1.0 and 20 gave byte-identical pixels; after the fix `scene.environmentIntensity` 0 → 1 moves the glass from RGB(38,75,130) to RGB(135,172,212). Note `material.envMapIntensity` is the WRONG knob for a scene environment — see the comment in `three/textures.ts`. |
| **Sun ramp** (part of 2) | ✅ `duskFactor = sunAltitude/0.25` had altitude in RADIANS, so it pinned at 1 above 14.3° and noon and 16:50 were lit identically. Now air-mass based. |
| **Shadow bias** | ✅ `NORMAL_BIAS` 0.03 → 0.007. 30 mm of bias on a 45 mm module is what made the array look like it floated. |
| **7** threshold bloom | ✅ added between AO and the tone-mapper. The threshold is measured, not chosen: at 1.1 it moved 97.3 % of the frame, at 2.0 it moves 43.7 %, at 5.0 nothing at all. The numbers are in `three/ScenePost.tsx`. |
| **9(a)** the vent spun its own base | ✅ animation removed. The GLB is a SINGLE mesh (`Mesh_0`, one material) — there is no rotor node to turn on its own, so this could not be narrowed, only removed. The procedural fallback models the parts and still spins. |
| **9(b)** square tank / dish footprint | ✅ one target per axis. A surveyed 3.0 × 1.5 m tank drew 1.5 × 1.5 m. |
| **9(c)** both legs under one edge | ✅ four legs, the back pair rising with the tilt. Read-verified only: the path runs for a tilted panel with **no** table, and a table draws its real parametric structure instead. |
| **9(d)** cells on all six faces | ✅ six materials in BoxGeometry group order — cells on +Y, backsheet on −Y, anodised frame on the four sides. `spec.bifacialityPct` switches the underside to muted rear glass, so the picture and the quote agree about which module this is. |
| **10** AO tier | ✅ measured, not guessed. `hardwareConcurrency <= 4` or a mobile User-Agent regex described no GPU at all. Now: start optimistic, time real frames, drop AO once if the median is slower than 22 ms. |
| **8** hero at print size | ✅ the capture raises the drawing buffer to a 2500 px long edge and restores it, so AO, bloom, SMAA and tone mapping all run at that resolution. Measured before: 1097 × 930, because `dpr={[1,1.5]}` clamps to the device's 1. A4 at 300 dpi wants ~2480 px. |
| **54 / 11** model weight | ✅ **168.7 MB → 8.1 MB**, in two passes. Meshes first (`scripts/decimate-glb.mjs`, meshoptimizer): 4,770,015 → 148,265 triangles, each mesh rescaled back onto its authored bounding box so the `*_REF` constants and `obstruction-assets.test.ts` are untouched. Then the textures (`scripts/shrink-glb-textures.mjs`, sharp): 2048 → 1024 px, quality chosen per image against a PSNR floor, geometry copied byte for byte. The blanket `useGLTF.preload` is already per-type. |

| **14** clamp orientation | ✅ `StructureNodesInstanced` composed every instance with a `Quaternion` that was created once and never assigned, so all hardware stood square to the WORLD while the rails ran at the table's azimuth. `lib/structure.ts` now exports `tableAxis` / `tableYawRad`, read from the table's own members, averaged as doubled angles so two rails stored head-to-tail cannot cancel. **Test-verified only** — `topologyOf` builds an elevated table solely on a roof with pitch < 0.5°, and the sample project's roof is 1.6°, so no hardware renders in it at all. |

| **13** module variation | ✅ mostly. Chasing it found that **`instanceColor` never reached the module fragment at all** — three declares the `vColor` varying in the vertex shader for `USE_INSTANCING_COLOR` but in the fragment only for `USE_COLOR`, which comes from `material.vertexColors`. So the per-module soiling, the brass SELECTED tint and the hover tint were all uploaded and discarded: **selecting a module in 3D highlighted nothing**. Fixing it needs the flag AND a white per-vertex `color` attribute, or `color_vertex` reads (0,0,0) and the modules lose their whole diffuse. Painting every instance brass now moves 30.5 % of the frame; it moved 0 % before. Also shipped: per-module soiling (23 modules, 23 distinct values, stable across reloads), soiling that ROUGHENS as well as dims (12 % of frame), and glass waviness at a measured strength (0.25 — 0.045 was inert at 0.04 %). **Open:** frame anisotropy, junction box, MC4 tail. |

| **11** plain/realistic toggle | ✅ the other half of 11. Plain mode does not draw a box over the model — `AssetBoundary` returns the fallback **without mounting the child**, so `useGLTF` never fires and the GLB is never requested; `useWarmObstructionAssets` returns early too. Measured on a roof with a water tank: realistic = 29 meshes / 9,177 tris / `tank.glb` fetched; plain = 30 meshes / 2,870 tris / no fetch, `Mesh_0` gone. Shadows unchanged both ways — solar access reads the proxies in `lib/scene-model.ts`. Also fixed the menu lying about its own state: `plainModels` and `project.obstructions` were missing from the `haloGroups` deps, so the button read "Plain" with `aria-pressed=false` while plain mode was on, and the item would not have appeared when a user added their first obstruction. `exhaustive-deps` does not flag that memo; the browser did. |
| **16** tree variety (partial) | ✅ **the two halves that were real.** Per-instance character keyed on the obstruction id — yaw, a permanent lean off plumb (±0.05 rad), sway phase and rate, and a foliage tint — so four trees no longer read as four copies of one mesh swaying in lockstep. Measured on four deliberately same-sized trees: yaw + lean alone moves **12.31 %** of the frame, the tint alone **9.73 %**, two-grab control **0.00 %**. Nothing that a surveyor typed varies (height, footprint) — the shading engine builds its solid from those, and a test pins the allow-list so a `scale` cannot be added later without someone reading the rule. Also shipped, and overdue: **`prefers-reduced-motion` now reaches the 3D scene.** All four decorative `useFrame` loops (tree sway, turbine vent, both windmills) ran regardless of the setting; the repo's three reduced-motion blocks are CSS and cannot touch a WebGL frame. **Open:** real species meshes, which need 3D art, not code — same blocker as item 15. |

**Item 16's LOD half is obsolete, and the report's own number was the reason.** The
row above says "five trees is 5.7 M triangles"; that was true of the pre-decimation
asset and is not true now. Measured from the shipped GLB's JSON chunk: `tree.glb` is
**46,239 triangles / 2.50 MB**, so five trees is **231 k triangles**, 4 % of the
figure that motivated the remedy — and the whole prop set is 8.08 MB, so the download
argument is gone outright. `<Detailed>` would also need decimated levels that do not
exist (the tree is one mesh, one primitive, one material), and an alpha billboard
would change the cast-shadow silhouette, which is the one thing an EPC scrutinises in
a shading tool. The coarse escape hatch is already built and free: the plain toggle.
**So the LOD and billboard remedy is struck, not deferred.** If tree counts ever pass
~30 the axis that bites is draw calls, not triangles, and the tool for that is
`<Instances>`/`<Merged>`.

| **15** the ladder (partial) | ✅ **a ladder is a ladder now**, not the grey box the report names. Built from its own parts — two leaning rails, level rungs at a 300 mm pitch, rubber feet — all derived from the surveyed box, so `lengthM` is the foot's stand-off, `heightM` is the climb, and the lean falls out of the two. Deliberately NOT a GLB: for a regular object made of straight sections a parametric mesh is sharper than photogrammetry (AI reconstruction turns thin rungs to sludge), it is ~200 triangles against a scanned prop's 46,000, and it is exactly the size the surveyor drew rather than scaled to it. One shared box geometry and two shared materials across the whole project, with the rungs instanced — the habit item 23 complains about. Caveat, pre-existing and unchanged: the engine still shades a ladder as a solid slab, because `lib/scene-model.ts` approximates every obstruction by its bounding solid. That is conservative, the same way it is for trees. |

| **15** the AC outdoor unit (partial) | ✅ **a new `ac_outdoor` type, not just a mesh.** The most common object on an Indian terrace could not be *recorded* before — there was no type for it, so a surveyor logged one as "Other" and it drew as a grey box. Built rather than scanned, for the ladder's reasons: it is a painted sheet-metal box. The shape is the real machine — an MS angle stand under it (an AC flat on the slab is wrong to anyone who has been on a terrace, and the gap is where the drain and the monsoon water go), one axial fan offset towards the end away from the compressor, the condenser coil L-shaped across the whole back and one end, and the service panel with its valve cover on the other. Preset is a 1.5 T on a 300 mm stand: 845 × 300 × 595 mm case (Daikin RKM50) / 835 × 295 × 555 (Voltas). Case finish is one of the three the brands actually use, picked by hashing the obstruction id. Capabilities carry the OEM clearances — bridgeable, 500 mm above, 500 mm at the service end, engineer confirmation on the load path. |
| Adding a type is now GATED | ✅ the audit behind that row found the real problem: adding an `ObstructionType` raises **three** compiler errors and opens **six silent holes**. A new type could compile and ship while being unplaceable (missing from the Step-3 palette array), drawn as a grey box (missing from the mesh switch's `default:`), and silently rewritten to `'other'` by both AI whitelists — throwing away a correct detection. Nothing in the suite enumerated the union. `lib/__tests__/obstruction-type-coverage.test.ts` now does, and it names the type it caught. Proven by deleting `ac_outdoor` from three of those sites: three tests failed, each naming it. It also pins that label prefixes are unique and that no default size is a placeholder — which is how the ladder preset was caught at `0.6 × 1.5 × 3`, i.e. 600 mm of stand-off and 1.5 m between the rails. |

**Still open on this list:** the remaining empty model folders (`building`,
`elevated`, `other` and `windmill` ship no GLB — but `building`, `elevated` and
`other` are deliberately generic types, and a massing box is the honest drawing for
them; inventing a specific mesh for "other" would be worse than the box. What is
genuinely missing is `windmill`, and the types an Indian rooftop has that we cannot
represent at all: AC outdoor unit, skylight, light pole, fence — those need new
`ObstructionType` values, not just meshes),
tree species meshes (the rest of 16, blocked on the same missing art), item 2 (soft
shadows — drei's `<SoftShadows/>` does not work with this three build; see the note at
the Canvas), and the three remaining pieces of 13 above.

**Item 12 is DONE, and the row above was wrong about what shipped.** It said the roof
carried "the satellite tile planar-projected by world XZ at 1280 px over ~93 m = 7.3
cm/texel". It did not. The roof was flat `vec3(0.66, 0.64, 0.60)` — the WALL colour —
and had been since the material was written.

The cause was not in the material at all. `lib/scene-model.ts` emitted roof solids
**inside-out**: the top cap was wound with a hard-coded `(i, k, j)` and a comment
claiming it was up-facing, but `ShapeUtils.triangulateShape` preserves the contour's
winding, a hand-traced polygon can be wound either way, and the vertices are mirrored
(EN y → three −z) on the way in, flipping handedness again. Measured on the seeded
project: all 69 vertices with `normal.y > 0.8` sat at y = 0 and all 69 with
`normal.y < −0.8` sat at y = 5.7…6.47. DoubleSide lighting hid it, because three flips
the FRAGMENT normal for back faces — but `vUpness = normal.y` reads the raw attribute,
so `smoothstep(0.4, 0.8, vUpness)` was 0 on every deck and the branch never ran.

Proven by recompiling the shader with `deck` forced to each constant: forcing `0.0` was
**byte-identical** to production, forcing `1.0` moved 17 % of the frame. A threshold
ladder then put `vUpness` at ≤ 0 everywhere. Cross-checked by patching the vertex
shader — `vUpness = objectNormal.y` matched production exactly, `= 1.0` and
`= sign(position.y)` both changed the frame, so the plumbing was fine and only the
normal was wrong.

Two commits: the winding is derived from each triangle's own normal so it cannot be got
backwards again (6 tests, 5 of which fail on the old code), and the covering then
becomes the deck ALBEDO with the photo demoted to a self-normalising stain — sharp
sample over a blurred one, so the picture's exposure divides out and the old magic
`vec4(0.46, 0.46, 0.46, 1.0)` is gone. After the fix, toggling the stain moves 18 % of
the frame and swapping the covering for flat grey moves 65 %; both were 0 % before.

**Still open in this cluster:** 34 (the on-object card — needs the three-way split,
see the correction below), 35 (a table as a first-class 2D object — the hit-test half
is done, the pick model is not), 38 (lasso, Tables mode, subtract), 41 (fill a roof
from the editor), 43 (shaded-panel slider), 77 (the sixteen missing BOM lines).

**Scope note:** mobile work in this repo is cancelled — the phone client ships native
from the HelioGrid repo. Any row here framed as a touch or 375px problem keeps its
FEATURE and drops its mobile framing. See `CLAUDE.md` and DESIGN-SYSTEM §0.

## Corrections — read before you use a line number

*2026-09-08. Items 34–43 were re-verified against the working tree at `eb74da2` before Weeks 2–6
began. Ten claims did not survive. Each is fixed in place below; this block exists so nobody acts
on a stale row from memory.*

**Line numbers moved.** Commit `23e22d9` (Week 1) shifted `screens/Step6Editor.tsx` by ~+11 in the
300–900 band and ~+45 from ~1900 onward, and `three/Scene3D.tsx` by ~+75 below line 2400.
`lib/segment-ops.ts` and `types.ts` did not move. **Re-grep before you edit.**

| Row | Was claimed | Verified truth |
|---|---|---|
| **M31 / plan 34** | "Mount `EntityLabel` in 2D — a placement gap, not a build" | **Not executable.** `three/EntityLabel.tsx:7,31` positions only through drei `<Html>`, which calls `useThree`/`useFrame` and throws outside an r3f `<Canvas>`; its `position` prop is a world triple, not a plan XY. The MARKUP is reusable, the component is not. The card also ships 28 px and 30 px buttons and 11.5 px labels — under N2 and N3 — so it cannot be re-used unchanged either. |
| **selection parity / plan 35** | The 2D tap "selects the module to the LEFT" | **Real bug, confirmed, but the direction varies.** Every catalogue module is 1048–1134 mm wide, so short-axis pitch is 1.098–1.184 m, always inside the 1.3 m radius. `planCellM` swaps the lattice axes on a pitched roof and in landscape, so it is the previous ROW there; `layoutGroup` re-appends panels, so order is not stable either. `screens/step6-erase.ts` copied the same test, so **the eraser deleted the wrong module too.** Fixed in `lib/plan-pick.ts` (`nearestPanelAt`). |
| **M13 / plan 36** | "The grow path never re-selects"; "add a direction picker" | Both partly wrong. `growSelection` **does** re-select; only the 2D edge-drag commit (`onTableDragEnd`) fails to, and only an OUTWARD grow loses the handles. The **direction picker already exists** — axis pills, side pills and a count stepper are all in the grow popover. What is genuinely missing is **panels-per-row**. |
| **M14 / plan 37** | "Deleting an interior row is impossible by any route"; "the only feedback is `−N` with no count" | **False on both.** Marquee-select a row and press Delete: `cascadeDeletePanels` → `reindexSegment` keeps the table's geometry and records the gap in `segment.removed` — exactly what a Delete Rows feature should produce. The 3D edge shrink already prints a full impact readout via `previewOp`. **The affordance is missing, not the mechanism.** |
| **M9–M11 / plan 38** | "The 3D marquee is gated on `e.shiftKey`, so a phone can select exactly one module"; "2D replaces unless shift" | **Stale — `23e22d9` fixed both.** `Scene3D.tsx` now reads `if (!(e.shiftKey \|\| boxSelect) …)` with a touch Select mode, and `Step6Editor.tsx` has a persistent `addSelect` toggle. Do not re-add a touch escape from Shift; extend those two into a three-state mode. What survives: rectangle-only (no lasso anywhere in `src/`), no Tables target, no subtract, and a real defect — `Scene3D.tsx` hardcodes `onSelectPanels?.(ids, true)`, so **a second identical 3D box silently deselects the whole block.** |
| **M6 / plan 41** | "`runAutoPlace` fills ONE roof"; "we have nothing for tap-to-fill in the editor" | **Both wrong, and the first is dangerous.** `lib/auto-design.ts` passes `avoidPanels: []` and loops every roof; `layoutAutoDesign.apply` returns whole `panels` and `segments` arrays. It **replaces the entire project and destroys every hand-placed module** — the dead dialog's own title, "Auto-fill this roof?", is a lie. A per-roof fill must pass `avoidPanels: p.panels`. And the editor is not empty of fill: **the Table tool already does an additive, collision- and obstruction-aware per-area fill on drag.** Copy that, not `autoDesign`. |
| **M27 / plan 42** | "A keepout drawn over live modules is silent" | **False, loudly.** `lib/drc.ts` carries a dedicated `panel_in_keepout` **hard error** whose own comment names this exact case, and it runs unconditionally. The keepout half is built; only the auto-disable is missing. **The genuinely silent cases are walkways and rails** — and rails are worse, because `lib/scene-model.ts` has no rail caster at all, so a 1100 mm barrier neither displaces a module nor shades one. **Rails are a separate, larger item; do not fold them in.** |
| **M28 / plan 43** | An unscored panel reads as `solarAccess === undefined`; `isShadingFresh` lives in `lib/derive/freshness.ts` | `solarAccess: number` is **required** (`types.ts:649`) and every creation site stamps an **optimistic 1.0**. A just-placed module reads as 100% sunlit and is indistinguishable from a measured one — invisible, so worse than `undefined`. There is no per-panel scored flag. `isShadingFresh` is defined in **`lib/fingerprints.ts`**; `freshness.ts` only imports it. |
| **M20 / plan 40** | Row gap and column gap are two engine capabilities the UI fails to expose | **One scalar.** `FillOptions.gapM` drives both the column step and the row step, and `ArraySegment` carries exactly one `moduleGapM` feeding both `pitchX` and `pitchY`. Two independent gaps need a **new field**, and it must be optional/lazy — `lib/fingerprints.ts` serialises the segment tuple, so an always-present field re-keys every existing project and stales its captures. |
| **§7.2 long-press**, assumed by 34, 35, 37, 41 | "Already specified, so the trigger is just policy" | Specified in `docs/DESIGN-SYSTEM.md` §7.2 and §12, **implemented nowhere** — grep for `longPress` / `long-press` across the studio returns nothing. The timer, the movement cancel, the `pointercancel` cleanup and the `navigator.vibrate(10)` are all new code, and the gesture must not fight the drag-fill on the same tool. |

Two further findings that are not corrections but change the work: **a one-module tap is a footgun**
(`classifySelection` returns `kind:'row'`, so `hasSegment` is true and the Table sheet's Delete and
Duplicate act on all 40 modules while the bar reads "1 panel"); and **the studio is entirely legacy-
styled** — `Step6Editor.tsx` holds ~100 raw hex values and only `/design` uses `.ds`, so every new
control in Weeks 2–6 is built as a token-only `.ds` island inside a legacy screen.

## The verdict in 60 seconds

- **We win on physics.** Real 8760-hour TMY/Perez engine, DSM neighbours as shadow casters, per-module caster attribution, P50/P90 (`lib/energy/report.ts`, `lib/shading.ts:208-211`). Their bundle proves the absence: no P50/P75/P90, no sub-module shading, no AI roof or obstruction detection.
- **We win on provenance and on structural honesty.** A **four-tier provenance on every BOM line** (measured / derived / estimated / assumed, `types.ts:926` makes `formula` non-optional), a recorded engineer sign-off (`screens/Step8Sld.tsx:126-147`) and a disclaimer stamped on every sheet — ResLink carries none of the three, and sells "Detailed Structure Analysis" as a paid tier. Caveat: coverage is not blanket — `lib/roof-factory.ts:131-153`, `lib/roof-map-fit.ts:100-102` and `lib/roof-ai/gemini-client.ts:82-85` all mis-stamp a tier.
- **We win on decision support.** A **six-candidate comparison matrix** that runs the full production pipeline per candidate (fill → string → 8760-hour energy → BOM → finance) with the real string-refusal message. ResLink's bundle has no equivalent.
- **They win on product surface, by a wide margin.** Their design tool is one of four products (`Proposals`, `CRM`, `Projects`, `3D Design`) and carries **512 panels / 245 inverters** against our 15 / 12 (`data/panels.ts:6-22`, `data/inverters.ts:4-17`), **17 obstruction types + 4 skylight sub-shapes** against our 11 — of which only 6 have a GLB, `windmill`'s folder holds a lone `.gitkeep`, and `elevated`/`ladder`/`other` fall back to a grey box (`three/ObstructionMesh.tsx:255-270`).
- **Nothing we make can leave the building.** No PDF file, no Excel, no GLB, no WhatsApp; the **QR and share link printed on the customer's cover page resolve for nobody** — `screens/ShareViewer.tsx:9` reads the *viewing* device's localStorage. They ship `Download PDF Report`, `Download Excel BOM`, `Share via WhatsApp`, `Copy 3D View Link`.
- **The four money bugs, all on documents a customer sees.** (1) The discount never reaches the price — `lib/fingerprints.ts:183` omits `pricing.discount` from the memo key; reproduced live, the customer is quoted **₹27,688 more** than promised. (2) `lib/sld.ts:52` hardcodes AC cable at 10 mm² — **wrong on 24 of 24 three-phase catalogue combinations** (a 120 kW plant prints 10 mm² where 150 mm² is derived), printed on the DISCOM sheet *and* the customer spec table. (3) A GST-inclusive quote is issuable at the default **12 % margin the EPC has never seen** (`screens/Step7Proposal.tsx:366-376` jumps straight to `/proposal`; `data/pricebook.ts:152`). (4) `review.issuable` is computed and consumed only by tests (`lib/review.ts:37,161`), so **"Not ready to issue" prints above an enabled button**.
- **Our hard gate is narrower than it looks.** The electrical gate covers **wizard routes only** — `router.ts:49-51` exposes `/proposal` and `/bom` ungated and `screens/Dashboard.tsx:278` navigates straight there from a project row. Claim: "the wizard cannot advance past Step 6 until the design strings." Nothing more.
- **The button we sell the product on throws away yield.** A budgeted auto-design truncates in raw lattice order (`lib/layout.ts:398`), so 11 panels landed at **58.7 % measured solar access where the best 11 sat at 100 %** — roughly 40 % of annual yield — while the log printed "91% sun access". The same engine never receives the surround grid (`lib/auto-design.ts:90`), and neither does the customer-facing heatmap (`lib/solar-heatmap.ts:237`), so the picture and the number answer different questions.
- **On a phone we are not usable.** Verified live at 390×780 on `3700f6b`: the **Next button renders 240 px off-screen on all ten steps** (`screens/Wizard.tsx:287-296`); **234 of 234 controls on the BOM screen are under 44 px**; the 2D canvas has **zero touch-gesture code** (no pinch, no pan — `components/SatCanvas.tsx`), "Delete Roof 1" is physically unreachable at 25×15 px under a colliding overlay, and there are **zero error boundaries and no loading/empty/offline states** anywhere. ResLink positions itself as "India's First Mobile-First 3D Solar Design & Proposal Software" with a six-item header and a 4-tab bottom nav.
- **Do first, in this order.** Four one-to-two-line fixes that stop wrong rupees printing — `fingerprints.ts:183`, `sld.ts:52`, the Step-7 escape hatch, wiring `review.issuable` (all **S**, payoff **high**); then pass the surround to `auto-design.ts:90` and `solar-heatmap.ts:237` (**S**); then the auto-design budget picking best-first (**M**); then `SatCanvas` touch gestures + `zoomCovering` (**M**) and the header (**S**). Hyperrealism changes no number and blocks no user — it goes after all of that.

## Their wizard vs ours

Ours: `screens/Wizard.tsx:266-275`. Theirs: routes `/wizard/0` … `/wizard/10`, `step: "Step {{current}} of {{total}}"`.

| # | ResLink step | Our step | The gap in one line |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | Project Setup | **1 Setup** | They open with `use my current location`, typed lat/long, satellite fullscreen and a **monthly GHI + temp table** ("Data: NASA POWER (2001-2020 avg)") before any design exists; we fetch `monthlyGhi` (`lib/pvgis.ts:102,127`) and never draw it, have **zero `geolocation` hits repo-wide**, and offer `residential | commercial` only against their residential/commercial/**industrial**. |
| 1 | Roof Setup | **2 Roof** | Our geometry engine is ahead (pitched L/T/U faces, DSM-measured height/pitch/azimuth) but the **canvas is hard-capped at ~93 m** (`components/SatCanvas.tsx:104-120`, `SAT_ZOOM=20`) so a C&I shed cannot be shown, and we have no reference-drawing import, no carport, no roof openings — against their 7 roof types incl. `Carport` and `Auto Roof (any shape)`. |
| 2 | Obstructions | **3 Obstructions** | **17 types + 4 skylight sub-shapes, box/lasso bulk select, mirror, array, align, two-tap ladder, per-edge sizing** vs our 11 types, single selection and 4 taps per object; we are ahead only underneath, on DSM auto-detection with measured dimensions. |
| 3 | Components | **4 Components** | **512 panels / 245 inverters + datasheet-PDF extraction + manual entry + DCR/ALMM/BIS filters + the 2× sanctioned-load rule** vs our 15 / 12 mock entries; we answer neither "is my module in here?" nor "what kWp do I quote?", though our 6-candidate comparison beats anything they have. |
| 4 | Auto Placement | **5 AutoDesign** | They give roof-priority drag-reorder, orientation, row spacing ("Prevents shading on Dec 21"), live progress narration, per-surface cards with graded % access — **and place the inverters here, ending in `Confirm & String`**; ours is a wall of text with no map, and inverter placement is four taps deep inside the *next* step. |
| 5 | Manual Edit | **6 Editor** | They edit **N tables at once with "–" for mixed values**; our Table sheet is structurally single-segment (`lib/segment-ops.ts:349-362` returns `'other'` on a two-segment selection, so `screens/Step6Editor.tsx:622-624` resolves `undefined` and the sheet never opens) — and manual stringing in 2D refuses every tap on any auto-strung design, i.e. all of them. |
| 6 | **BOM** | **9 BOM** — three steps late | **They cost the job BEFORE they quote it. We quote at step 7 and cost at step 9.** Their `Download Excel BOM` and per-line edits happen at 6; our readiness panel at Step 7 literally sends you to "Step 9" to fix pricing you have not reached (`lib/review.ts:104,115`). Their BOM also has its own error boundary and a `Skip this step`; ours has neither. |
| 7 | Proposal | **7 Proposal** | Theirs builds from **captured 3D renders** (`Tap to capture`, `Shadow Analysis`, `Capture All`) and ends in a real file plus `Share via WhatsApp`; ours ends in a browser print dialog, a dead share link, and a price that ignores the discount. |
| 8 | SLD | **8 SLD** | Our stringing engine is ahead; their **sheet** is ahead on everything a DISCOM reads — HT group (transformer/VCB/CT/PT), `Grid & Standards`, editable earth pits quoting `IS 3043: min 2 (≤3kW), 3 (>3kW)`, `SPD Type`, free `Notes`, `Reset to calculated values`, and **DXF/PDF/PNG of the SLD itself**; we export DXF of the *layout* no matter which tab is open (`screens/Step8Sld.tsx:177-183`). |
| 9 | **Layout Drawings** | **— no step of ours** | **Their step 9 has no equivalent step on our side.** We fold five sheets into a chip row inside Step 8 (`Step8Sld.tsx:212-218`), have **no Earthing & LA sheet at all** (no earth-pit or earthing-path type exists; `CableRoute.kind` declares `'earth_conductor'` and nothing emits one), no `All Sheets (PDF)`, no revision/designer/company in the title block — and our five sheets are numbered **"OF 2" across three different paper sizes**, a standard DISCOM/CEIG rejection reason. |
| 10 | Final | **10 Done** | Theirs is `Done & Return to Proposal` — the design tool hands back to a separate Proposals product; ours ends on a share link that works for nobody, but is the only one of the two that hands over an **Installation Plan** (`screens/Step10Done.tsx:67-69`) — rooftop tables only, the monorail path emits no rails or standoffs (`lib/installation.ts:97,114,130,145`). |

Two things to say plainly:

1. **Order.** They cost the job at step 6 and quote it at step 7. We quote at step 7 and cost at step 9 — so the customer's price is generated before the bill of materials the price is supposed to come from, at a 12 % margin nobody approved.
2. **Coverage.** Their Layout Drawings step is a whole step of ours that does not exist. It is also the step whose Earthing & LA sheet is a CEIG submission item on every Indian plant — and we price `safety.earth_strip` in the BOM while printing it on no sheet.

## The scoreboard

| Area | Who's ahead | Why, in one line |
|---|---|---|
| Wizard shape & parity | **ResLink** | Our 10 steps cover 9 of their 11, we price two steps after the quote escapes, and we ship **none** of their support / tour / offline / plan / skip scaffolding — but their BOM and SLD are skippable and ours are gated. |
| Roof modelling | **Split** | Our engine builds pitched L/T/U faces from DSM-measured height and pitch; their **authoring surface** wins on reference-drawing import, carport, roof openings, per-edge lock, and a canvas that is not capped at ~93 m. |
| Obstructions & surroundings | **ResLink** | **17 types + 4 skylight sub-shapes, bulk select, mirror, array, per-edge sizing** against our 11 types and one-at-a-time placement — we lead only on DSM auto-detection and real neighbourhood geometry. |
| Components & sizing | **Split** | We own decision support (6-candidate full-pipeline matrix); they own **getting a component in** — 512 panels, PDF datasheet extraction, manual entry, DCR/ALMM/BIS filters, the 2× sanctioned-load rule. |
| Auto placement & inverter placement | **Split** | Our ranking uses measured access × POA and auto-strings in one undoable patch; their step has settings, progress, per-surface cards **and inverter placement** — and our budgeted fill demonstrably keeps the **worst** panels. |
| Manual editor | **ResLink** | They edit N tables at once with mixed-value "–"; our Table sheet cannot open on a multi-segment selection, and none of our better engineering underneath is reachable with a thumb. |
| Sun path, shading & energy | **Us** | 8760-hour TMY/Perez, DSM casters, per-module attribution, string-level electrical shading, P50/P90 — against a product whose bundle contains no P50/P75/P90 at all; undermined only by the heatmap that ignores the neighbours. |
| Stringing & SLD | **Split** | Our temperature-correct series windows refuse an impossible pair **by name**; their sheet wins on HT, grid/standards, earth pits, notes and its own DXF/PDF — and three hardcodes in ours contradict our own BOM in print. |
| BOM / BOS | **Split, leaning us** | Per-string cable from real Isc and routed drop, four-tier provenance on every line, per-field overrides — against **14 fewer procurement line kinds**, no supplier field, no Excel, and pricing that happens after the proposal. |
| Proposal, sharing & export | **ResLink** | Better engineering content on our side is irrelevant when there is **no PDF file, no Excel, no WhatsApp and a share link that is dead for every recipient**, printing a price that ignores the discount. |
| 3D realism & views | **Us on technique, ResLink on delivery** | ACES + IBL + N8AO/SMAA + clearcoat glass + 4096 shadow map vs their one directional light and 2048 map — but **168.7 MB / 4,771,015 triangles with no LOD, no compression and no context-loss recovery** will not load on the phone this is sold on, and camera presets recompute from the bounding sphere every mount (`three/Scene3D.tsx:444-455`), so there is no view persistence and no saved views. |
| Ground mount & carport | **ResLink** | We have the better **engine** (tracker, bifacial, physics-solved pitch, foundations) and no **product**: you reach it by drawing a "roof" on a ~93 m canvas, so a 1 MW site cannot be traced — and **carport does not exist in our codebase at all**. |
| UI quality, mobile & platform | **ResLink** | Our own binding DESIGN-SYSTEM.md specifies a better mobile contract than they ship, and we violate nearly every clause: **Next 240 px off-screen, 234/234 controls under 44 px, no pinch, no pan, zero error boundaries**. |

## Gap register — wizard, roof, obstructions

### Wizard & shell

**Verdict.** Our 10 steps cover 9 of their 11 — we fold their whole Layout Drawings step into a chip row inside Step 8, and we price the job two steps *after* the quote can leave the building — and we ship none of their support / tour / offline / plan scaffolding; but our per-step help, universal undo, engineer sign-off and disclaimer-on-every-sheet are things ResLink demonstrably does not have.

| Gap | What ResLink does (quoted) | What we do (file:line) | Why it matters in India | Effort | Payoff |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **A dedicated Layout Drawings step with an Earthing & LA sheet** | `/wizard/9` with three sheets — `PV Array Layout`, `Earthing & LA`, `DC String Route`; guard "Add earthing paths and lightning arrestors in Step 5 (Manual Edit) to generate this drawing."; step-5 tooling `Tap points to draw earthing path. Double-tap to finish.`, `Path {{num}} — {{length}}m · {{pits}} pits`, `Pit spacing:`, LA masts at 8 m | We have 4 sheets (`Step8Sld.tsx:212-218`) but **no earthing sheet is possible**: `types.ts` has `LightningArrester` (:681) and `arresters` (:1180) and **no earth-pit or earthing-path type**; `CableRoute.kind` declares `'earth_conductor'` (`types.ts:709`) and nothing emits one. Placed arresters reach no on-screen sheet (0 hits for `arresters` in `Step8Sld.tsx`) while `lib/export-dxf.ts:131-134` does draw them | IS 3043 earthing and LA layout is a **CEIG/DISCOM submission item on every plant**. The pack an inspector receives is incomplete for scope our own BOM already prices (`safety.earth_strip`) | L | high |
| **DXF per sheet, and an All-Sheets PDF** | Step 9: `DXF (AutoCAD)`, `PDF (A1)`, `PNG (High-Res)`, and **`All Sheets (PDF)`**; Step 8 adds `Share` | `Step8Sld.tsx:170-183` — three chips: `SVG (CAD)`, `PNG`, `DXF (layout)`. SVG/PNG serialise only the **currently visible tab** (:41); the DXF chip always calls `layoutToDxf(project)` regardless of tab, so **the SLD, Cable Schedule and Structure sheets have no DXF path at all**. PDF only via the `@media print` block (:190-206), one sheet at a time; no pdf dependency in package.json | A consultant/DISCOM file is a **stapled set**. Today an EPC exports five times and assembles by hand — and cannot hand the drafter a CAD file of the SLD | M | high |
| **Skippable steps with a visible Skip control, and a per-step error boundary** | BOM boundary labelled `Bill of Materials` — "Your design is safe — nothing here has changed it… carry on without the bill and come back later" — plus `Skip this step`; SLD "Required for DISCOM approval", skippable, "Run auto-stringing in Manual Edit to draw the SLD, or skip this step." | Steps 3, 5, 7, 8, 9 **are** skippable (`Wizard.tsx:111-140` blocks only 1, 2, 4, 6) and nothing says so. One Skip control in the whole product (`Step7Proposal.tsx:146`). **Exactly one error boundary in the tree** — `three/ObstructionMesh.tsx:41`, for GLB loading; `find src/app -name 'error*'` returns nothing, so a throw in Step 9 blanks the wizard | An EPC quoting 3 kW residential **does not want an SLD** and must guess Next works; one BOM exception loses the whole session — the exact failure ResLink wrote a reassurance paragraph for | S | high |
| **In-product support attached to the step** | `Chat on WhatsApp`, `Email us`, `YouTube Tutorials`, `Tutorial: Getting Started`, `aboutStep: "About: {{step}}"` — the WhatsApp message is **pre-filled with step, project name, project id, phone and user id**; a tutorial video in 4 languages | grep for `whatsapp\|wa.me\|mailto\|youtube\|support` over `src/` returns **one hit, a demo button on the design-system page** (`src/app/design/page.tsx:52`). The Help button (`Wizard.tsx:381-389`) opens `STEP_HELP` text only — no channel, no video, no contact | **WhatsApp is how Indian EPCs get unstuck.** A message carrying step + project id is a 30-second fix versus a lost user. DESIGN-SYSTEM §1 already names WhatsApp as the primary channel and we ship it nowhere | S | high |
| **A guided tour of the header tools, with "Don't show again"** | Tour over `Save & go home`, `Save your work`, `Need help?` with `Don't show again`; also `Quick tour` | grep for `tour\|onboard\|coachmark\|walkthrough` across screens/components/store returns **zero**. The only first-run affordance is Step 8's intro Dialog gated on `project.derived.sldIntroSeen` (`Step8Sld.tsx:26, 224-232`) — one step, and **per-project, not per-user** | Our header carries **ten icon-only controls** whose only labels are `data-tip`/`title` — unreachable on touch. Without a tour the chrome is undiscoverable on the device we target | S | medium |
| **Offline operation and a visible save/sync state** | `Offline`, `loadedFromCacheOffline: "Loaded from cache (offline)"`; drawing sync `Saved to your account` / `On this device only` / `Saving…`; boot "watchdog + self-repair"; `webglcontextlost`/`restored` → "Recovering 3D view..."; zoom guard "Pinch two fingers together to zoom back out." | grep `navigator.onLine\|offline\|serviceWorker` over the feature root → **zero product hits**; no manifest file anywhere. We **do** persist with debounced dirty-key writes (`store/store.tsx:412-451`) and **do** compute `persistStatus: 'ok'\|'quota'\|'error'` + `retryPersist` (:351-359, 447, 497) — with **zero consumers in any screen** | A rooftop in a **tier-2 town has no signal**. We are technically offline-capable and tell the user nothing — and when the save fails on quota we stay silent | M | high |
| **A plan model that degrades to read-only instead of blocking** | `/api/me/plan` cached in `reslink_plan_cache_v1`; `readOnlyPlanLimit: "Read-only · Plan limit"`, `locationLocked: "Location locked — upgrade to edit"`, `proOnlyGroundMount: "Pro only · Ground Mount"`, `exportNotOnPlan`, `planExpired`, `onFreePlan`; over-limit projects stay viewable: "You can still view 3D, export, or see the proposal from the menu."; `Upgrade` in the header on every step | No entitlement code exists. Meanwhile `Step1Setup.tsx:155-171` renders a `Ground Mount Project` card with **`BETA` and a `PRO` lock badge** whose control is a bare `<div className="toggle" />` — no onClick, no role, no input — and whose copy "Open access / PPA — no sanctioned load required" is **verbatim ResLink's string** | We **advertise a PRO tier and a project type that do not exist** on the first screen. Read-only-instead-of-block is what keeps a lapsed customer's data usable and their renewal likely | L | medium |
| **Project list: sort by system size, four statuses, employee filter, Export 3D, ground-mount badge, survey type** | Sort by last updated / newest / name / **system size** · status `Draft`, `In progress`, `Design ready`, `Proposal ready` · **filter by employee** (multi-select, select-all, search) · row menu `Show PDF`, `Show 3D`, `Export 3D`, delete · `Ground mount` badge · `openingSurvey` | `Dashboard.tsx:47` sorts `'updated' \| 'name'` only; **`ProjectStatus = 'in_progress' \| 'proposal_ready'`** (`types.ts:21`) — two values; row menu (:275-280) has no 3D export — grep for `GLTFExporter` across three/, screens/, lib/ returns **zero** | An EPC with 40 live jobs **sorts by kWp to find the C&I ones** and filters by the rep who owns them. Two statuses mean the pipeline cannot express sent/viewed/accepted — the Dashboard is a file list, not a board | M | medium |
| **Inverter placement and "Confirm & String" inside the Auto Placement step** | `Place Inverters` → `Tap to place Inverter {{number}}` → "Tap on a wall or obstruction surface" / `TAP A WALL` → "All inverters placed! Drag to reposition." → **`Confirm & String`**; hint 'Orbit to find a wall, then tap "Place"' | Our Step 5 (`Step5AutoDesign.tsx`) only picks a `DesignObjective` and runs `layoutAutoDesign`; **inverter placement is a tool inside Step 6** (`I inverter`, `Wizard.tsx:88`). Auto-placement finishes and hands back an un-inverter-ed, unwired design with no prompt | We then **hard-block Next on Step 6** until strings exist (`Wizard.tsx:126-136`) — the user is stopped by a gate on the step *after* the one that should have walked them through it | M | medium |
| **A post-wizard route and a client-facing share/replay surface** | `"/wizard/0"`…`"/wizard/10"`, **`"/wizard/complete"`**, `"/share/:shareId"`, `"/replay/:sessionId"`; the shared 3D view is server-minted and revocable — "This design link has expired or been revoked." | `router.ts:38-51`; **`'bom'` (:49) is a dead route** — no `/bom` page exists and nothing consumes it. Our share resolves from **the viewing device's localStorage** (`ShareViewer.tsx`), so `Step10Done.tsx:23`'s `${location.origin}/share/${project.shareId}` **is dead for every recipient** | Step 10's headline deliverable — the share link — **works only for its author**, and we route a name that renders nothing | L | high |
| **Complete, consistent drawing title blocks** | An A1 sheet with a title block carrying company name, designer name, drawing title, project, client, plant capacity, site address, **coordinates**, total panels, module model, **inverter model**, date, **revision**, sheet number; plus `Project Info Table`, `System Configuration Table`, `Typical Table Arrangement`, `Typical Earthing Detail`, `Cable Specifications`, editable `+ Add Note` | **Two competing title blocks in one file**: the SLD hand-rolls PROJECT/CLIENT/DRAWING/SHEET/DATE/BY (`Step8Sld.tsx:599-615`), LayoutSheet uses shared `TitleBlock` with **no DATE and no BY** (:741-750). `grep -i revision` → 0 hits. No company name (`AppUser.companyName` never reaches a sheet), no coordinates, no inverter model, no notes | A drawing with **no revision and no designer cannot be reissued or signed** — and our three sheets disagree about what a title block even contains | S | medium |
| **Industrial as a distinct site type** | Step 0: "site type residential / commercial / industrial" | `types.ts:19` — **`SiteType = 'residential' \| 'commercial'`** | Half our declared scope is C&I; industrial differs on **tariff, subsidy eligibility, HT connection and works-contract GST**. Folding them removes the hook every downstream rule hangs on | S | low |

**We already beat them at**

- **A wizard that cannot advance past Step 6 until the design actually strings** — `Wizard.tsx:126-136` (`electricalGate`) plus `allowedStep` (:208-216), which force-navigates back to the first unsatisfied step. ResLink's SLD is explicitly skippable and their BOM ships `Skip this step`. **Caveat, and it is a big one:** the gate covers **wizard routes only** — `router.ts:49-51` exposes `/proposal` and `/bom` as ungated top-level routes and `Dashboard.tsx:278` navigates straight to `/proposal` from the row menu on any project, so the quote escapes through the side door.
- **Per-step help with real operating content** — `Wizard.tsx:37-108` gives every step a purpose paragraph plus concrete tips, including the full Step 6 shortcut set. Theirs is `aboutStep: "About: {{step}}"` plus a YouTube link.
- **Undo/Redo in the wizard chrome on all ten steps, labelled with the operation** — `Wizard.tsx:145-183` + the Cmd/Ctrl+Z handler at :219-237. ResLink has undo only inside Step 2 bulk toasts and Step 5.
- **A Design Health score with band, explanation sheet and a provisional marker** — `Wizard.tsx:196-203`, chip at :320-358, backed by `memoizedHealth`/`describeHealthCode`. Nothing comparable in their dictionary.
- **Recorded structural engineer sign-off, and a disclaimer on every exported sheet** — `Step8Sld.tsx:126-147` writes `project.structuralVerification`; `drawingFootnote(project)` is stamped at :617, :746, :808. They sell "Detailed Structure Analysis" as a tier and carry no sign-off state or drawing disclaimer.
- **Two sheets their Layout Drawings step does not have** — Cable Schedule (`CableScheduleSheet.tsx`, paginated at `MAX_ROWS = 34`) and Structure (`components/drawing/StructureSheet.tsx`).
- **An Installation Plan handed over at Final** — `Step10Done.tsx:67-69` → `InstallationSheet.tsx`. **Rooftop tables only**: `lib/installation.ts` filters `roof_anchor`, `front_leg`, `back_leg`, `rafter`, `purlin`, `brace` (:97, 114, 130, 145) — **the monorail path emits no rails and no standoffs**, while the BOM bills both.
- **A layered, true-metre DXF carrying setting-out data for the steel** — `lib/export-dxf.ts:17-63`, 13 named layers including `structLegs` ("setting-out points") and `structFootings`.
- **Duplicate-project from the row menu, and an honest quarantine banner for unreadable saves** — `Dashboard.tsx:78-86` and :103-119.
- **m/ft switch on every wizard step**, not just the drawing step — `Wizard.tsx:359-362`. Theirs is evidenced only inside Step 1.

### Roof

**Verdict.** Our roof **geometry engine** is ahead of theirs — real straight-skeleton pitched faces over L/T/U/plus footprints, and DSM-measured height/pitch/azimuth, both of which ResLink either gates at PRO or does not have — but the roof **authoring surface** is behind on almost everything an EPC touches: no reference-drawing import, no carport, no roof openings, no ridge height, no per-edge lock, no parapet validation, and a satellite canvas hard-capped at ~90 m that cannot even show a C&I shed.

| Gap | What ResLink does (quoted) | What we do (file:line) | Why it matters in India | Effort | Payoff |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ★ **Reference drawing — import a DXF or PNG/JPG site plan, scale it by two taps, trace on top** | "Satellite image out of date? Add your own site plan or CAD layout, set it to the right scale, and trace the roof on top of it." · `Set scale` · move / rotate / width / opacity / lock / centre · `Text & dimensions` · DXF parsed client-side with `dxfStats: "{{lines}} lines drawn, {{skipped}} items skipped."`, `errorDxfBinary` ("That is a binary DXF. Save it as an ASCII DXF"), `errorDxfNoGeometry`, `errorDxfUnusableSize` ("Check its units in CAD"), caps DXF < 8 MB / images < 25 MB / 12 MP | **Zero underlay support.** `components/SatCanvas.tsx:236` renders exactly one hardcoded `<img src={staticSatelliteUrl(...)}>` and nothing can be layered under the SVG. **DXF is write-only**: `lib/dxf.ts:1-10` is "a minimal AC1015 ASCII DXF **writer**"; `lib/export-dxf.ts:49` `layoutToDxf`. No import path exists anywhere | The EPC holding **the architect's DWG**, or working a tier-2/3 site where Google imagery is 3-5 years stale, re-traces the whole building by hand. This is the one feature ResLink **charges** for on this step, and combined with our ~90 m cap it is why a 120 m factory cannot be modelled here at all | L | high |
| **Imagery wide enough to hold a C&I roof, at the resolution the 3D already uses** | Their step assumes imagery covering the site; the reference drawing exists as the escape hatch when it does not. `Auto Roof (any shape)` is sold for "L-shaped / complex footprints" | `lib/maps.ts:9` `SAT_ZOOM = 20`; `SatCanvas.tsx:105` uses a 640 px tile at `scale` default 1. At 20°N that is **89.8 m edge to edge** (83.9 m Delhi, 93.1 m Chennai) — pan past it and the canvas is empty. `three/Scene3D.tsx:2609` fetches **the same tile at `scale = 2`** and :2622 calls `zoomCovering(...)`; **`zoomCovering` is exported at `lib/maps.ts:62` and SatCanvas never calls it** | Half our declared scope is C&I. A **120 × 80 m shed has no picture to trace on**, and the surface where a 0.4 m vent is placed runs at half the resolution of the 3D behind it. Two-line fix that unblocks the C&I half of the product | S | high |
| ★ **Carport as a first-class roof type with real structural parameters** | `Carport` in the roof-type list · `clearHeight` "Ground to the underside of the beam at the low edge — what a van needs to pass under. Stays fixed when you resize the outline or change the tilt." · `carportTilt` "Carports stay shallow — the high edge and the wind load both climb fast with tilt." · `Low edge faces toward` · `postSpacing` "One post every two bays (5.5 m) is the usual." · `postLayout`: **Centre (T-frame) / One edge (cantilever) / Both edges (H-frame)** · readouts `Deck at low edge`, `Deck at high edge`, **`Parking spaces (standard car)`**, `Posts` · `cars.glb` shipped | `types.ts:158` — **`RoofType = 'rcc_flat' \| 'metal_shed' \| 'tile' \| 'ground'`**, no carport. The Roof Type sheet offers RCC Flat / Metal Shed / Tile / Ground Array / Gable / Hip (`Step2Roof.tsx:1921-2010`). No clear-height field, no post layout, no post count, no parking readout anywhere in src. `lib/structure.ts` models legs/rafters/purlins/rails and **has no post-and-beam carport frame** | Carports are a large, growing slice of Indian C&I (malls, IT parks, factories, schools) and are **sold on the parking-space count, not the kWp**. We cannot model, price or draw one. Their clear-height-stays-fixed behaviour is the first thing a customer asks about | L | medium |
| ★ **Roof openings — cut a hole (Free shape / Rectangle / Circle) with a full rejection vocabulary and parapets on the openings** | `Cut an opening` with editable side length and diameter; rejections `too-small`, `self-intersecting`, `outside-roof`, `overlaps-opening`, `wrong-roof-type` ("Only a flat concrete roof can have an opening"), `roof-shrinks-past-opening`, `no-room-for-parapet`; openings carry their own `Parapet wall` | `types.ts:178-217` — **`Roof.polygon: XY[]` is a single ring; there is no inner-ring field.** `lib/roof-factory.ts:84` `sanitizeRoofPolygon` validates one ring only. grep `opening\|innerRing\|courtyard\|atrium` → zero feature hits. A central lightwell must be faked as a `building` obstruction | **Courtyards, lightwells, stairwell voids and atria are everywhere** on Indian institutional, hospital and older residential buildings. Faked as an obstruction it gets the wrong keepout, the wrong caster, the wrong parapet and the wrong civil BOM — and the 3D draws a solid box where a hole should be | L | medium |
| **Ridge height as a first-class, coupled input, plus the low-edge-at-ground warning** | `Height from Ground`, `Eave Height` ("The ridge is computed from this, the pitch and the width"), `Ridge Height` ("Stays fixed when you resize the roof or change the slope"), `lowEdgeAtGroundWarning` | We **compute ridge height correctly and never show or accept it**: `lib/roof-plane.ts:101` `surfaceHeightAt()`. The only height control is `SliderRow label="Height from Ground"` (`Step2Roof.tsx:2027-2043`) — **eave only, min 2 m, no numeric field**; `ridge` in that file returns only `ridgeMode`/`ridgeAngleDeg` for orientation | An Indian surveyor on a pitched roof **measures the ridge**, off the parapet or the neighbouring terrace — the eave is the number they cannot reach. Eave-only entry makes them do trigonometry on a phone, and **the municipal height limit is written against the ridge** | M | high |
| ★ **Per-edge lock/unlock with a real constraint solver, and Snap to 90° as a visible control** | `bothEdgesLocked: "Both edges locked — unlock one to move this corner"`, `unlockAdjacentEdge: "Unlock an adjacent edge to snap to 90°"`, `Snap to 90°` | Our lock is **per-roof and session-only**: `Step2Roof.tsx:147` `useState<ReadonlySet<string>>(new Set())` — **there is no `locked` field on `Roof`** in `types.ts:178-217`, so navigating away silently unlocks a surveyed roof. **No per-edge lock at all.** Our 90° snap is `ortho`, a boolean defaulting **false** (:154), applied **only while drawing** (`snapDraft`, :712-758); after close, vertex drag snaps to other roofs' vertices/edges (:702), never to 90° | Almost **every Indian rooftop is rectilinear**. ResLink lets a user nail down the two dimensions they measured with a tape and drag the rest; **one stray drag skews a roof we already measured**, and the lock is forgotten the moment they leave the step | M | high |
| **Type a length, then click to place the point** | `typedLengthHint: "{{value}} {{unit}} — click to place"` | `Step2Roof.tsx:1050-1074` `handleClick` — in draft mode the only input is a tap position through `snapDraft`. **No keyboard-length buffer, no bearing entry.** Numeric length editing exists only *after* the roof is closed (`beginEdgeEdit` :895-904 → `EdgeLengthInput` :3139) | **A surveyor works from a tape, not a photo.** Typing "18.4" then tapping the direction is how a measured building gets in exactly; tracing pixels and correcting four edges afterwards is four times the work and never closes cleanly | S | high |
| **Mounting settings on the roof step** | `Monorail Settings`, `Standoff Height`, `Rails / Panel`, `flushMountNote: "Flush-mount: follows roof slope"`, `Edge Setback (m)` — all on Step 1 | Only Edge Setback is here (`Step2Roof.tsx:2161-2171`, plus per-edge overrides at :2172-2222 — we are ahead there). **Standoff height is a hardcoded module constant with no UI**: `lib/structure.ts:790` `const STANDOFF_HEIGHT_M = 0.1;`. grep `standoff\|monorail\|railsPerPanel` across Step2Roof / Step6Editor / StructEditPanel → one prose comment, no control | On a metal shed standoff height and rails-per-panel decide **rail metres, fastener count and sealing-washer count — three BOM lines**. The user cannot change 0.1 m and the rail bill is computed from a number nobody can see | M | medium |
| **3D roof editing on the roof step** | `tap3DHint: "Tap a building to edit its height"` and, honestly, `editRoofIn2DOnly: "Edit this roof in the 2D view"` for what 3D cannot do | Step 2's 3D is **read-only and unmounts the editor**: `Step2Roof.tsx:1144-1146` `return <Scene3D … initialViewMode="mesh" stage={2} />`. We **do** have the roof card in 3D (`three/Scene3D.tsx:2968-3000`, `EntityLabel` with name, area, eave, height source, pitch, provenance, aerial reading) **and it carries a "Use map height" action** — but that action list is gated `onPlaceKind && !meshMode` (:2990), and mesh mode is exactly what Step 2 opens (:1131) | The owner asked for controls on **2D and 3D**. On the roof step we ship neither an editor in 3D nor the boundary sentence ResLink writes when they decline. **The fix for half of it is changing one string literal and dropping `!meshMode` from one gate** | S | high |
| **Parapet validation and per-edge parapet height** | Stated validation: "parapet height 0–3 m, parapet width 0–1 m"; openings carry their own `Parapet wall`, with `no-room-for-parapet` as a rejection | `Step2Roof.tsx:2247-2274` — parapet Height and Width are bare `<input type="number" step={0.1}>` with **no min, no max and no NaN guard**, writing `Number(e.target.value)` straight through. No parapet clamping anywhere in lib/ (`lib/drc.ts` has zero parapet hits). `ParapetWall.perEdge` is `boolean[]` with **one `heightM`/`widthM` for the whole roof** (`types.ts:160-169`) | Indian terraces routinely have a **1.2 m street-side parapet and a 0.4 m side wall** — we model one height for all four. Parapet feeds shading, the caster set and the civil BOM, so a fat-fingered "30" builds a **30 m wall that destroys the yield and the quote**; select-all-backspace (the normal phone gesture) sets it to 0 with the toggle still reading ON | S | high |

Trace-time edge labels are a 46×16 px chip at `fontSize={9}` that self-hides below 30 px of edge length (`components/EdgeLabels.tsx:28, 88-100`) | **"Is this roof big enough for 10 kW?" is the question being answered while tracing.** Making them close the shape first, then read a 12.5 px chip, is one more round trip on a roof in the sun | S | medium |
| **An explicit multi-roof list with Add New Roof / Copy / Delete and an Active marker** | `All Roofs ({{total}})` list with `Active` marker, `Copy roof`, `Add New Roof`, `Delete roof` | We have **chips, not a list**: `Step2Roof.tsx:1621-1698` — each chip is a 30 px `.chip` holding three stacked buttons (select / lock / delete). Duplicate lives elsewhere, on the selected-roof rail (:1746-1763). **There is no "Add New Roof" affordance at all** — adding a roof means opening the Halo and picking Draw | A mixed **RCC + tin-shed site** is our stated scope and their flagship demo. Ours is three sub-44 px targets in one 30 px pill, delete 6 px from select (measured 25×15 px, 375 px² against 1936 required), and the primary add action is **not on screen** | S | medium |
| **Numeric edge editing that respects the user's unit and the whole polygon** | `Edit Edge Length` with increase/decrease and a max-length error, alongside the **m/ft unit switch** | We have it (`Step2Roof.tsx:895-934` + :3139-3199) but it is **metric-only and single-vertex**: it writes raw METRES into the box, renders a hardcoded `m` (:3195), validates "between 0.2 and 500 m" (:915), and `commitEdgeEdit` moves **only the edge's end vertex** (:922-930) instead of re-solving the neighbours. No +/− steppers | For an imperial user this is **not a gap, it is data corruption** (see the bug register). For a metric user, fixing one measured wall **skews the two beside it** — which is exactly why per-edge locks exist on their side | S | high |

**Owner's named items on this step.** The **reference drawing (DXF/PNG import)** is the single biggest hole in the roof step and the one thing they charge for: we can only *write* DXF (`lib/dxf.ts:1-10`), and with the ~90 m imagery cap there is no path at all to a large C&I building. **Roof openings** need a data-model change first — `Roof.polygon` is one ring (`types.ts:178-217`), so this is an L, not a UI job. **Carport** is a new roof type *and* a new structure family in `lib/structure.ts`, sold on parking spaces. **Per-edge locking** is cheap by comparison and pays immediately, because `Roof` needs one new persisted field and the drag path already has a snap hook (`Step2Roof.tsx:660-703`).

**We already beat them at**

- **Pitched faces over any footprint including reflex corners** — L, T, U and plus/cross all build and tile exactly, via a real straight-skeleton wavefront with edge *and* split events (`lib/skeleton-wavefront.ts:1-14`); `lib/roof-skeleton.ts:298-306` gates on a 1 % area-tiling residual and refuses with a reason rather than shipping a wrong roof. Proven in `lib/__tests__/roof-skeleton.test.ts:112, 217, 235, 255`. **ResLink sells the same idea as `Auto Roof (any shape)` and charges PRO for it.**
- **AI roof detection with measured height, pitch and azimuth from Google's DSM**, presented as reviewable ghosts nothing enters the project without — `lib/roof-ai/pipeline.ts` + `plane-fit.ts`, review UI at `Step2Roof.tsx:1447-1533`. **Their bundle proves they have no AI roof detection at all.**
- **A provenance system on roof height/pitch/facing**, with the aerial map offered as a one-tap correction and the disagreement shown — `types.ts:207-210` `heightSource`, `Step2Roof.tsx:2044-2069`, `three/Scene3D.tsx:2976-2986`. **Claim the system, not the coverage:** `makeRoof` never sets `heightSource` (`lib/roof-factory.ts:131-153`), a sub-5° slope has its *median* height stamped `aerial_map` (`lib/roof-map-fit.ts:100-102`), and a Gemini obstruction carries a confidence score on a preset height (`lib/roof-ai/gemini-client.ts:82-85`).
- **Per-edge setbacks** drawn live as a true polygon-clipping inset, not a circle approximation — `types.ts:198`, `Step2Roof.tsx:2172-2222`, cached inset at :2792-2801. They have one uniform `Edge Setback (m)`.
- **A destructive-change impact dialog before roof geometry edits land** — `Step2Roof.tsx:574+`, :1855-1883, itemised as panels / obstructions / walkways / rails / arresters / inverter placements.
- **Site calibration from a known distance, plus a north offset** — every polygon rescales together in one undoable patch (`Step2Roof.tsx:1537-1587`, `CalibrateDialog` :3203-3290). Their two-point scaling exists only inside the reference-drawing overlay, never for the site itself.
- **An exact vertex coordinate inspector** (east/north metres, commit on blur/Enter) — `Step2Roof.tsx:2602-2690`. They offer edge lengths only.
- **Face-group coupling that keeps a converted gable/hip buildable** — `types.ts:201-206`, `lib/roof-face-group.ts`; `lib/roof-gable.ts:136-146` refuses an asymmetric footprint with "The two halves would meet the ridge 0.16 m apart" and names Hip as the fix.
- **Covering and shape are separate facts** (`types.ts:140-158`), so a metal shed converted to a gable stays a metal shed and does not re-price from clamps to hooks. Their seven "roof types" conflate covering, shape and structure in one list.
- **Mangalore / clay tile as a covering with its own mounting hardware** — `types.ts:158`, `Step2Roof.tsx:1935-1949`. Absent from their seven roof types entirely, and a very large share of Indian residential.
- **A roof drawn inside another is auto-recognised as a mumty / stair room** and stacked above its parent, with the height sync keeping the child riding it — `Step2Roof.tsx:764-770`, `lib/roof-factory.ts:138`, `lib/roof-map-fit.ts:206-215`.

---

### Obstructions & surroundings

**Verdict.** Badly behind on productivity and on-step tooling — they have **17 types, box/lasso bulk select, mirror, array, align, two-tap ladder, per-edge sizing, a sun/solar-access panel and a full height chain on this very step**; we have 11 types, single selection, one-at-a-time placement at four taps each, and a 3D button that opens the one view mode which strips the sun, the gizmo and the measure tools — while being meaningfully ahead underneath, because we detect raised objects from the DSM with measured dimensions, import the whole neighbourhood as real shading geometry, and can say what one tree costs in kWh/yr.

| Gap | What ResLink does (quoted) | What we do (file:line) | Why it matters in India | Effort | Payoff |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ★ **17 obstruction types vs our 11** — missing AC Unit, Keep-out Zone, Light Pole, Transformer Pad, Fence, Lightning Arrester, Skylight | "17 obstruction types: Water Tank · AC Unit · Tree · Chimney · Dish/Antenna · Elevated Roof · Nearby Building · Other · Solar Water Heater · Ladder · Windmill · Keep-out Zone · Light Pole · Transformer Pad · Fence · Lightning Arrester · Skylight" | `types.ts:220-231` — exactly `tank \| dish \| chimney \| tree \| elevated \| building \| solar_wh \| ladder \| windmill \| turbine_vent \| other`; palette at `Step3Obstructions.tsx:68-80`. **Mesh coverage is worse than the type count:** of the 11, **6 have a shipped GLB** (tank, tree, chimney, dish, solar-wh, turbine-vent); **`windmill.glb` is declared at `three/ObstructionMesh.tsx:51` and its folder holds only `.gitkeep`** (procedural fallback at :234-243, with `WINDMILL_REF = {x:1,z:1} // placeholder`); `building` gets a tinted box (:194-204); **`elevated`, `ladder` and `other` all fall through to the same grey box** (:255-270). Keepout and arrester exist in the model (`types.ts:632`, ops `keepout.add` / `arrester.add`) but **only from Step 6** (`Step6Editor.tsx:736, 858`) | A real RCC terrace has **2-4 split-AC outdoor units** and a railing; a factory shed has **light poles and a transformer pad**. All become "Other" — a grey box with no name, so the installer's drawing says OB3 where it should say AC-1. And the engineer standing on the roof — the one moment they can see the fire lane — is not offered the keep-out zone | S for the 6 flat types (palette row + preset + icon; `OBSTRUCTION_PRESETS` at `lib/roof-factory.ts:196-211` is the only structural edit) · M to also surface keepout + arrester here | high |
| ★ **Skylight as a first-class type with four sub-shapes** | "`Flat glass`, `Pitched` (hip glass roof), `Tilted` (mono-pitch), `Arched` (curved barrel vault) — with ridge height, tilt angle, tilt direction, arch rise, base height" | No skylight type (`types.ts:220-231`); `ObstructionShape` is **`'rect' \| 'circle'` only** (:233). A skylight must be faked as `other` → solid grey box (`three/ObstructionMesh.tsx:255-271`) casting a **solid box shadow** (`lib/scene-model.ts:244-248`) | **North-light and monitor-roof sheds — the dominant Indian factory roof — are half skylight.** Modelling one as opaque both over-states shading and hides that it is a **fall hazard you must never walk or mount on**. We already have the vocabulary (`mustRemainOpenToSky`, `types.ts:271`) and no type that uses it | M | medium |
| **Ladder placed by two taps — base then top** | "`Tap where the ladder base sits` → `Tap where the ladder top rests` → confirm or reset." | `Step3Obstructions.tsx:159-172` `place(m)` — **one tap, one centre**, preset [0.6, 1.5, 3] (`lib/roof-factory.ts:207`). Shipped as **BETA** (:87) and rendered as the default grey box (no `case 'ladder'` in `ObstructionMesh.tsx`) | A ladder's whole meaning is that it **spans two heights**. As a grey box it neither looks like a ladder nor tells the crew where roof access is — and we label it BETA, which reads as "we know" | S (two-point capture already exists in `components/MeasureTool.tsx:45-58`) | medium |
| **Per-edge numeric sizing with an on-canvas edge map** | `canvasEdgeMap: "On canvas: A·C = Length, B·D = Width"`, `edgeLengthsPick: "Edge lengths ({{unit}}) — pick an edge"`, `Edit edge {{letter}}` | `Step3Obstructions.tsx:564-615` — **three bare number fields** labelled Length / Width / Height plus a Rotation slider (:616-624). **Nothing on the canvas says which drawn edge is "Length"**; `EdgeLabels.tsx` is mounted for roofs only (:824), never for obstructions | Once a tank is rotated 37° there is **no way to tell whether the 2.0 m you are typing runs toward the parapet or along it**. A surveyor works from a tape and a lettered sketch; we make them guess and watch the shape jump | S | medium |
| **A Height Info panel showing the whole placement chain, with Casts shadow / Blocks placement flags** | `Placement:`, `Base surface:`, `Tree height:`, `Building height:`, `Object height:`, `Surface height:`, `Top from ground:`, plus `Casts shadow` and `Blocks panel placement` | `Step3Obstructions.tsx:729-752` — **three rows only**: Placement, Base surface, Top from ground. **The object's own height is not in it** — it lives in a different sheet (:584-592). Our shadow/placement flags are ToggleRows (:641-650), i.e. **editable rather than read-out — better than theirs** | The single number that drives every shadow is **the one number missing from the panel that explains the height chain**, and reading it costs a sheet close plus a sheet open | S (one row; already in scope as `selected.heightM`) | medium |

Stacking an object on an object needs a three-level conversion | S for an override row · M to anchor to another obstruction | medium |

One id.** The hit test (:318-322) returns a single object and :322 overwrites the selection. **No marquee, no lasso, no additive modifier, no select-all** | **This is the root of every other bulk gap** — until a set can be selected, mirror/array/align/bulk-delete have nothing to operate on. It is also the single biggest reason a **20-vent tin shed is unusable** on our product | M | high |

Bulk delete, bulk change, each with an inline `Undo` / `Dismiss` toast." | `Step3Obstructions.tsx:174-189` copies **exactly one** object at a fixed 2 m offset; :191-202 deletes **exactly one**. **The ops kernel has no bulk obstruction op** — the complete list is `obstruction.duplicate / move / remove / rotate / setCastsShadow` (`lib/ops/site-ops.ts:72-138`) plus `obstructions.addFromMap` | Their "N skipped — no roof under the copies" is the detail that shows this was built by watching users. Our duplicate **does** re-resolve the copy's anchor (:185), so the plumbing for a safe bulk copy exists — **the selection and the confirm loop are what is missing** | M (once bulk select lands) | high |
| ★ **Mirror a selection across a drawn line or a tapped roof edge** | "Mirror — pick a mirror axis by drawing a line (two taps) or tapping a roof edge, then `Mirrored {{count}} obstructions`." Their tin-shed video sells it: "mirror roof sections to avoid redrawing" | **Zero.** No mirror op in the registry (no mirror id anywhere in `lib/ops/*.ts`), no mirror control in `Step3Obstructions.tsx` | Indian **gable and north-light sheds are near-symmetric by construction** — vents, chimneys and monitor openings repeat on both slopes. Their marketing leads with this because it **halves the survey**; we would make the engineer place the second half by hand | M | high |
| ★ **Array — rows × columns with independent gaps, direction, flip, off-roof warning** | "Array — Columns × Rows, gap between columns, gap between rows, direction, `Centre-to-centre: {{across}} × {{down}} {{unit}}`, `Grow the other way`, `Rows on the other side`, and a warning that N copies would land off the roof." | **Zero.** No array op, no grid-repeat control. The only repeat path is `duplicateSelected` (:174-189) — one copy at a time at a fixed +2 m/+2 m offset with **no spacing input** | **Eight turbine vents on a purlin line is the most common repeated object on an Indian metal shed.** Today that is 8 × 4 taps to place + 8 × 4 taps to size ≈ **64 taps** and eight chances to mis-space; with an array it is one placement and one dialog. This plus mirror is where their "design in 20 minutes" claim comes from | M | high |

It snaps to nothing (no edge pick, no object pick) and **the number is not editable**. Mounted at `Step3Obstructions.tsx:111, 379` behind a Halo item (:443-449) | This is **the difference between a sketch and a survey**. "The tank is 1.2 m off the north parapet" is how a roof is measured with a tape and is the input an engineer trusts. Ours can *show* a distance; theirs can *set* one — and ours carries your finger's error because it snaps to nothing | M | high |

The heatmap **is** here (:1140-1148, forces map mode at :792-793) — so heatmap is parity, the scrubber is not | Their own framing is that sun-path is a **sales** tool: "Traditional solar access maps are not easy for customers to understand… built for engineers, not buyers." | **Per-object view toggles on the step** | "show/hide labels · show/hide dimensions · show/hide height labels · toggle measurement labels · show/hide reference drawing · `Show solar access heatmap` · `Back to 3D view` · pan mode · `Export 3D model (.glb)`" | `Step3Obstructions.tsx:404-453` — the Halo carries **four items**: Add, Hide (**hides every obstruction at once**, :424-433), Dims (roof edge lengths only — `EdgeLabels` is mounted on roofs at :824, never on obstructions), Dist. **No height labels, no per-object label toggle, no reference drawing, no GLB export** | "Hide" being all-or-nothing means **the only way to see the roof under a crowded corner is to hide the whole survey**.
