| ★ **Reference drawing underlay on this step too** | "Satellite image out of date? Add your own site plan or CAD layout, set it to the right scale, and trace the roof on top of it." — DXF, PNG or JPG; `Set scale` by two taps and a typed distance; move/rotate/width/opacity/lock/centre; `dxfStats: "{{lines}} lines drawn, {{skipped}} items skipped."` | `lib/dxf.ts` and `lib/export-dxf.ts` are **export-only** (`export-dxf.ts:42` maps CableRoute kinds to layers). No import path. Our only backdrop is the Google tile at `SatCanvas.tsx:236-250` — **no onError, no loading state**, capped at roughly a 93 m square | This lands squarely on obstructions: on a C&I shed **the architect's DWG is the only source that shows where the vents, monitors and skylights actually are**. Tier-2/3 imagery is years stale, so the object you must place is often literally not in the picture | L | medium |

**Owner's named items on this step.** The **type count** is the number to quote: **11 types against their 17 plus four skylight sub-shapes**, and only **6 of our 11 have a real mesh** — `windmill.glb` is declared against an empty folder, and `elevated`, `ladder` and `other` share one grey box (`three/ObstructionMesh.tsx:255-270`). Six of their seven missing types are a palette row and a preset (S); skylight is real geometry (M). The **bulk / mirror / array / align** family is one project, not four: **bulk select is the prerequisite** (`Step3Obstructions.tsx:108` holds a single id), and mirror + array are where their "20 minutes" claim actually comes from — an eight-vent purlin line is ~64 taps here and two actions there.

**We already beat them at**

- **Automatic obstruction detection from Google's aerial height map, with measured length, width and height and a recorded confidence** — `lib/ops/roof-ops.ts:32-69` builds each object through the same factory as a hand placement but with real DSM dimensions and `provenance: { source: 'dataLayers', confidence: min(1, cells/16) }`; classifier at `lib/roof-map-fit.ts:310-316`. Their inventory states plainly: "No AI roof detection and no AI obstruction detection (every obstruction is hand-placed)." **Two caveats:** the classifier returns only elevated / tank / other, and **the action is currently unreachable from Step 3** — `Add N raised objects from map` is gated `onPlaceKind && !meshMode` (`three/Scene3D.tsx:2990`) and both Step 2 and Step 3 open in mesh mode.
- **The real neighbourhood as shading geometry, fetched automatically** — `lib/surround.ts:20-27` fetches the DSM at 100 m radius, downsamples 0.1 → 0.5 m, cuts out the site's own roofs; `lib/scene-model.ts:305-323` turns it into a BVH-accelerated caster. Theirs is a hand-placed "Nearby Building" box; their bundle proves "No LIDAR or photogrammetry import."
- **What one obstruction costs the plant, in energy, on the object itself** — `lib/energy/report.ts:341-362` `casterCost`, rendered on the 3D card at `three/Scene3D.tsx:3105-3116` as "shades N modules · costs ≈ X kWh/yr (Y%) — remove it and the plant gains that". Nothing comparable anywhere in their inventory. **This is the most persuasive sentence in our product** — and the reason the on-canvas card is worth mounting in 2D: `three/EntityLabel.tsx` already *is* that card, so the work is a 2D mount plus a trigger policy (DESIGN-SYSTEM §7.2 specifies long-press for touch), not building one.
- **A capability model — panels can BRIDGE over an obstruction** instead of the binary blocks/does-not-block every other tool ships — `types.ts:259-281` (`panelsMayCross`, `mustRemainOpenToSky`, `minVerticalClearanceM`, `supportsStructuralLoad`, `requiresEngineerConfirmation`), honoured by `lib/layout.ts:127-133` and `lib/structure-edit.ts:232-245`. Their physics copy is the binary version.
- **Turn an obstruction into a rooftop platform that carries panels** — `lib/roof-factory.ts` `obstructionToPlatform`, wired at `Step3Obstructions.tsx:700-728`, so stacking, shading, structure and BOM all flow through the normal pipeline. Their nearest equivalent is "Elevated Roof" as a placement surface, not a conversion of a surveyed object.
- **The parent surface is re-resolved from position on every move, so an anchor can never go stale** — `Step3Obstructions.tsx:130-146` funnels every move through one `update()` re-running `resolveAnchorRoofId`. Their design asks the user to pick the parent instead.
- **Placing or moving an obstruction switches off the modules under it in the same undoable patch** — :145, 169, 187, 194 all patch through `withObstructions` (`lib/structure-edit.ts`), so blocked modules go off and come back on undo without a re-fill. No live reconcile appears in their step-2 inventory.
- **Design health cross-checks the traced roof height against the aerial map and says so in plain words** — `lib/surround-check.ts:24-45` emits `roof_height_vs_map` past 1.5 m of disagreement, naming the direction and the consequence.


---

## Gap register — components, auto placement, manual edit

Effort: **S** under a day · **M** a few days · **L** a week or more. Payoff: high / medium / low.
Every "Ours today" cell is a file:line in `src/features/solar-studio/` unless stated. Every "Their
evidence" cell is a string quoted from ResLink's public bundle via `reslink-feature-inventory.md`.

---

### Components & sizing

ResLink Step 3 vs our Step 4 (`screens/Step4Components.tsx`). Their step answers the two questions an
EPC actually asks — "is my module in here?" and "what size do I quote?" Ours answers neither, and is
far ahead on a third question nobody at ResLink can ask at all (costed option comparison). 13 gaps.

| # | Gap | Their evidence | Ours today | Why it matters | Effort | Payoff |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| C1 | **Datasheet PDF → extracted specs.** Brand picked by logo, 10 MB cap, `Extracting specs...`, `"{{value}} variants found — pick one to use"` with prev/next, `Review extracted data`, `Already in database`, `Save & Select` | Inventory Step 3, "Three ways to get a component" #2 | **The affordance is a dead `<div>`** — `Step4Components.tsx:792-806`, no onClick, no handler, text reads `upload datasheet (PDF extraction) — mocked in POC`. `grep -rni datasheet` over the feature root returns comments and this one string. No extraction code, no upload route, no custom-spec store. The InverterPicker (`:811-987`) has not even the dead affordance | The Indian EPC's real module is a Waaree/Premier/Goldi SKU that changes quarterly. Not in `data/panels.ts` = **cannot design at all**: the Step 4 gate (`screens/Wizard.tsx:118-123`) refuses Next without a panel. **The single hardest wall in the product**, and we advertise the escape hatch then do nothing | L | high |
| C2 | **Manual spec entry.** Panel: brand, model, wattage, length, width, Voc, Vmp, DCR, ALMM. Inverter: brand, model, AC kW, MPPT min/max V, MPPT count, count, String/Central | Inventory Step 3, "Three ways" #3, with the full field list | `Step4Components.tsx:804` — `or enter specs manually` is text inside the same dead div. No `PanelSpec`/`InverterSpec` is ever constructed outside `data/panels.ts` and `data/inverters.ts`. `Components.panel` is `PanelSpec \| null` (`types.ts:428`) with **no user-authored variant and no provenance field** to mark one | The cheap half of C1, unblocking the same wall. Needs a provenance stamp so a hand-typed Voc never prints with catalogue authority — the discipline `data/catalog.ts:15-19` already models | M | high |
| C3 | **A real catalogue — 512 panels, 245 inverters**, searchable, paginated, user-extensible | Inventory Step 3: `Search by brand or wattage...`, Min/Max Watt + Type filters, `{{count}} panels`, prev/next page. `our-audit-critique.md §F`: `GET /design-api/api/components/{panels,inverters}` is **publicly readable unauthenticated** | **15 panels** (`data/panels.ts:6-22`) and **12 inverters** (`data/inverters.ts:4-17`), both headed `// Mock … representative Indian-market …` and stamped `provenance: 'mock-representative'` (`data/catalog.ts:41`). List UI is a `maxHeight: 320` scroller with no pagination (`Step4Components.tsx:745`) | **34x and 20x.** Their catalogue is unauthenticated — it can seed ours today. Our filter UI is already their shape (Min/Max Watt, type chips, count line), so this is data + pagination, not a redesign | M | high |
| C4 | **`BIS Approved` filter** alongside DCR and ALMM, on the components step | Inventory Step 3: "Indian compliance filters up front — `DCR Compliant`, `ALMM Listed`, `BIS Approved`" | Two of three, panels only: `almm`/`dcr` on `PanelSpec` (`types.ts:315-316`), filtered at `:658-659`. **`InverterSpec` (`types.ts:333-345`) has no almm/dcr/bis field at all** — the inverter list (`:830-832`) filters on free text only. The only BIS in the tree is prose we assert and never verify: `screens/Step9Bom/index.tsx:392` `ALMM module + BIS inverter certificates` | BIS / IEC 61683 registration is **what the DISCOM asks for on the inverter**. We print the claim five steps after the choice with no data behind it. A boolean plus a chip | S | medium |
| C5 | **Multiple inverter models in one design** — list editor, rule `"A design cannot mix string and central inverters. Remove one kind."`, live `"{{total}} total MPPT ({{inverters}} × {{mppt}})"` chip | Inventory Step 3 | `types.ts:428-431` holds **ONE `inverter` plus `inverterCount`** — a design is N identical boxes; `1× 20 kW + 1× 10 kW` is unrepresentable. `:870` shows `{selected.mppt.count} MPPT` for one unit and never multiplies by count | A 60–80 kW C&I roof in India is routinely 2×25 + 1×10. The total-MPPT chip is a half-day fix; the heterogeneous list touches `PvString.inverterIndex` (`types.ts:735`) and the SLD | L | high |
| C6 | **Target capacity capped at 2× sanctioned load**, with `"Above 2× sanctioned load ({{value}} kW) — may need DISCOM approval."` | Inventory Steps 0 and 3: `maxAllowed: "Maximum allowed: {{max}} kWp (2x sanctioned load)"`, `exceedsMax: "Cannot exceed 2x sanctioned load"` | We collect it (`screens/Step1Setup.tsx:211-219` → `info.sanctionedLoadKw`) and never use it where capacity is chosen. `grep -rni sanctioned` finds **one** engineering consumer: `lib/auto-design.ts:257-270`, which fires at **1×**, one step later, as a post-hoc warning. The capacity card (`Step4Components.tsx:154-243`) has no cap and never names the number | This is the Indian net-metering rule and it governs whether the quote is approvable. **Ours is wrong in both directions at once**: it cries wolf on every legal system between 1× and 2×, and it never warns on the screen where the number is typed. | C7 | **The sun read live inside the sizing assistant** — `"reading the sun across the year…"`, then `"≈ {{value}}% of ideal"` and `"{{sun}}% sun · {{orientation}}% facing/tilt"` | Inventory Step 3 ("the smartest screen in the product") | Nothing at Step 4 reads the sun. **Both halves ship today and neither is connected**: `computeHeatmap(project)` (`lib/solar-heatmap.ts:192-229`) needs only `location` + `roofs` — both present at Step 4 — is async, chunked, cancellable, already emits `onProgress(done,total)` and `monthlyRoofAvg`, and is called only from `Step6Editor.tsx:402` / `three/Scene3D.tsx:809`. The orientation half is `poaBeamRatio` (`lib/poa.ts:27`), consumed only by `lib/auto-design.ts:99` and `lib/energy/report.ts` | Their number tells the user **before placing a panel** whether the roof is worth 5 kW or 12 kW, and why. **Clearest "already built, not connected" win in the product** | M | high |
| C8 | **Live what-ifs under `Assumptions`** — `Flip which way the panels face`, `Lower tilt`, `Raise tilt`, `Panels follow roof slope`, `By roof` | Inventory Step 3 | No tilt or azimuth control on Step 4. Flat-RCC pose is **hardcoded**: `lib/layout.ts:79` `return { tiltDeg: 10, azimuthDeg: 180 };`. The only way to change it is per-table in Step 6, four screens away. The energy consequence is computable now via `poaBeamRatio` and is never offered as a choice | 10° south is a ballast compromise, not an answer. On a Chennai shed or a north-facing Delhi block the flip/tilt question is worth several percent of annual yield — asked at the moment capacity is argued with the customer | M | high |
| C9 | **The roof-estimate readout** — `Roof Capacity`, `kWp fits`, `"{{panels}} panels · {{area}} m² usable · inverter ≈ {{kw}} kW AC"`, closing with `"{{wattage}}W × {{panels}} panels = {{capacity}} kWp"` | Inventory Step 3 | We have the better estimate and **throw away the explanation**: `Step4Components.tsx:188-191` takes `est.kwp` and discards `est.panels` (computed at `lib/layout.ts:695-715`). No usable m², no implied AC size, no equation. One sentence of prose at `:197-199` | Our Auto is *more* honest than theirs — it fills through the same door the real layout uses, so setbacks, obstructions and bridging all apply (`lib/layout.ts:701-711` documents the bug that forced this). We just print a bare number. `18 panels × 540 W = 9.7 kWp · 43 m² usable · inverter ≈ 8.5 kW AC` is a one-line change to code we already run | S | medium |
| C10 | **"Panels on structures" as one sizing-time question** — `Keep clear` vs `Panels on top`, reporting `{{count}} fixed structures kept clear · {{area}} m²` | Inventory Step 3 | We model it per object: `blocksPlacement` (`types.ts:250`), defaulted true (`lib/roof-factory.ts:252`), toggled one object at a time (`screens/Step3Obstructions.tsx:646-650`), plus a richer bridging rule (`lib/layout.ts:129-133`, `lib/capabilities.ts:95-99`). **No global switch, no count, no m², and none of it visible from Step 4** | The more capable model and the worse experience: on a shed with 14 objects, their question is one tap and ours is 14. Roll the per-object truth up into one Step-4 line and we keep the capability and win the tap count | M | medium |
| C11 | **A `Needs fix` badge distinct from `Not selected`** | Inventory Step 3: "`Step {{step}} of {{total}}` inside the step, with `Needs fix` / `Not selected` section badges" | `Step4Components.tsx:606-621` — `SectionHead` has exactly **two** states: green `Check` for any truthy `state`, or a dot plus `Not selected`. The inverter's `state` is `c.inverter ? … : null` (`:248`), so **any** selected inverter reads as done regardless of validity | A DC/AC 1.9 selection shows a green tick in the header and an amber clipping warning 200 px below, on the same screen. A third state is ~10 lines | S | medium |
| C12 | **The SCB rule stated where central topology is chosen** — `scbNote: "Strings will be grouped into SCBs (20 strings/box)"` | Inventory Step 3 | We have the number and don't print it: `data/rules/india.ts:505-508` `combiner: { maxStringsPerBox: 12, … }`, consumed at `lib/electrical/combiner.ts:35-41` and `lib/bom/emitters/electrical.ts:235`. Our hint (`:278`) says only "the BOM adds combiners + string fuses" | **We are right on the engineering** (12-in is the commoner Indian box) **and silent in the UI**. Interpolating `{rules.combiner.maxStringsPerBox}` into the existing hint makes the BOM's combiner count predictable before it appears | S | low |
| C13 | **Brand-logo picking and paginated browse** | Inventory Step 3: "pick the brand by **logo**"; `{{count}} panels`, prev/next page | `Step4Components.tsx:763-778` renders `{p.brand[0]}` — a coloured **single-letter avatar**. Our count line (`:742-744`) is already their copy; pagination is absent because 15 rows fit | Cosmetic at 15 entries, load-bearing at 512 — an installer scans for the Waaree or Adani mark, not a purple "W". Bundle the logos with the C3 import | S | low |

**We already beat them at**

- **A real comparison matrix.** Six candidate panels each run through the FULL production pipeline — roof fill → auto-string → 8760-hour energy → BOM → finance — reporting annual kWh, net cost after subsidy, payback, 25-yr savings, ROI %, DC/AC ratio, string feasibility *with the actual refusal message*, array weight in kg, install complexity and both warranties, plus a written decision log. `lib/comparison.ts` (439 lines): `shortlistPanels:206-227`, `buildCandidateProject:93-133`, `compareCandidates:234-330`, ranking `:328-340`; rendered at `Step4Components.tsx:382-559`. **Their bundle has no equivalent of any of it. This is the thing not to throw away.**
- **DC/AC ratio as a live, named check with a band and a diagnosis**, on the picker and every comparison row — `Step4Components.tsx:833, 901-925` (`>1.35` high/clipping, `<0.9` low/oversized, healthy 0.90–1.35), same band driving `recommendInverterFor` (`lib/comparison.ts:142-154`) and `warnNote` (`:272-275`). Theirs shows only `inverter ≈ {{kw}} kW AC` — no ratio, no clipping language.
- **Sizing from the customer's electricity bill** — ₹/month + tariff + peak sun hours → kWp in one tap: `lib/solar.ts:83-91`, banner at `Step4Components.tsx:101-108, 216-233`. They size from roof area and sanctioned load only.
- **An independent third-party cross-check on roof capacity** — Google Solar's `maxPanels` rendered beside our own estimate, honestly flagging the wattage mismatch (`Step4Components.tsx:201-215`). They show NASA POWER irradiance at Step 0 and no second opinion on capacity.
- **A measured, not estimated, roof capacity** — `estimateMaxCapacityKwp` fills through `fillRoofAsSegment`, so setbacks, obstructions, keepouts and bridging clearance all apply (`lib/layout.ts:695-715`, with the comment documenting the "141.5 / 139.7 kWp" bug that forced it).
- **Battery / BESS selection with DC-hybrid vs AC-coupled coupling** — `Step4Components.tsx:299-320`, `screens/Step4Battery.tsx`, `data/batteries.ts`, `types.ts:435-440`. Inventory: "No battery / storage."
- **DC optimisers that change the engineering** — MLPE relaxes the same-orientation/tilt/shade constraint on what may share a string: `types.ts:419-424`, consumed in `lib/electrical/grouping.ts`. Inventory: "No optimiser or micro-inverter modelling."
- **Bifacial as a module property with a real rear-gain engine** — `types.ts:306-314` `bifacialityPct`, populated for Waaree Bi-55-550 and Vikram HYPERSOL 555 (`data/panels.ts:12, 14`), consumed by `lib/energy/bifacial.ts`. Inventory: "No bifacial."
- **A versioned catalogue envelope whose provenance is printed to the user** — `catalogVersion`, `effectiveFrom`, `provenance`, per-entry overrides (`data/catalog.ts:21-61`), surfaced verbatim as "representative data — not confirmed vendor quotes" (`Step4Components.tsx:403-405`). Nothing in their bundle labels catalogue trust.
- **A panel swap is a first-class undoable operation** that re-lays every table on the new module's lattice and re-keys `layoutFp` so stored captures go honestly stale — `lib/ops/components-ops.ts:23-64`, header comment "A panel swap is not a field write."
- **Temperature-correct string feasibility feeding the pick, per candidate** — `validateSystem(...resolveDesignTemps(project))` on every comparison row, surfacing the real error in `feasibilityNote` (`lib/comparison.ts:262-267`), so an unstringable option cannot be chosen by accident.

### Auto placement & inverter placement

ResLink Step 4 vs our Step 5 (`screens/Step5AutoDesign.tsx`, `lib/auto-design.ts`, `lib/layout.ts`,
`lib/spacing.ts`) plus the inverter path in `screens/Step6Editor.tsx` / `three/Scene3D.tsx` /
`lib/ops/electrical-ops.ts`. We are ahead on the physics and behind on everything the engineer
touches. 12 gaps.

| # | Gap | Their evidence | Ours today | Why it matters | Effort | Payoff |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A1 | **A visible design on the placement step.** Their Step 4 renders the 3D scene — it is where inverters are tapped onto walls — with per-surface cards beside it | `Place Inverters` → `Tap to place Inverter {{number}}`; hint `'Orbit to find a wall, then tap "Place"'` | `Step5AutoDesign.tsx:83-167` is **one `<div>` with a header, two objective buttons, two buttons and an `<ol>` of text cards**. No `SatCanvas`, no `Scene3D`, neither imported. After "Design it" the user sees a one-line status and a text log; the only route to seeing 693 placed panels is the button labelled `Place manually` (`:137`) | An EPC presses the product's flagship button and **sees prose**. Nothing shows a shaded corner, a table hugging a parapet, or the array on the wrong face — the three things one glance would catch. It also makes the log unverifiable: trust "91% sun access" with no picture | M | high |
| A2 | **Roof Priority as a drag-to-reorder list** — the engineer overrides fill order and can implicitly drop a roof | `Placement Settings`: **Roof Priority**, `Drag to reorder placement priority` | `lib/auto-design.ts:114` sorts by score and `:173` iterates every roof in computed order. **No `priority`, `order` or `excluded` field on `Roof`** (`types.ts:178-217`), no override arg on `layoutAutoDesign` (`lib/ops/layout-ops.ts:352`), no roof list on Step 5. **The only way to keep panels off a roof is to delete the roof** | Physics is not the whole ranking: the customer owns only the front block, the rear shed is a tenant's, the west face is reserved for expansion. Our engine cannot hear any of it — and "delete the roof" also deletes its obstructions, walkways and shadow contribution (`lib/cascade.ts:65`) | M | high |
| A3 | **Panel Orientation on the placement step, including `Auto (best fit)`** | `Panel Orientation` incl. `Auto (best fit)` | `lib/layout.ts:57` `DEFAULT_FILL = { orientation: 'portrait', … }`; `lib/auto-design.ts:71-73` and `:179-183` spread it and never override. Every other call site hardcodes it (`Step6Editor.tsx:1396, 1418, 3022, 3040`). `grep "'landscape'"` outside tests returns only `types.ts:445`, `three/textures.ts` and `three/PanelsInstanced.tsx` — **the renderer knows about landscape and no placement path ever produces one** | Indian metal-shed and north-light roofs are laid landscape along the purlins, and C&I is half our declared scope. `panelFootprint` (`lib/layout.ts:83-91`) already swaps w/h correctly — **the engine has supported this the whole time and the product never let anyone ask**. `Auto (best fit)` is the cheapest capacity win: on a long thin shed often **5–15% more modules** for one comparison run | M | high |
| A4 | **Row Spacing as a placement-step control, with the reason in the UI** | `Row Spacing`, `Enable for tilted roofs`, `"Prevents shading on Dec 21"` | **We solve it better and expose it worse.** `lib/spacing.ts:23-51` sweeps the real sun 09:00–15:00 on Dec 21 (Jun 21 south of the equator) for the true shadow-free pitch, applied automatically at `lib/layout.ts:246-264`. But `FillOptions.rowPitchM` (`lib/layout.ts:47`) — the expert override the comment advertises — **is passed by nothing**, and Step 5 renders no spacing control. The only spacing UI is a one-shot `Apply shadow-free spacing` button (`Step6Editor.tsx:2233-2236`) needing a table already selected | The pitch is **the single biggest lever on how many kWp fit a flat RCC roof**. Today the engineer cannot loosen it for a windy coastal site or tighten it to hit a target before the fill runs. Their toggle is cruder physics with better reach | S | high |
| A5 | **Narrated progress while placement runs** | `Preparing placement surfaces...`, `Creating exclusion zones...`, `Analyzing edges and determining orientation...`, `Placing tables on roof...`, `Segmenting panels into tables...`, `Compiling statistics...`, `Placement complete`, preceded by `Calculating solar access (month {{month}}/{{total}})...` | `Step5AutoDesign.tsx:45-49` runs `ops.run(layoutAutoDesign, …)` **synchronously on the main thread** — no `isRunning`, no `aria-busy`, no disabled button, no spinner. Measured here: 45 ms for one 40×60 m roof with 12 obstructions, **331 ms for six (4,113 panels)**. The worker that would fix it already exists and is unused: `lib/analysis-client.ts:68`, `workers/analysis.worker.ts:34` | On a mid-range Android in the sun that 331 ms is plausibly **1–3 s of frozen UI with zero feedback**, on the button the product is sold on. The narration is not decoration — it says why it takes time and what was considered. **We cannot distinguish a slow run from a hung one** | M | medium |
| A6 | **Per-surface result cards** — surface name, panel count, `{{value}}% avg` solar access graded Optimal / Good / Moderate / Poor | Inventory Step 4 | We have the data and print it as prose: `lib/auto-design.ts:154-168` emits one `DesignDecision` per roof whose `choice` is `"190 panels possible · 91% sun access · 104% orientation"` and whose `reason` is three lines of POA/GHI jargon, rendered as identical bordered `<li>` cards (`Step5AutoDesign.tsx:151-164`). **The card states CAPACITY, not what was placed** — verified: a 2-roof project with a 6 kWp target put 11 panels on roof 1 and **0** on roof 2, and roof 2 still read "190 panels possible · 91% sun access". Our grading vocabulary already exists (`Step6Editor.tsx:1514-1517`) and this step does not use it | This is where the engineer decides whether to accept the machine's answer. Five sentences per roof instead of five chips — **and the number printed is the wrong number**, so a roof that received nothing looks like a roof that received 190 | S | high |
| A7 | **A `Target: {{value}} kWp` chip per surface, marked `Met` or `Exceeded`** | Inventory Step 4 | `lib/auto-design.ts:239-250` emits **one project-level** `capacity-outcome` decision. No per-surface attribution, no chip; the status line (`Step5AutoDesign.tsx:141-146`) prints `N modules · N kWp · N strings` in one sentence | On a multi-roof C&I job the useful question is "which surface got me there and which under-delivered". Also, because the budget is `Math.floor((requestedKwp*1000)/spec.watt)` (`lib/auto-design.ts:136`), **we can never Exceed** — we always land up to one panel short and call it "met" | S | medium |
| A8 | **Monthly bars Jan–Dec on the placement result** | Inventory Step 4, on the per-surface cards | Step 5 renders nothing monthly. The structure exists — `lib/solar-heatmap.ts:31-37` `monthlyRoofAvg` / `monthlyRoofHours` — but it is **project-wide** (`HeatCell` carries `world` and no `roofId`, `:20-27`), computed only from the Step 6 / Scene3D flow, and `Step5AutoDesign.tsx` imports none of it | **December is the month an Indian rooftop sale is won or lost**, and the annual average hides it. A south face at 96% annual can be 78% in December behind a parapet. We show one annual number per roof from 12 probe panels | M | medium |
| A9 | **Inverter placement as an in-flow, guided, multi-inverter step ending in an explicit commit** | `Place Inverters` → `Tap to place Inverter {{number}}` → "Tap on a wall or obstruction surface" / `TAP A WALL` → "All inverters placed! | A10 | **Solar access recomputed as an explicit, month-by-month part of the run** | `Calculating solar access (month {{month}}/{{total}})...` before the placement narration, `{{value}}% avg` after | Ours is a **12-panel probe sample per roof** (`lib/auto-design.ts:48` `PROBE_PANELS = 12`, `:88-94`) — the only shading the placement step performs. The real per-module pass runs later, asynchronously and invisibly, from `store/useDesignSync.ts:52` | The "% sun access" printed on Step 5 and the access the engine computes seconds later are **two different numbers from two different sample sets**, with nothing reconciling them and nothing marking the log's figure provisional — on the screen where the user decides whether to trust the machine | M | medium |
| A11 | **`Table Layout: Grouped / Separate` as a placement choice**, and an explicit `Segmenting panels into tables...` stage | Inventory Step 4 | `FillOptions.grouped` exists (`lib/layout.ts:38`) but **only widens the row gap** (`:365` `rowGap = opts.grouped ? gap : gap + 0.4`) and is hardcoded `true` in `DEFAULT_FILL` (`:60`) and at all four Step 6 call sites. `fillRoofAsSegment` always produces **exactly ONE `ArraySegment` per roof** (`lib/layout.ts:450-520`), obstruction gaps recorded as `removed` cells (`lib/segment-ops.ts:194-199`) | The steel is modelled correctly (`lib/structure.ts:386` "Holes (removed cells) split runs") but **the editing model is not**: two halves of a roof split by a stair block are one segment and cannot take different tilts, azimuths, profiles or pitches without hand-splitting. On a real Indian RCC terrace that is the normal case | M | medium |
| A12 | **`Recalculate Placement` as a named, settings-driven re-run** | Sits under `Placement Settings` (Roof Priority, Panel Orientation, Row Spacing) | We have `Design it again` (`Step5AutoDesign.tsx:130`) — **with no settings above it there is nothing to recalculate with**. The only variable is a two-option objective radiogroup (`:22-33`), so pressing it twice produces an identical layout | A recalculate button with no inputs is a redraw button. Theirs closes the loop: change priority or orientation, recalculate, compare. **Ours cannot express a change** | S | medium |

**Carry-over flagged by the completeness critic** (full detail in the bug register, repeated here
because it appears in only one dimension and corrupts documents that leave the building):
`lib/ops/electrical-ops.ts:145-149` — placing one more inverter than `components.inverterCount`
silently deletes the **oldest** placement and shifts every remaining index down by one, re-pointing
existing strings at physically different boxes. `PvString.inverterIndex` is positional
(`types.ts:735`), nothing remaps it, and `toRef: 'inverter/${placementIndex}'` (`lib/routing.ts:490`)
carries the wrong box onto the **SLD, the DXF and the installation sheet the crew pulls cable from**.
The hint bar calls this safe: "tap an edge to move the oldest one" (`Step6Editor.tsx:1727`).

**We already beat them at**

- **Automatic roof ranking by real expected yield per panel** — measured beam access × plane-of-array orientation factor, not a human drag list. `lib/auto-design.ts:64-115`: up to 12 of each roof's own candidates through the real raycast shading engine (`:90`) AND transposed through `poaFactor` (`:97-101`), sorted by `access * poa` (`:110, 114`). The comment at `:56-60` names the failure it fixes: an unshaded **north** face reads ~100% access and yields far less than a south one.
- **A complete machine-generated decision log** — every choice, its reason and the numeric inputs behind it (`capacityKwp=`, `beamAccess=`, `orientationPoa=`, `tilt=`, `latitude=`): `lib/auto-design.ts:139-271`, rendered at `Step5AutoDesign.tsx:148-166` and in Step 6's "Why this layout?" (`Step6Editor.tsx:1911-1927`). **ResLink narrates progress; it never explains a decision.**
- **Obstruction bridging** — the array spans a crossable object when the structure's own clearance clears it, with the arithmetic logged and an engineer-confirmation flag: `lib/layout.ts:121-134`, `:456-470`, `lib/auto-design.ts:192-223`. Their obstruction physics stops at blocks-placement true/false.
- **Auto-design auto-strings AND auto-routes the cable in the same single undoable patch** — `lib/ops/run.ts:20-24` folds `syncElectrical` into the op's own patch, so one run yields panels + segments + strings + `cableRoutes` as one undo step (`lib/derive/electrical-sync.ts:35-56`). Theirs requires a separate manual `Confirm & String`.
- **Site-solved shadow-free row pitch, plus GCR, as-built pitch and a tracker-aware variant** — `lib/spacing.ts:23-51` (hemisphere-correct at `:39`), with Recommended pitch, GCR and — uniquely — "Rows as built" with its measured GCR, a tracker branch recommending by GCR, and a warning when built rows force hard backtracking (`Step6Editor.tsx:2172-2239`). Their rooftop equivalent is a checkbox captioned "Prevents shading on Dec 21".
- **Inverter placement is not wall-only** — wall, free-standing on a roof stand, or at ground level (`types.ts:687-699` `pos` + `level`), decided by distance in both views (`Step6Editor.tsx:761-766`, `three/Scene3D.tsx:2935-2941`), and the same grammar places DCDB, ACDB, battery and the service meter. Theirs names only inverters on a wall or obstruction.
- **Placement drives MEASURED cable metres and vertical drops into the BOM** — `lib/routing.ts:46-53` derives drops from roof height and the placed inverter's own mounting height; `:480-497` stamps `assumedTarget: true` when the placement is missing so the BOM downgrades that line to `estimated` (`lib/bom/emitters/electrical.ts:95-96`). ResLink asks the user to type `dcCableRunM` / `acCableRunM` — **a typed guess where we measure geometry**.
- **Live, collision-aware placement previews** — the drag-to-fill ghost runs the real `autoFillRoof` each frame so panels part around a tank as you drag (`Step6Editor.tsx:3012-3026`), and a single-panel ghost carries a `panelFitsAt` verdict (`:3029-3050`). Their `Grid Size (Rows × Columns)` → `Next ({{count}} panels)` is a numeric commit, not a preview.
- **Step 5 is the only accessible, touch-compliant screen in the wizard** — real arrow-key roving on `role="radiogroup"` (`:72-81`), every button `min-h-11`, one of only three screens with an `<h1>`, written entirely in design-system tokens with no inline styles.

---

### Manual edit — tables, selection, on-canvas controls

ResLink Step 5 ("the step with lots of options") vs our Step 6 — `screens/Step6Editor.tsx`,
`three/Gizmos.tsx`, `three/MarqueeSelect.tsx`, `three/StructEditPanel.tsx`, `three/EntityLabel.tsx`,
`components/RadialMenu.tsx`, `lib/segment-ops.ts`, `lib/panel-move.ts`.

**Verdict.** We are behind — badly on the two things the owner named (table selection/adding, and
on-object cards in 2D) and **catastrophically on multi-table editing**: ResLink edits N tables at once
with `-` for mixed values, while our Table sheet is *structurally* single-segment
(`lib/segment-ops.ts:349-362` returns `kind: 'other'` the moment two segments are selected, so
`Step6Editor.tsx:622-624` resolves `selectedSegment` to `undefined` and the sheet never mounts). We
are ahead only on the engineering underneath — ops-kernel preview/refusal, the member model, the
tracker, cable-route editing — **and none of it is reachable with a thumb.** 37 gaps.

Lasso and the Tables target are 2D-only for now |

DESIGN-SYSTEM N8 requires Undo reachable by thumb. Compounded by `store/store.tsx:191, 207, 217, 228` wiping the undo stack on hydrate | S | high |

**We already beat them at**

- **Every 3D manipulation is a live PREVIEW of a typed operation that can refuse before it commits** — `three/Gizmos.tsx:404-408` runs `previewOp` on every pointermove, `:479-500` runs the same op on release, `:270-300` `Readout` prints `summarizeImpact(preview.impact)` or `Refused — {reason}`, and `:334-339` holds a refusal on screen for 2.2 s so the table does not just snap back unexplained. **No equivalent mechanism appears anywhere in the ResLink inventory.**
- **Collision-aware live ghosts before any commit** — the table drag paints the exact modules that will land, already avoiding obstructions, walkways, keepouts and setbacks, with `{rows} rows × {cols} · {n} modules · {kWp} kWp` (`:3012-3026, :3702-3739`); a single-panel ghost turns red on "no room for a module here" (`:3030-3050, :3741-3750`); the grow ghost previews before Add (`:2613-2616, :2653-2662`).
- **A full parametric member model per table, inside the editor** — leg / rafter / purlin / brace counts and metres, total steel kg by section, structural DRC and a standing engineer-sign-off disclaimer (`:2269-2344`), with `{kgPerM}` on the profile chip (`:2264`).
- **A per-table leg-plan editor** — individual leg positions, not just a spacing number: `three/StructEditPanel.tsx:519-552` mounting `three/LegPlanEditor`, committing a validated `segments` patch as one undoable step.
- **Foundation choice with an honest dead-load statement and an explicit refusal to guarantee slab capacity** — `three/StructEditPanel.tsx:56-70` `foundationNote`, and `lib/drc.ts:274-284` `foundation_dead_load`: "roof capacity is NOT checked. Have a structural engineer confirm the slab can carry it."
- **Single-axis tracker tables designed in the editor** — rotation-limit control, a plain-language torque-tube explanation, and a GCR-based row-pitch recommendation that warns when built rows are too tight to backtrack (`:2125-2154, :2172-2239`). Inventory: **"No tracker."**
- **Hand-editable DC cable routes on the plan** — grab a corner, or press the run itself to create a corner and drag it in one gesture, committed as one undo step and then marked hand-routed so auto-stringing leaves it alone (`:909-947, :3267-3311`).
- **Temperature-corrected cold-Voc shown live while a string is being built**, against the inverter's own limit — `{n} of {min}–{max} · {V} V cold`, red with "· over {maxDcV} V limit", amber with "· below MPPT floor when hot" (`:376-387, :1762-1783`).
- **An explained design log and Copilot suggestions attached to the layout** — "Why this layout?" with topic / choice / reason / inputs per decision (`:1912-1943`).
- **A validation banner that is a working index into the design** — tap an issue and the offending modules are selected and centred (`:1247-1275, :2377-2399`), backed by `lib/drc.ts` codes `panel_overlap`, `setback_breach`, `panel_in_keepout`, `panel_over_obstruction`, `bridge_clearance`, `foundation_clash`, `foundation_too_tall`.
- **Loose hand-placed modules can be promoted into a real parametric table** — racking, legs, member model — in one action: `:566-583` → `lib/ops/layout-ops.ts:114-135` → `lib/segment-ops.ts:47`.
- **Battery cabinets and DCDB / ACDB enclosures are placeable physical objects** — drawn on the plan and draggable along walls in 3D (`:1695-1717, :768-779, :3370-3386, :3510-3523`, `three/Gizmos.tsx:591-760`). Their inventory records no battery/storage; DCDB/ACDB appear only as BOM lines.
- **A layout lock that disables every mutating tool with a single explanation** instead of relying on care — `:182-190` `MUTATING_TOOLS`, `:432-434` `flashLock`, `:449-458` `toggleLock`, with a `locked` guard on all fourteen mutating handlers.


---

## Gap register — ground mount, sun path, stringing

### Ground mount & carport

**Verdict.** We have the better ground-mount **engine** — tracker, bifacial, physics-solved row pitch, foundation assemblies, free-field site works — and no ground-mount **product**. You reach a ground array by drawing a "roof" and then telling the app it is not a roof. Carport does not exist anywhere in the codebase.

**Two facts to read before the table.**

- **A 1 MW job cannot be traced.** The 2D design canvas is one 640 px static tile. `components/SatCanvas.tsx:104-120` fixes `sizePx = 1000` and derives the span from `SAT_ZOOM = 20` (`lib/maps.ts:9`) at `scale = 1` (`components/SatCanvas.tsx:237`). At Bengaluru that is 0.1455 m/px × 640 = **93.1 m edge to edge**; at Delhi **83.9 m**. The SVG is `viewBox="0 0 1000 1000"` (`:250-254`), so world coordinates beyond roughly ±46 m are **clipped out of the drawing surface** and the imagery past that one tile is blank. A 1 MWp field at GCR 0.4 is ~110 m square of array and ~127 m square with setbacks and roads. `zoomCovering()` exists at `lib/maps.ts:56-66` to pick a wider tile, is imported by `three/Scene3D.tsx`, and **is never called by SatCanvas** — which also passes `scale = 1` where `three/Scene3D.tsx:2610` passes `scale = 2`, so the surface you design on has a quarter of the pixels of the one you look at.
- **Carport is zero lines of product code.** `types.ts:158` — `RoofType` is rcc_flat, metal_shed, tile, ground. grep for carport / parking / canopy across `src/` returns **zero** product hits; the only mentions are unbuilt plan rows (`docs/STUDIO-MASTER-PLAN.md:381`, `docs/studio-gap-analysis.md:124` "carport: 0"). No car, no post, no beam in `lib/structure.ts`. ResLink ships it as a first-class roof type with `clearHeight`, `carportTilt`, `postSpacing`, `postLayout` (Centre T-frame / One edge cantilever / Both edges H-frame), readouts `Deck at low edge`, `Deck at high edge`, **`Parking spaces (standard car)`**, `Posts`, and a real `cars.glb`.

---

### Sun path, shadow simulation & the energy report

**Verdict.** Our physics is genuinely a class above theirs — a real 8760-hour TMY/Perez engine, DSM neighbour casters, per-module caster attribution, string-level electrical shading, P50/P90. It is undermined by presentation and by one silent modelling hole.

**The headline.** **The customer-facing solar-access heatmap ignores the neighbouring buildings the same product spent a network round-trip fetching.** `lib/solar-heatmap.ts:237` calls `buildShadowCasters(project)` with **no options**; `lib/scene-model.ts:189` defaults `opts = {}` and `:309` gates the whole Google DSM block on `if (opts.surround)`. `computeHeatmap`'s own opts type (`lib/solar-heatmap.ts:194-202`) has no `surround` field, and **neither call site could pass one** (`three/Scene3D.tsx:809-813`, `screens/Step6Editor.tsx:402`). Meanwhile `lib/shading.ts:208-211` does pass it and `store/useDesignSync.ts:50-52` resolves the grid before every run. Net effect on any urban Indian site: **the modules render red at 78% access from the G+4 next door while the heatmap cell underneath them renders green at 96%, in the same frame, both labelled "Solar access."** The heatmap is the one that reaches the customer.

#### What they have that we don't — 9

| Gap | Their evidence | Ours today | Why it matters | Eff | Pay |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **A per-loss EDITOR on the energy report** — every line typeable, per-loss reset, a Custom badge, Reset to defaults, with shading and temperature explicitly LOCKED, and a >100% guard | `Edit losses`, per-loss `reset`, `Reset to defaults`, `Custom`; locked pair: "Calculated from the site temperature and the shadow analysis — these two cannot be edited"; guard: "These losses add up to more than 100%. This design would generate nothing." | **Zero editability anywhere.** grep soiling in `screens/`, `components/`, `three/` = 0. Every loss is a module constant: `lib/energy/hourly.ts:424-426` (soiling 1%/3%), `:543-544` (LID 1.5%, mismatch 2%), `lib/energy/report.ts:263` (degradation 0.75%), `lib/solar.ts:50-55` (fixed 8.1% temperature). `components/EnergyReportSheet.tsx:172-189` renders `r.losses` read-only | An EPC in Ludhiana knows his soiling is 6-8%, not 3%, and his client's lender will ask him to justify it. **He either ships a yield he does not believe or leaves the tool.** Their lock design is also our own provenance rule expressed as a control — the two figures we MEASURE are the two they refuse to let anyone touch | M | high |
| **A month-by-month solar-access pass in a dedicated worker with a real progress model** | `solarAccessWorker-Bja0y9sw.js`; `Calculating Solar Access...`, `Month {{month}} of 12`, `~{{count}} min left`, `Under a minute left`, `Preparing…` | Our per-module shading IS in a worker (`workers/analysis.worker.ts`, `lib/analysis-client.ts:30-61) — **but the ROOF heatmap is not**: `lib/solar-heatmap.ts:192-307` runs main-thread, chunked 150 cells per rAF. 3D progress is a bare percentage (`three/Scene3D.tsx:2145-2160`); ~~**in 2D there is no progress at all**~~ ✅ 2026-09-09 (honest-numbers 6): the 2D editor now shows "Computing solar access… NN%" through `lib/heatmap-cache.ts`, and the map is shared with 3D so the pass runs once per geometry. Still main-thread, still not a worker — that half stays open | A 600 m² shed is ~1800 cells × 12 months × ~25 samples ≈ **540,000 raycasts**. #### Made too complicated here — 10

| What is hard | Our flow today | Their flow | Fix here | Eff |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Seeing a shadow move on the step where you place the thing that casts it** | Step 3 → 3D opens in mesh studio mode with a fixed sun-independent key light: no time bar, no season chips, no sunrise/sunset, no widget, no chart. **Four taps to see a shadow you were already looking at the object for** | `Winter` / `Summer` / `Equinox` / `Today`, `Sunrise:`, `Sunset:`, `Alt:` and `Show solar access heatmap` inline on the step | `screens/Step3Obstructions.tsx:295` — `initialViewMode="mesh"` → `"map"`; ungate the measure tools and obstruction gizmo from `!meshMode` (`three/Scene3D.tsx:1305`) | S |
| **Working out WHAT is casting the dark patch** | **Turning the heatmap on deletes the entire 3D model.** `three/Scene3D.tsx:2727-2739` returns a flat unlit plane with `<ambientLight intensity={1}/>`, the satellite ground and the cells — no tanks, trees, parapets or buildings. Orbit is disabled (`:1484-1485`) and the camera forced to top (`:792-798`). **You see a stain and must turn the heatmap OFF to find out what made it** | Their heatmap is one of a row of VIEW toggles — it **overlays** the design; `Back to 3D view` is separate | `three/Scene3D.tsx:2725-2740` — render HeatmapLayer as an overlay instead of early-returning a stripped scene; drop the orbit lock | M |
| **Knowing whether a toggle is on** | In one radial menu `heatmap` and `access` set `active` when the feature is ON, while `sun-path`, `surround` and `electrical` set `active: !showSunPath` etc — pressed when the feature is **HIDDEN**. `active` drives both the visual state and `aria-pressed` (`components/RadialMenu.tsx:225, :241). **So the Sun button glows while the sun path is off** | Labelled by action, stated consistently (`Hide/Show sun path`, `Hide/Show external buildings`) | `three/Scene3D.tsx:1190-1216` | S |
| **Waiting for the 2D heatmap** | Tap Heat → `setHeatResult(null)` empties the canvas → ~540,000 raycasts main-thread in rAF chunks with **no onProgress and no spinner** → seconds of blank janky canvas → the map appears | `Calculating solar access…`, `{{count}} panels`, `Month {{month}} of 12`, `~{{count}} min left`, `Preparing…` | `screens/Step6Editor.tsx:402` (pass onProgress) and move `lib/solar-heatmap.ts:192` into `workers/analysis.worker.ts` | M |
| **Reading the loss breakdown** | An 86 px label column at 11.5 px holds ~90-character strings ("DC wiring — your cable runs (4 strings, 118 m of 6 mm² Cu, 0.42% at full load)"); **every bar saturates at 12%, so a 12% loss and a 25% loss draw the same length**; colours cycle a 6-entry array by index, so the same colour means different losses on different designs | Seven named losses, each an editable row, with a Total and two locked entries explained in a sentence | `components/EnergyReportSheet.tsx:174` (label width), `:178` (`(l.pct / 12) * 100`), `:181-183` (index-cycled colours) | S |
| **Moving the sun with a thumb** | Time-of-day slider `height: 26` and 3D month slider `height: 26` (`three/Scene3D.tsx:1981, :2137`); the 2D month slider has **no height at all**, so it renders at the UA default ~16-20 px (`screens/Step6Editor.tsx:1520-1526`). DESIGN-SYSTEM N2 says ≥44×44, "Non-negotiable at 375px" | Built phone-first — their positioning is "India's First Mobile-First 3D Solar Design" | `three/Scene3D.tsx:1981, :2137` and `screens/Step6Editor.tsx:1520-1526` | S |
| **Reading the sun chart on a phone** | A fixed panel `W + 24` = 484 px anchored `right: 126` — **it needs 610 px measured from the right edge on a 390 px screen**, so ~220 px hangs off-screen with no scroll container; the close button is a 14 px `<X>` | Inline chips on the step (`Sunrise:`, `Sunset:`, `Alt:`), not a floating desktop panel | `three/SunChart.tsx:24, :129-143, :149-156` | S |
| **Naming the thing** | One feature, four names in three places: 3D radial "Heat / Solar access heatmap / Sun-hours on the roof, by month" (`three/Scene3D.tsx:1141-1145`); 2D radial "Heat / Irradiance heatmap" (`screens/Step6Editor.tsx:1053-1057`); both legends title it "Solar access"; the 2D readout says "% avg access". **The metric is none of those three things — it is a diffuse-floored beam-clear fraction** | One string everywhere: `Show solar access heatmap` / `Solar Access` | `screens/Step6Editor.tsx:1056` and `three/Scene3D.tsx:1144` | S |
| **Finding the bankable number** | **P90 exists in exactly one place in the whole product** — inside the Energy Report sheet at fontSize 11.5, opacity 0.9, on a blue gradient, in a run-on sentence carrying P99, sigma, the interannual split and two "assumed" caveats. grep p90Kwh in `screens/` = 0. **The customer proposal never prints it** | They have no P90 at all — **this is ours to lose, and today we lose it by burying it** | `components/EnergyReportSheet.tsx:88-96` and `screens/ProposalView.tsx:280-290` | S |

#### Bugs found in this dimension — 21 (20 here, plus the shadow-frustum finding cross-referenced from the 3D dimension)

| Bug | Evidence | Sev |
|---|---|---|
| **The heatmap silently ignores the neighbouring buildings — the roof map and the module numbers answer two different questions, and the heatmap is the one that reaches the customer** | `lib/solar-heatmap.ts:237` calls `buildShadowCasters(project)` with no options; `lib/scene-model.ts:189` defaults `opts = {}` and `:309` gates the DSM block on `if (opts.surround)`. `computeHeatmap`'s opts type (`:194-202`) has no surround field and **neither call site could pass one** (`three/Scene3D.tsx:809-813`, `screens/Step6Editor.tsx:402`), while `lib/shading.ts:208-211` does. Secondary mismatch in the same pair: the heatmap's eye sits 0.45 m above the deck (`lib/solar-heatmap.ts:111`) while the engine's rays leave the module plane (`lib/shading.ts:155`) | **critical** |
| ✅ **FIXED 2026-09-09 (honest-numbers 6).** ~~Nudging one module by a centimetre throws away the entire 12-month heatmap and re-runs ~540,000 raycasts — twice, once per view~~ | New key `lib/fingerprints.ts heatmapFp` = geometryFp + the neighbourhood suffix (`geometryFp` alone was NOT the right key: switching the real surroundings off must re-key the map, and the masts it casts were not in any key until item 4). New `lib/heatmap-cache.ts`: one module-level result cache (last 4 keys) and ONE in-flight run per key that every asker shares — progress fans out to all of them, the run is cancelled only when the last asker releases. Both `three/Scene3D.tsx` and `screens/Step6Editor.tsx` read it through `peekHeatmap` / `requestHeatmap`; the per-view `heatCacheRef`s are gone. The 2D editor now shows "Computing solar access… NN%" while the map rebuilds (it used to just empty). **Measured on a scratch copy of the owner's project (23 modules, 1303 cells):** an arrow-key nudge moved all 23 modules 0.10 m and the map survived — legend still up, 1303 cells, the progress pill never reappeared; on the old key every module centre is in `shadingFp`, so this rebuilt it. Heat off → on returns the map in **51 ms** from the cache. The pill read 12% → 93% over ~2 s on a fresh build. Note: with the tab visible a fresh build takes well under 3 s; the "26 s" seen earlier in this session was the hidden-tab timer throttling, not the raycast. Gate: `lib/__tests__/heatmap-cache.test.ts` (a nudge keeps `heatmapFp`, an obstruction move and the neighbour switch change it; two askers share one run; a released lone asker cancels it). | ~~high~~ |
| ✅ **FIXED 2026-09-09 (honest-numbers 2).** ~~"Shading — electrical (strings)" is printed in the loss waterfall and never deducted from anything~~ | `lib/energy/report.ts` now computes `electricalPct` ONCE above both engines and composes `electricalFactor = 1 − pct/100` into every downstream figure: hourly branch — `annualKwh`, `monthlyKwh`, `performanceRatio`, `totalLossPct`, lifetime and P-values; monthly branch — `annualKwh`, `monthlyKwh`, `pr` (after `reportPoa` is taken, so orientation stays a non-loss). The row now sits directly under the near-shading row it adds to. **Measured on the owner's project (hourly engine, row = 0.5%):** PR 77.7 → 77.3%, Total System Loss 22.3 → 22.7%, headline 20.4 → 20.3 MWh, lifetime 465.6 → 463.3 MWh; the twelve printed rows now multiply out to 0.773 = the PR. Gate: `lib/__tests__/energy-report-electrical.test.ts` pins `on.annualKwh = off.annualKwh × (1 − row)` and `1 − total = Π(1 − row)` for BOTH engines (hourly via a mocked typical year); on the old code it fails by the whole string term (3.8 MWh monthly, 4.5 MWh hourly). | ~~high~~ |
| **Two entirely different energy engines, chosen by whether one network fetch succeeded, with no marker on the customer document** | `store/useTmySync.ts:15-16` declares module-scope `skippedWeather`/`skippedTmy` Sets; the only mutations are `.add` at `:53` and `:95` — **no delete, no clear, no retry**, so one failed PVGIS call on a rooftop 4G connection pins the whole session to the weaker engine. `lib/energy/report.ts:102-103` runs the hourly engine only `if (tmyYear)`; the monthly fallback returns the **same** `irradianceSource: 'PVGIS'` (`:223`) with 5 constants including a fixed 8.1% temperature loss for every site in India. `r.engine` is read in exactly one place in the whole UI | high |
| ✅ **FIXED 2026-09-09 (honest-numbers 4a, 4c).** ~~The isotropic sky-view factor uses 1 − sin(β) where the physics is cos²β~~ | New `lib/energy/sky-view.ts`: `skyViewFromSkyline` is cos²β averaged over azimuth, no floor (a uniform 30° skyline → 0.75, 15° → 0.933, 80° → 0.03); `moduleSkyViews` gives EVERY enabled module its own skyline, raycast with `lib/sun-chart skylineOf` against the engine's caster group (parapets, masts, obstructions, neighbours, and the row in front — `includePanels: true`, other plates within 8 m), from the module plane, at 30° azimuth × 3° elevation, cached on `shadingFp` + whether the grid is loaded. `hourly.ts` reads `EnginePanel.skyView` / `horizonView` per module (`input.skyView` is now the array mean, kept for the tests and any one-number reader) and the rear side uses the same per-module factor. Fixture: a module 0.7 m from a 1 m parapet reads skyView < 0.85 while the one mid-deck reads > 0.9. Gate: `lib/__tests__/hourly-sky-view.test.ts`. | ~~high~~ |
| ✅ **FIXED 2026-09-09 (honest-numbers 4b, 4c).** ~~The horizon-brightening band and the ground-reflected component pass through completely unshaded~~ | `lib/energy/hourly.ts` now reads `poaShaded = beam·access + iso·sv + circ·access + hor·hv + ground·lit`, per module: `hv` is the share of the Perez horizon band (6.5° deep) still open under the module's own skyline, and `lit = (dni·cosZ·access + dhi·sv) / ghi` is what lights the ground in front of it — the same beam and the same sky that light the module, so a shadow lying on the ground reflects less. `poaEff` (after IAM) uses the same terms. Gate: a module with no sky and no sun now makes nothing; on the old code it made **553 kWh/yr** of phantom energy on a 5.4 kWp string (4.7% of the open figure) from these two terms alone. **Measured on the owner's project after items 3 + 4 (hourly engine, no parapet, neighbours off):** near-shading row 1.5 → 1.9%, PR 77.3 → 77.0%, Total System Loss 22.7 → 23%, headline 20.3 → 20.2 MWh. A parapet site moves far more — the fixture above. Still open: the report reads the neighbourhood grid through `peekSurroundHeights`, so on a fresh page load before the 3D scene has loaded it, the sky view ignores neighbours (same as the old code did). | ~~high~~ |
| ✅ **FIXED 2026-09-09 (honest-numbers 1).** ~~Solar access can never read below 35% while every legend claims a 0-100% scale~~ | The floor stays (it is the metric's deliberate contrast floor — `lib/solar-heatmap.ts` loop comment). Skyline per azimuth = highest blocked elevation (2° scan from the zenith, bisected to 0.25°). Two deliberate exclusions, both said on-screen: guardrails (a skyline is solid below its line; a bar at 74° would wall off the whole morning) and the array's own modules (row-on-row shade is in the module figures; `includeModules` exists for the sky-view use in item 4). `HorizonProfile.sources` now counts parapets and masts and the chart legend names them. **Measured on the owner's project (4 obstruction blocks, no parapet, surround off):** skyline "highest 7° at 0°, corner 10° toward 87°" → "**33° at 0°, corner 40° toward 84°**"; 21 Jun shade window 6:05–6:20 → 6:05–7:05 AM; 21 Dec gains 5:05–5:50 PM. Fixture: a 1 m parapet 2 m from a module lifts the south skyline 0° → 24.25°, a 3 m mast 2 m north lifts it 0° → 54.5°; a 24-module corners profile costs 64 ms. Gate: `lib/__tests__/sun-chart-horizon.test.ts` (both read 0° on the old code). | **The time-of-day scrubber cannot reach hours the shading engine already counts** | `three/Scene3D.tsx:1975-1976` — slider `min={5} max={19}` in solar hours; `lib/shading.ts:72-73` samples 4→20 h and `lib/solar-heatmap.ts:161` does the same. North of ~30°N in June solar sunrise is before 05:00 (Srinagar 04:52 on 21 June), so **the user cannot scrub to a morning the engine has already priced** — the exact class of scrub/engine disagreement `lib/shading.ts:63-70` says it exists to remove | medium |
| **The per-module kWh number and the caster-cost figures are built on a five-month, 08:00-17:00 grid that systematically mis-values east and west roofs** | `lib/poa.ts:10-12` — `SAMPLE_MONTHS = [0,2,5,8,11]`, `SAMPLE_HOURS = [8..17]`. `poaBeamRatio` feeds `lib/energy/report.ts:200, :316`, `components/PanelYieldCard.tsx:36`, `casterCost` at `report.ts:344`, and `lib/solar-heatmap.ts:271-295` — **the kWh layer that wears the "Real · PVGIS" badge**. An east roof earns its best incidence at 06:00-08:00, outside the window. The same file uses `new Date().getFullYear()` at `:46` with no year in its cache keys — the defect `lib/shading.ts:76-82` already fixed by pinning SAMPLE_YEAR | medium |
| **When the in-memory shade profile is gone, the hourly engine silently flattens the year to each module's annual mean and the electrical-shading line disappears** | `lib/shade-profile-cache.ts:19` is a module-level `let current`, wiped on every reload. `lib/energy/hourly.ts:445-446` falls back to `p.solarAccess ?? 1` — the **annual mean for all 8760 hours**, erasing seasonality; `lib/string-shade.ts:86` returns null so the electrical row vanishes. `isShadingFresh` compares only the stamp, which IS fresh after a reload, **so no provisional banner shows**; the sole disclosure is a fontSize-10, opacity-0.7 sentence. `lib/derive/outputs.ts:20-26` documents the yield discrepancy this already caused on /proposal | medium |
| **The report says "shade by the hour" when the geometry is evaluated ~288 times a year and keyed by calendar month** | `components/EnergyReportSheet.tsx:104` claims "8760 hours × every module: Perez sky, shade by the hour…". `lib/energy/hourly.ts:299` indexes `beamAccess(month, solarHour)` and `lib/shading.ts:53-55` samples **the 21st of each month only**; `:28` states it is "a quadrature of the real integral, not the real integral". The 21st is five days past mid-month, so the bias is systematic: at Delhi a ±15-day declination swing moves the shadow edge behind a 3 m parapet by ~±1.2 m. **The irradiance IS hourly; the geometry is not, and the copy does not distinguish them** | medium |
| **The 2D heatmap legend drops the two readouts that carry provenance — on the touch surface an EPC actually uses on a roof** | `screens/Step6Editor.tsx:1486` labels itself "2D parity with the 3D view" then renders only the month, the average, the gradient and four band words. The 3D legend also renders `monthlyRoofHours` sun-hours/day (`three/Scene3D.tsx:2076`) and `monthlyRoofKwh` with the "Real · PVGIS" badge (`:2088-2103`). **All three values are already on the same object** | medium |
| **At night the sun widget reports "Alt 0°" instead of saying the sun is down, and the shadow map clips below ~34° so the picture and the engine part company during Play** | `three/Scene3D.tsx:1700` clamps with `Math.max(0, altDeg)` and hides the sign — ResLink ships the string `Below horizon` for this state. Separately `:2712-2720` documents the frustum as holding the shadow "down to a sun altitude of about 34°. Lower than that it clips again, and that is a deliberate trade" — **nothing on screen says so**, so during Play the drawn shadow stops growing at exactly the low-sun hours the engine is still integrating. (ResLink hardcodes `INDIA_MIN_SUN_ALTITUDE_DEG: 35`, so both products blind themselves at about the same altitude — **ours is worse only because our engine keeps going and never says the picture stopped**) | low |
| **Three hand-synchronised copies of one sampling question, kept aligned by comment** | `lib/shading.ts:92-126 buildSunSamples`; `lib/solar-heatmap.ts:145-180 buildMonthlySamples`, whose docstring calls it "DELIBERATELY IDENTICAL"; and `lib/poa.ts:10-12`, a third grid with different months and hours. The import path for unifying them already exists (`lib/solar-heatmap.ts:17` imports SAMPLE_YEAR) and was not taken | low |

---

### Stringing & SLD

**Verdict.** Our stringing **engine** is clearly ahead — temperature-correct series windows that refuse an impossible panel/inverter pair by name, plane/azimuth/tilt/shade grouping, PVsyst-style inverter balancing, per-string DC cable sized against both the fuse and the drop over its real routed loop. The **sheet** is behind theirs on nearly every axis a DISCOM reads, and three hardcodes make it contradict our own BOM on documents that leave the building.

**Two headlines.**

- **The hardcoded AC cable size printed on the DISCOM sheet.** `lib/sld.ts:52` — `acCableSizeMm2: inv.phases === 3 ? 10 : 6`, a bare literal sitting between `dcCableSizeMm2`, `dcFuseA` and `dcIsolatorA`, all correctly derived. It renders on the DISCOM sheet (`screens/Step8Sld.tsx:496, :502`) **and** on the customer proposal (`screens/ProposalView.tsx:498-499`), while the BOM (`lib/bom/emitters/electrical.ts:47`) and the cable schedule (`screens/CableScheduleSheet.tsx:82`) call `sizeAcCable`. Verified by executing the real code over the whole catalogue: Sungrow SG15RT ×2 = 30 kW → MCCB 63 A, sheet prints 10 mm², derived **16**. Amaze 30 kVA ×2 = 60 kW → MCCB 125 A, sheet prints 10 mm², derived **50**. Amaze ×4 = 120 kW → MCCB 250 A, sheet prints 10 mm², derived **150**. **10 mm² carries 55 A.** Only 2 of 36 catalogue combinations happen to agree — 0 of 24 on three-phase. `lib/electrical-sizing.ts:120-124` documents this exact constant as the bug it was written to replace, and `lib/sld.ts:16-17` claims "the sheet and the quote can never print two different ratings for one system."
- **Manual stringing is something they have and we effectively do not.** ResLink: `Manual String` ("Tap panels one by one to build custom strings"), then `{{count}} unassigned`, `Undo last panel`, `Clear All`, `Done String`. Ours, in 2D — the view a phone uses — refuses **every** tap. `screens/Step6Editor.tsx:805-809` rejects any module belonging to **any** string, autos included, and `store/useElectricalSync.ts:23-34` re-derives the auto plan on a 150 ms debounce so every enabled module always belongs to one. Five taps to reach a mode that cannot do anything; the only exit is Esc. **The 3D handler has no such guard** (`three/Scene3D.tsx:2365`) **and the op itself refuses only `s.manual` owners** (`lib/ops/electrical-ops.ts:55`) — 2D is stricter than both the op and the other view, so **the phone gets the broken half**.

| **Deleting an inverter silently re-points existing strings at a different physical box, and the wrong INV number prints on the SLD, the DXF and the installation sheet** | `InverterPlacement` has a stable `id` (`types.ts:689`) but `PvString` carries `inverterIndex: number` (`types.ts:735`), resolved positionally (`lib/routing.ts:478-479`). `lib/ops/electrical-ops.ts:187` deletes by id **with no remap**, and `:149` has the same shape when the count shrinks. The `assumedTarget` honesty flag (`lib/routing.ts:497`) fires only for the out-of-range case, **so the silently-remapped case is reported as measured**. Those indices print at `screens/Step8Sld.tsx:576`, `lib/export-dxf.ts:115` and `screens/CableScheduleSheet.tsx:50` | high |
| **"Save to INV n" is refused with a wrong reason on any design whose MPPTs are filled by AUTO strings — i.e. the normal state** | `lib/ops/electrical-ops.ts:44-48` builds `used` from ALL strings while its own `apply` at `:106` keeps only manual strings and re-plans the rest — **the validator refuses on slots the apply is about to free**. `components/InverterBay.tsx:98` greys the card from the same arithmetic. **The feature is dead exactly when slots are tight — exactly when a C&I engineer would use it** | medium |
| **String numbering goes non-contiguous and can collide after any hand-wired string, and those names become the cable tags an installer pulls from** | `lib/ops/electrical-ops.ts:64` takes `idx = p.strings.length` (autos included); the sync then discards the autos and re-plans with `nameOffset: kept.length` (`lib/electrical/derive-strings.ts:60`). On a 60-module / 3-inverter design auto-strung to String 1..6, hand-wiring one yields String 7 plus re-planned String 2..6 — **no String 1**. With fewer autos the manual name can equal an auto name. Those names become the schedule rows (`screens/Step8Sld.tsx:576`) and the tags `DC-S7+` / `DC-S7−` (`screens/CableScheduleSheet.tsx:48`) | medium |
| **`earth_conductor` is declared as a route kind, mapped to a DXF layer, exercised only by a test fixture, and produced by nothing** | `types.ts:709`, `lib/export-dxf.ts:42`, `lib/__tests__/dxf.test.ts:129` — three references, no producer. `syncElectrical` (`lib/derive/electrical-sync.ts:47`) composes routes from string home-runs, AC and battery only. **IEC 62548 requires the array earth conductor to be sized against the DC circuit; we ship an empty layer on the drawing and an untraced allowance in the BOM** | medium |
| **The layout sheet's legend advertises three symbols it never draws, and the sheet titled "DC STRING CABLE ROUTE" draws the series order** | `screens/Step8Sld.tsx:715-717` lists Walkway, Safety rail and Lightning arrester; `LayoutSheet` (`:645-757`) draws roofs, panels, obstructions and a north arrow only. `:714` promises "Obstruction + buffer" while `:694` draws every obstruction as a circle whatever its shape or rotation. `StringSheet` (`:754-812`) builds its path from `s.panelIds`; grep cableRoutes in that file = **0**, even though the routes are drawn in 2D, billed, scheduled and exported to `PV-DC-CABLE`. Same file: `:726` hardcodes `PV TABLE SECTION (10° TILT)` and "HDG steel legs · chemical anchor" reading no project data, and `:655 / :761 / :802` number a five-tab pack "SHEET 1 OF 2" and "2 OF 2" | medium |

**We already beat them at — 14**

| **A cold-Voc MAX SYSTEM VOLTAGE check drawn on the sheet**, at the site's design-minimum temperature, green/red with the exceedance named | `lib/sld.ts:26-34` derives `maxSystemVdc` from the LONGEST REAL string × `vocAt(panel, temps.minCellC)`; `screens/Step8Sld.tsx:436-457` draws it with "OVER LIMIT — shorten the string". **Nothing equivalent in their inventory** |
| **A refusal to draw an SLD from unstrung panels**, rather than fabricating an impossible all-panels series string | `screens/Step8Sld.tsx:270` gates on `hasStrings`; `UnstrungState` offers "Try again" and "String manually in the editor". The comment at `:271-274` records that the fabricated version was the audit's worst finding |

Proved live: Adani ELAN Shine 540 + Amaze AN HKVA 30 KVA-360V (MPPT 540-730 V) needs 16 in series when hot and fits only 14 when cold — **the planner leaves all 185 modules unstrung and says why** |
| **Grouping that constrains what may share a series circuit** — roof PLANE (co-planar union-find, not roofId), azimuth ±5°, tilt ±3°, shade tier, plus an MLPE mega-group | `lib/electrical/grouping.ts:1-20`; `computePlaneIds` merges co-planar skeleton faces; `serpentine` (`:312-340`) orders modules in the roof's own grid frame so the string reads the way the cable runs. **Their bundle shows only "Auto String" as an opaque action** |
| **PVsyst-style inverter/MPPT assignment** — largest-string-first, one tracker per string while trackers are free, identical strings only in parallel, lowest-DC-load inverter first — plus per-inverter Pnom ratio checks | `lib/electrical/balance.ts:1-22` documents all five rules; `lib/stringing.ts:139-165` warns above 1.35 / below 0.90 and flags an inverter carrying no strings |
| **DC cable sized PER STRING against both the fuse rating and the voltage drop over its real routed loop**, with the governing criterion named and one BOM line per size | `lib/electrical-sizing.ts:70-86` returns `{mm2, ampacityMm2, dropMm2, governedBy, dropPct, withinLimit}`; `lib/electrical/dc-cable.ts:67-93` splits the buy. **Their rule is a flat lookup: "DC cable mm² by kWp: ≤10 → 4 · ≤50 → 6 · else 10"** |
| **AC cable sized by breaker coordination AND drop over the routed run**, not a kW→mm² table | `lib/electrical-sizing.ts:194-216`; its docblock (`:180-192`) explains why ampacity alone would have been worse than the constant it replaced. **Theirs: "3-phase: ≤15 → 10 · ≤30 → 16 · ≤60 → 25 · ≤120 → 35 · else 50"**. (Note: our SLD sheet still prints the constant — see the critical bug above) |

---

## Gap register — BOM and proposal

### BOM / BOS automation

**Verdict.** Our engine is ahead on physics, provenance and edit-safety — we size cable per string
from real Isc and routed voltage drop where they use a kWp lookup table, we carry four provenance
tiers on every line where they carry none, and our per-field override model already beats their
`userEditedFields`. We are behind on **coverage and paperwork**: 14 procurement lines we emit
nothing for, no supplier or notes field, no Excel, no standards in the spec string, and the price
is set on Step 9 **after** the customer proposal has already been generated on Step 7.

| # | Gap | ResLink evidence | Ours today | Why it matters | E/P |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Module Cleaning Kit** | `pv-modules`: "Solar PV Modules · Module Cleaning Kit" | Only `modules.panel` exists — `lib/bom/registry.ts:14-94`; `lib/bom/emitters/modules.ts` is 21 lines, one line out. `grep -i cleaning` across `lib/bom/`, `data/pricebook.ts`, `data/catalog.ts` → 0 | Every Indian rooftop quote carries one; C&I carries a cleaning system with a water tap-off. The EPC adds it by hand or eats it | S / med |
| 2 | **Inverter Mounting Kit and Inverter Shed** | `inverters`: "Grid-Connected Solar Inverter · DC Combiner / SMB Box · Inverter Mounting Kit · Inverter Shed" | `lib/bom/emitters/inverter.ts:6-21` emits `inverter.unit` and nothing else; `registry.ts:17` declares one inverter key. No bracket, canopy or shed anywhere in `lib/bom` | An inverter on an Indian terrace without a canopy derates and the warranty gets argued. The mounting kit is a real ₹1,500–4,000 per box, currently invisible | S / med |
| 3 | **DC and AC Cable Glands & Lugs, quantified from cable length** | Two items, sizing rule written into the spec: "1 set covers ~10 m of DC cable" (qty = ceil(length/10)) | `grep -i gland` / `lug` across `lib/bom/` → 0. `lib/bom/emitters/electrical.ts:23-261` emits cable, MC4, DCDB, ACDB, conduit, meters, optimiser, combiner, string fuses — **no termination line of any kind** | Every real Indian BOQ has a termination kit; on 100 kW it is tens of thousands. We already hold the metres (`dcCableM`, `acRunM` — `lib/bom/context.ts:64-65`), so it is one emitter | S / **high** |

#### Their two mechanisms, and how ours actually compare

**1. `userEditedFields` — auto fields keep recomputing, typed fields survive.** Their recompute
pass walks `["itemName","brand","specification","quantity","unit","wastagePct","gstPct"]` and skips
any field the user has touched; the UI shows a per-field `hasAutoRefresh` badge with an `onRefresh`
button to re-take the computed value.

> **We already do this, and better on paper.** `OVERRIDABLE_FIELDS` (`lib/bom/merge.ts:26-36`) is
> **nine** fields against their seven (we add `unitPriceInr` and `included`), keyed on a stable
> semantic `LineKey` rather than `category|item` — `registry.ts:1-94` documents why the string key
> collides. Three real gaps remain, all cheap:
> - **No `supplier`, no `notes`** (`types.ts:917-968`) — so the two fields their EPCs type most have
>   nowhere to live in our tool.
> - **Our "take the computed value" control is per-field and good** (`StaleBanner.tsx:84-103`), but
>   **our derivation ⓘ is hover-only with no onClick** (`BomRow.tsx:262-268`) — on a phone the
>   formula that justifies the number is unreachable, while their refresh badge is a real tappable
>   button.
> - **Orphan rescue throws away the very edits it claims to keep**: `screens/Step9Bom/index.tsx:109-129`
>   hardcodes `category:'Civil & Misc'`, `gstPct:18`, `wastePct:0`, `spec:''` and reads only
>   item/qty/unit/price from `BomOrphan.fields`, so a rescued Modules line lands taxed at **18%
>   instead of 5%** and the brand/spec/waste edits are dropped.

**2. `...AtBurn` staleness stamps.** Each of their lines records the design state it was costed
against — `stringsAtBurn`, `inverterCountAtBurn`, `totalCableLengthAtBurn`, `kwpAtBurn`,
`panelCountAtBurn`, `totalStringsAtBurn` — and a dirty-check flags exactly which lines are stale.
Plus `dismissedBanners`, so a warning stays dismissed.

> **This solves a problem we designed away.** `lib/bom.ts:53-58` re-runs all seven emitters over a
> fresh `buildContext(project)` on every read, memoised on the design fingerprint
> (`lib/derive/outputs.ts:34`), not on a persisted document — so a non-overridden line **cannot** be
> stale and there is nothing to stamp. Three honest caveats:
> - **The one thing that does go stale is money.** `lib/fingerprints.ts:183` serialises
>   `p.pricing.marginPct` and **not** `p.pricing.discount`, so the memo key does not move when a
>   discount is entered. Reproduced live: discounted total should be ₹249,183; the warm cache
>   returns ₹276,871 with `discount: 0`. Our "re-derive live" architecture is right and this single
>   omitted field breaks it — an `...AtBurn` stamp would have caught it.
> - **We have no `dismissedBanners`.** `StaleBanner` and `OrphanBanner` re-fire on every open, so a
>   decision already taken looks like an unresolved problem.
> - **Our per-field staleness is genuinely better where it works**: `merge.ts:172-181` captures
>   `{field, yours, wasAtEdit, now}` and `StaleBanner.tsx:84-103` renders "yours X · design now Y"
>   with a per-field **Take Y**. Their badge re-takes the computed value **without showing the
>   delta**. Keep this.

**Provenance, stated honestly.** We ship the only provenance system in this comparison: four tiers
(measured / derived / estimated / assumed) declared at `types.ts:917-939`, rolled up by
`lib/bom.ts:87-118` into a quote badge with a named verification list, rendered as a labelled dot
(`BomRow.tsx:17-29`) and exported as its own CSV column (`lib/bom.ts:212`). ResLink has no
equivalent concept anywhere. Two things must be said with it:

- **Claim the system, not blanket coverage.** Four tiers on **every BOM line** is defensible.
  "Provenance on any number" is not: `lib/roof-factory.ts:131-153` never sets `heightSource`,
  `lib/roof-map-fit.ts:100-102` stamps a median height as `aerial_map`, and
  `lib/roof-ai/gemini-client.ts:82-85` carries a confidence on a height taken from a preset.
- **The badge currently fires on 100% of designs, so it says nothing.** `safety.ts:81-113` pushes
  `safety.earth_pit` and `safety.earth_strip` unconditionally at `assumed`, and the default AC cable
  is `assumed` until a meter is placed (`electrical.ts:122-123`); `lib/bom.ts:116` then sets
  `preliminary` on **any** assumed line. Every quote we have ever produced is stamped PRELIMINARY and
  lists the same five items. A threshold at `lib/bom.ts:116` is an S fix and restores the signal.

#### We already beat them at

| They have nothing like | Ours | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| **Any provenance on any BOM number** | Four tiers per line, rolled up to a quote badge with a named "needs site verification" list, exported as a CSV column | `types.ts:917-939`, `lib/bom.ts:87-118`, `BomRow.tsx:17-29`, `lib/bom.ts:212` |
| **A human-readable derivation per line** | `formula` required on every constructed line and exported — *"Routed home runs 41 m + 12 m inter-row hops (incl. 10% slack, 1.5 m drop/run) · Conductor 6 sq.mm for 3 strings: governed by VOLTAGE DROP on 2 of them"* | `lib/bom/line.ts:15-33`, `lib/bom.ts:187,213` |
| **Per-field staleness that shows the delta** | Three numbers — what you typed, what the engine said then, what it says now — with a per-field **Take Y** | `lib/bom/merge.ts:172-181`, `types.ts:970-978`, `StaleBanner.tsx:84-103` |
| **Live re-derivation** (their whole `...AtBurn` mechanism exists to patch a persisted document) | Seven emitters re-run over a fresh context on every read, memoised on the design fingerprint | `lib/bom.ts:53-58`, `lib/derive/outputs.ts:34` |
| **Orphan rescue** | An edit whose line the design no longer produces is surfaced with keep-as-custom / discard instead of silently dropped | `lib/bom/merge.ts:43-60,144-168`, `OrphanBanner.tsx` |
| **Per-line GST with pro-rata discount, margin below tax, below-cost guard** (theirs defaults 5% flat on every line) | Discount apportioned across the 5% and 18% buckets pro-rata, margin ordered below GST so the **declared taxable value** is right, and a warning when the discount sells below buy cost | `lib/bom/money.ts:1-24,136-187,117`, `Step9Bom/index.tsx:292-302` |
| **Design qty vs order qty** (their tool prints `116.15 Nos`) | Two columns, discrete units ceil, and `unit` constrained to a fixed list so a typed "pcs" cannot break the ceiling | `registry.ts:140-173`, `lib/bom/money.ts:36-40` |
| **A real steel member model** (theirs is two bundled MMS items) | One line per structural profile — *"HDG C-Channel · 80 × 40 × 8 × 1.6 · 2.2 kg/m · IS 2062 · HDG ≥ 80 µm"* — with the per-segment member breakdown in the formula | `mechanical.ts:196-228` |
| **Foundations as quantified lines** (theirs is "Concrete + Chemical Anchor") | Base plates with the anchor count off the node graph, ballast, driven piles, PCC pedestals reporting **concrete m³ and the kg of dead load added to the roof** | `mechanical.ts:235-325` |
| **Cable sized from physics** (theirs is a kWp lookup: ≤10 → 4 · ≤50 → 6 · else 10) | Per string from real module Isc, the string fuse **and** voltage drop over the routed loop, with one BOM line per conductor size and the governing criterion named | `electrical.ts:56-111`, `lib/electrical-sizing.ts:16-60` |
| **Battery storage at all** | Bank, DC leads measured off the placed cabinets, isolator + fuse per cabinet, AC-coupled interface | `emitters/battery.ts:12-72` |
| **Trackers or MLPE** (they model string vs central only) | Torque-tube metres, one bearing per post, drive units per row, a control unit — every one labelled ASSUMED market rate — plus per-module optimisers | `mechanical.ts:548-625`, `electrical.ts:216-229` |
| **Metal-shed penetration accounting** | Sheet standoffs counted off the node graph and **exactly one EPDM sealing washer per hole** — *"Every fixing is a hole in the roof"* | `mechanical.ts:473-499` |
| **Pitched hardware per covering** (not averaged) | Tile hooks + lead/EPDM flashing vs L-feet + PU sealant, each with its own price, its own note, and the covering as the `instance` so they cannot collide | `mechanical.ts:73-112,378-419` |
| **Free-field site works** (they draw walkways and rails in step 5 and bill none of the three) | Perimeter fencing measured off the boundary, gates, buried earthing ring, plus walkway m² and safety rail m billed from drawn geometry | `emitters/safety.ts:126-182,10-39` |
| **Site-dependent scope made visible** | Crane, scaffolding, civil works and trenching ship at qty 0 and **excluded** — *"Omitting them silently is how they end up missing from the quote and eaten as margin"* | `emitters/civil.ts:34-82` |
