### Why the tests did not catch these

**Every gate is green while all 176 ship.** Verified live this session:

| Gate | Result |
|---|---|
| `npx tsc --noEmit` | **0 errors** |
| `npx vitest run` | **1859/1859 passed**, 148 test files |
| `npm run cycles` | **0 dependency violations** |
| `npm run lint` | **0 errors** |
| `npm run dead` (knip) | at its 2 known findings |

Two structural reasons, not one.

**1. Screen code has no render coverage at all.** Of 150 test files, **one renders screen code**
(`axe.dom.test.tsx` → `BomRow`). Nothing renders `Scene3D` (4,029 lines), `Step6Editor` (3,753),
`Step2Roof` (3,378), `Step3Obstructions` (1,128), `Step4Components` (987), `Step8Sld` (948),
`Step1Setup` (771) or `ProposalView` (579) — **12,573 lines, 15 % of the tree**, and the location of
every layout, touch-target, dead-control and off-screen defect above. The 240 px off-screen Next
button, the unreachable Delete Roof, the button with no `onClick`, the 197 inputs under 16 px: all
machine-checkable, none checked.

**2. The tests that do cover `lib/` assert invariants, not values.** The discount fingerprint
(`lib/fingerprints.ts:183`), the ground pedestals (`lib/bom/context.ts:208-212`), the monorail work
order (`lib/installation.ts:97-145`), `parallelPerMppt` (`lib/electrical/autostring.ts:59`), the
inverter index shift (`lib/ops/electrical-ops.ts:145-149`), string naming
(`lib/ops/electrical-ops.ts:64`), the gable flatten (`lib/roof-factory.ts:131-153`), the sky-view
factor (`lib/energy/hourly.ts:439`), the electrical-shading loss (`lib/energy/report.ts:128-132`),
the end clamps and the rail metres (`lib/bom/emitters/mechanical.ts:444,467`) are **all in `lib/`,
all covered by unit tests, and all green.** The clearest example: `lib/__tests__/solar.test.ts:112-116`
asserts only `totalLossPct < sum(losses)`, which multiplicative composition satisfies trivially — so
a loss line that is printed and never deducted passes. `lib/__tests__/bom-discount.test.ts` tests
`bomMoney` directly and therefore cannot see that the memo never reaches it.

The one-frame gate (`lib/__tests__/one-frame.test.ts`) is the right idea and imports **only panel
functions** — structure, rails and foundations are outside it, which is why the monorail work order
ships green.

**What would have caught them.** A screen-level render test at 375 px asserting three
machine-checkable facts — no horizontal document overflow, no interactive element under 44×44, no
computed font below 12 px — plus value assertions (not inequality assertions) on the money and loss
paths. Neither exists today.


---

## Hyperrealism — how to make the scene beat theirs

Their entire realism is ten GLB props plus a satellite texture on **one directional light with a 2048 shadow map** — `meshStandardMaterial` throughout (glass at `roughness .12 / metalness .55`, which is physically wrong), and the bundle shows "No tone mapping, no env map / IBL, no PMREM, no post-processing stack" (inventory §"How their 3D actually works", lines 508-523).

We already have the better pipeline — ACES filmic tone mapping and an HDR HalfFloat buffer (`three/ScenePost.tsx:33-45`), an IBL, N8AO + SMAA, clearcoat physical glass (`three/textures.ts:214-224`), a module face drawn from the datasheet (`three/textures.ts:40-42, 97-158`), a 4096 shadow map (`three/Scene3D.tsx:2838-2839`), a live Preetham sky (`:2801-2811`) and DSM neighbour relief (`three/SurroundRelief.tsx:56-125`). **Every item below is tuning a pipeline that already exists, not building one.** Nine of the top ten are S.

---

### The order

**1. Bake the sky, the sun disc and the ground into the environment map**
What it does: today the IBL is three flat colour rects — `#dbe7f7` sky, `#f6ecd8` horizon, `#6b6357` bounce — at `resolution={128}`, and the live `<Sky>` and the satellite ground sit **outside** it. Every reflective surface mirrors a featureless slab. The code already documents the symptom: `three/textures.ts:220-222` — "the environment's overhead light panel fills a module's whole mirror image; at full strength every cell read as sky-grey" → `envMapIntensity: 0.2`. Render the existing sky + sun + ground into the Environment at 256, then restore glass **and** frame to `envMapIntensity: 1.0`.
File: `three/Scene3D.tsx:2746-2770`, `three/textures.ts:200, 214-224` · **Effort S** · **Payoff high**
Why it is next: the `0.2` was a fix applied to a symptom. Undoing it is what turns glass into glass and the frame from grey plastic into anodised aluminium — in one edit, on every module in every scene.

**2. Soft shadows with a real penumbra (PCSS)**
What it does: `three/Scene3D.tsx:1401` uses `shadows={{ type: THREE.PCFSoftShadowMap }}` — one fixed kernel for every occluder. A 17 m tree edge and a 35 mm module frame edge are equally crisp. `grep -rn 'SoftShadows|AccumulativeShadows|ContactShadows' src/` returns **zero**. drei 10.7.7 is already a dependency (`package.json:17`) and ships `<SoftShadows/>`.
File: `three/Scene3D.tsx` Canvas children · **Effort S** · **Payoff high**
Why it is next: **the single biggest perceptual jump per line of code.** Equal-blur shadows are the fastest "this is CG" tell there is, and this is one component inside the Canvas.

**3. Drop `shadow-normalBias` from 0.03 to ~0.005-0.01**
What it does: kills peter-panning. `three/Scene3D.tsx:2795` and `:2846` both set `shadow-normalBias={0.03}` — **30 mm of bias on a 45 mm-thick module** (`three/PanelsInstanced.tsx:145`, glass box `[p.w, 0.045, p.d]`) and a 20 mm frame (`:164`). A ribbon of light survives under every module edge and every leg base, so the array reads as floating.
File: `three/Scene3D.tsx:2794-2795, 2845-2846` · **Effort S** · **Payoff high**
Why it is next: re-tune it in the same sitting as #2 — PCSS changes the bias you need, and doing them apart means tuning twice.

**4. Put obstructions inside the shadow frustum**
What it does: `three/scene-bounds.ts:22-56` builds the bounds from `roofs[].polygon` and `project.panels` and **nothing else** — obstructions never enter the loop. `three/Scene3D.tsx:2719` then sizes the only shadow camera from it. Worked example: a 10×12 m house gives `shadowHalf = 20.25 m`, so a 12 m neighbouring building 25 m south draws **no shadow at all** — while `lib/shading.ts` raycasts it correctly and takes the kWh off.
File: `three/scene-bounds.ts:22-56`, `three/Scene3D.tsx:2719` · **Effort S** · **Payoff high**
Why it is next: this is not a beauty item. **The picture contradicts the engineering number** on the exact feature we sell, and CLAUDE.md's one-frame rule says the scene and the model agree. It is a second loop over `project.obstructions` in a bounds function.

**5. Sun colour and intensity versus altitude**
What it does: `three/Scene3D.tsx:2680` — `duskFactor = min(1, max(0, sunAltitude / 0.25))` where `sunAltitude` is **radians**, so 0.25 rad = 14.3°. Above that it pins at 1 and `:2834 intensity={0.4 + 1.35 * duskFactor}` is a flat 1.75 for the whole rest of the day. `:2835 color="#fff4e0"` never changes. 10:00, noon and 16:00 are lit identically; `:2679 sunVisible = sunAltitude > 0` is a hard boolean, so the light dies in one frame at the horizon. Add a colour-temperature ramp and a smooth altitude curve on the `duskFactor` that already exists, plus a twilight crossfade.
File: `three/Scene3D.tsx:2679-2680, 2834-2835` · **Effort S** · **Payoff high**
Why it is next: this is on the screen sold as "Real-Time Sun Path Simulation". **ResLink is ahead of us here** — their sun ramps `1 + alt/45` clamped 0.5-2 and drops to 0.2 below the horizon (inventory §3D, lines 512-515). We are losing a comparison we should win by a mile.

**6. Tie the fog colour to the sky's horizon**
What it does: `three/Scene3D.tsx:2822` fixes fog at `#b9c7d8` while `<Sky>` above it (`:2801-2808`) correctly reddens through Preetham scattering. At 17:30 the sky is orange, the haze is cold blue, and there is a visible band where they meet. Sample the sky's horizon colour for the `FogExp2` set up in `<Atmosphere>` (`:177-194`, density 0.0006).
File: `three/Scene3D.tsx:2802-2848` · **Effort S** · **Payoff medium-high**
Why it is next: same edit block as #5, and the seam it removes is in the middle of every wide shot.

**7. Threshold-gated bloom**
What it does: the effect chain is N8AO + SMAA + ToneMapping and nothing else (`three/ScenePost.tsx:33-45`); `grep 'Bloom' src/` returns zero. A sunlit array rolls off to flat grey with no light bleed on the specular or the sun disc. The HDR HalfFloat buffer that makes threshold bloom cheap and correct is already the default (`@react-three/postprocessing` `frameBufferType = HalfFloatType`), and ACES is already in the chain to absorb it.
File: `three/ScenePost.tsx:40-46` · **Effort S** · **Payoff medium-high**
Why it is next: **the cheapest single change that makes a frame read as a photograph.** One component in an existing chain.

**8. Render the proposal hero offscreen at ~2500 px**
What it does: `three/Scene3D.tsx:1128` — `onCapture(gl.domElement.toDataURL('image/jpeg', 0.85), ...)` grabs the **live canvas** at `dpr={[1,1.5]}` (`:1405`). From a 390 px phone that is ~585 px wide, and it goes straight into the customer PDF, where A4 at 300 dpi wants ~2480 px. Render to an offscreen `WebGLRenderTarget` at ~2500 px with the shadow map and AO samples temporarily raised, encode PNG or JPEG 0.95, restore.
File: `three/Scene3D.tsx:1125-1129`, consumed at `screens/Step7Proposal.tsx:43-64` · **Effort S** · **Payoff high**
Why it is next: **this is the image the homeowner and the CFO judge the product by.** Every item above it is currently being thrown away at 585 px. Their capture flow already exposes `Cover Image` · `Capture All` · `Retake` · `Orbit to adjust angle` and a `Texture Quality` setting on GLB export (inventory §7).

**9. Fix the four props that are geometrically wrong**
What it does: (a) the GLB turbine vent spins its **whole body**, base and kerb included, at ~200°/s — `three/ObstructionMesh.tsx:685-709` puts `rotorRef` on a group wrapping the entire `<Clone>`; the procedural fallback at `:452-486` gets it right and is the template. (b) A rectangular tank or dish draws with a **square footprint using the smaller dimension** — `:614-615` and `:655-656` pass `target` to both X and Z; `ChimneyAsset` (`:632-633`) and `SolarWhAsset` (`:673-674`) already do it correctly. A surveyed 3.0 × 1.5 m tank draws 1.5 × 1.5 m. (c) Both heuristic legs sit under the **same** edge — `three/PanelsInstanced.tsx:179` computes one `legZ` reused at `:181-188`, so the array has no front support and leans. (d) The module is one box with the cell texture on **all six faces** (`three/PanelsInstanced.tsx:145`), so in walkthrough mode the back of a module shows cells.
File: `three/ObstructionMesh.tsx:614-615, 655-656, 685-709`, `three/PanelsInstanced.tsx:145, 179` · **Effort S** · **Payoff high**
Why it is next: no lighting work survives a spinning tank base. (b) also makes the picture disagree with the dimension the surveyor typed, and (c) is what the user sees **first**, before the parametric structure resolves (`three/Scene3D.tsx:2466`).

**10. Ambient occlusion by measured tier, not by User-Agent regex**
What it does: `three/ScenePost.tsx:20-25` — `lowPower()` returns true for any UA matching `/Android|iPhone|iPad|Mobile/i` **or** `hardwareConcurrency <= 4`, and `:31-38` then ships SMAA only, deleting N8AO. AO is the single effect that stops a table looking like it floats, and it is switched off by regex on **100 % of the target devices**, an iPhone 15 Pro included. There is no `AdaptiveDpr`, no `PerformanceMonitor`, no `useDetectGPU` anywhere in `src/`. Replace with a 30-frame probe or `useDetectGPU` feeding three tiers.
File: `three/ScenePost.tsx:13-46` (delete `POST_MODE` and `TONE_MAP_EFFECT`, the two frozen compile-time switches) · **Effort S-M** · **Payoff high**
Why it is next: a flagship Android renders our scene beautifully and we refuse to let it. This also compounds #3 — AO is what would darken the crease that a bad `normalBias` opens.

**11. Compress the 169 MB of GLBs, add a plain/realistic toggle, stop the blanket prefetch**
What it does: measured from the GLB JSON chunks — turbine-vent 39.2 MB / 1,128,385 tris; tree 34.7 MB / 1,141,083; chimney 34.5 MB / 929,969; solar-wh 26.8 MB / 692,340; tank 21.2 MB / 623,983; dish 12.2 MB / 255,255. **Total 168.7 MB / 4,771,015 triangles**, `extensionsUsed` absent on five of six — no Draco, no meshopt, no KTX2, raw float32 geometry, embedded JPEGs up to 3,334 KB against `public/models/obstructions/README.md`'s own "1K or 2K" rule. For scale: the entire 200-module array is **~2,400 triangles** (`three/PanelsInstanced.tsx:135-152`). A 0.4 m turbine vent carries 470× the triangles of the whole design. `three/Scene3D.tsx:2387` `useWarmObstructionAssets` then prefetches every type in the project the moment 3D opens — a normal RCC roof (tank + vent + dish + solar WH) is 99.4 MB before the first frame; add one tree and it is 134.1 MB. A Draco decoder is already wired (`three/RealSurround.tsx:58-59`), so `useGLTF(url, true)` needs no new infrastructure.
File: `public/models/obstructions/` (gltf-transform: Draco/meshopt + `simplify` to ~15k + KTX2 at 1K), `three/ObstructionMesh.tsx:60-67`, `three/Scene3D.tsx:1181-1187, 2387` · **Effort S-M** · **Payoff high (mobile)**
Why it is next: ResLink's one tap — `Switch to realistic models` / `Switch to plain models`, "Plain mode = primitive boxes" — is **the entire reason their 3D works on a phone and ours does not**. Our only view switch is Map/Mesh, which swaps the sky and the ground and drops zero GLBs. Nothing below this line is worth doing on mobile until it is done.

**12. Restore the roof coverings — 226 lines of finished material code are unreachable**
What it does: `three/roof-textures.ts` draws weathered concrete (`:67`), colour-coated trapezoidal sheet at a real 250 mm rib pitch (`:117-131`) and Mangalore clay tile at 420 × 260 mm (`:165-196`), with normal maps, in world units. None of it renders: `three/Scene3D.tsx:3860` computes `surface`, `:3863` computes `photoMat`, and `:3871` is `{photoMat ? null : surface ? (...)}` — in map view `photo` is always non-null (`:2964`, falling back to `groundTex` at `:2674`), so the `surface` branch never runs. `getRoofSurface` is imported at `three/Scene3D.tsx:51` and is **dead in practice**. What ships instead is `roofPhotoMaterial` (`:3768-3796`): the satellite tile planar-projected by world XZ at 1280 px over ~93 m = **7.3 cm/texel** (a 1.7 m module is 23 texels long), multiplied by a magic `vec4(0.46, 0.46, 0.46, 1.0)`, foreshortened by 1/cos(pitch) on every sloped face, carrying the imagery's own baked sun, its own shadows and any existing panels. Restore the procedural covering as base albedo, multiply the photo on top as a stain layer, add a triplanar fallback for pitched faces.
File: `three/roof-textures.ts:218`, `three/Scene3D.tsx:3853-3892` · **Effort M** · **Payoff high**
Why it is next: the roof is the largest surface in every frame and it is currently a 7 cm/texel smear. The material is already written and tested — this is a branch fix, not new work.

**13. Module soiling and per-module variation**
What it does: `three/textures.ts:214-224` — the glass material carries `map` only. No `roughnessMap`, no `normalMap`, no per-instance roughness or colour jitter; the frame at `:200` is a flat `metalness: 0.85, roughness: 0.3` with no anisotropy. 500 modules are pixel-identical and impossibly clean, **in the country where soiling is the most visible rooftop condition there is**. Add per-instance roughness/soiling jitter via a second instanced attribute, a glass-waviness normal, frame anisotropy, and a junction box and MC4 tail.
File: `three/textures.ts:200, 214-224`, `three/PanelsInstanced.tsx:186-200` · **Effort M** · **Payoff high**
Why it is next: it is the difference between a render of modules and a photograph of modules, and it costs nothing per frame — the geometry is already instanced.

**14. Orient the clamps we already bill and draw**
What it does: **correction to the 3D diff** — the mid and end clamps *are* rendered. `three/StructureNodesInstanced.tsx:84` calls `nodeHardware(node.kind)` and `lib/hardware.ts:26-42` gives the mid clamp a 32 × 34 × 46 mm box and the end clamp the wider casting, matching the BOM lines counted at `lib/structure.ts:1033-1034`. The real defect is narrower and still visible: `three/StructureNodesInstanced.tsx:97` creates `const q = new THREE.Quaternion()` and **never sets it**, and `:117 mat.compose(pos, q, scl)` uses that identity. Every clamp, bolted joint, standoff and pedestal is **axis-aligned to world north** regardless of the table's azimuth or tilt — so on any array not facing due south the clamps sit square while the rail runs at an angle under them. Second half: before the parametric structure resolves, `PanelsInstanced` draws legs and no clamps at all.
File: `three/StructureNodesInstanced.tsx:95-119` · **Effort S** · **Payoff medium**
Why it is next: ghosting the modules to inspect the steel is the one thing that view exists for (`lib/hardware.ts:1-7` says so), and it currently shows rotated rails held by unrotated hardware.

**15. Ship meshes for the types that have none**
What it does: `types.ts:221-231` defines **11** `ObstructionType` values. `three/ObstructionMesh.tsx:60-67` gives a GLB to **6** (tank, tree, chimney, dish, solar_wh, turbine_vent). `windmill` renders procedurally on purpose and its folder holds only a `.gitkeep`, with `WINDMILL_REF = { x: 1, z: 1 }` still a placeholder (`:602`). `building` is a tinted box (`:187-206`). `elevated`, `ladder` and `other` fall to `default` at `three/ObstructionMesh.tsx:255-270` — a plain grey `#7d8590` box. **A ladder renders as a grey box.** Against ResLink's **17 types plus four skylight sub-shapes** (Flat glass / Pitched / Tilted / Arched), with `ladder.glb`, `LA.glb`, `fence.glb`, `street_light.glb`, `cars.glb`, `inverter.glb` shipped. We have no AC outdoor unit, no skylight, no light pole, no transformer pad, no fence, no cars.
File: `three/ObstructionMesh.tsx:60-67, 255-270`, `public/models/obstructions/` · **Effort M** · **Payoff high**
Why it is next: realism here is not shader work — it is **having the right objects**. An Indian rooftop the customer recognises has an AC outdoor unit, a ladder, a parapet LA and a Sintex tank, and we draw three of those as grey boxes. Corollary: our LAs, walkways and rails are already first-class project entities (`three/Scene3D.tsx:3353-3396`) that ResLink only draws in 2D — we need meshes for data we already hold.

**16. Tree variety, per-instance phase, LOD**
What it does: `three/ObstructionMesh.tsx:49` — a single `TREE_MODEL_URL` for coconut, neem, banyan and gulmohar alike, **and crown shape is precisely what decides the shading answer**. `:514-521` gives every `TreeAsset` a `t = useRef(0)` starting at zero and `rotation.z = Math.sin(t.current * 0.6) * 0.02`, so five trees lean left and right in perfect unison — the clone-army tell. No LOD, no billboard, no instancing: five trees is 5.7 M triangles rendered twice per frame (colour + shadow). Add 3-4 Indian species, per-instance phase offset and scale jitter, and drei `<Detailed>` with an alpha billboard past ~40 m.
File: `three/ObstructionMesh.tsx:49, 489-536` · **Effort M-L** · **Payoff medium-high**
Why it is next: trees are the tallest, most-photographed thing in most residential frames, and this one is both a realism and a shading-accuracy item.

**17. Cascaded shadow maps**
What it does: one 4096 map covers the whole design box. `three/Scene3D.tsx:2701-2718` states the trade in its own comment: "The allowance is 1.5 × the design's height, which holds the whole shadow down to a sun altitude of about 34°. Lower than that it clips again, and that is a deliberate trade." On a 120 × 80 m shed, `shadowHalf ≈ 90 m` → a 180 m box over 4096 texels = **4.4 cm/texel**, so a 35 mm module frame is under one texel and module-level shadows dissolve exactly where row shading is reviewed. Fix with three-csm or a two-tier near/far rig.
File: `three/Scene3D.tsx:2701-2721, 2838-2839` · **Effort M-L** · **Payoff medium-high (C&I)**
Why it is next: C&I is half the declared scope and this is where the picture fails there. Worth telling the owner: ResLink hard-codes `INDIA_MIN_SUN_ALTITUDE_DEG: 35`, so both products go blind at the same sun angle — **but theirs is honest about it and ours keeps casting analytically**, so during the "play the day" animation an EPC uses to check morning and evening shading, our picture and our number silently disagree.

**18. Adaptive DPR and an on-demand frameloop**
What it does: `three/Scene3D.tsx:1402` runs `frameloop={visible ? 'always' : 'never'}` with `dpr={[1, 1.5]}` fixed (`:1405`) and `preserveDrawingBuffer: true` always on (`:1406`). Demand mode is **half-built**: `invalidate()` is already called correctly in `three/RealSurround.tsx:361, 484, 502` and `three/SurroundRelief.tsx:157, 173` and is currently a no-op. Add `<AdaptiveDpr pixelated />` and `<PerformanceMonitor>`, move to `frameloop='demand'`, and drop `preserveDrawingBuffer` except during capture (which #8 makes offscreen anyway).
File: `three/Scene3D.tsx:1400-1412` · **Effort M** · **Payoff medium (thermal)**
Why it is next: blocked by two `useFrame` loops that render every frame regardless — the spinning vent (`three/ObstructionMesh.tsx:698`) and the tree sway (`:516-521`). Do #9 and #16 first or this buys nothing.

**19. Focal-length control and true orthographic elevations**
What it does: "Front", "Back", "Left" and "Right" are sold as elevations and are none of those things. `three/Scene3D.tsx:464-471` — the four `VIEW_DIRS` carry a `0.3` Y component, so the camera looks **down at ~17°** through a `CAMERA_FOV = 40` perspective (`:454`, `presetPose` at `:444-455`). Print one and no dimension on it is measurable. There is no orthographic mode and no focal-length control anywhere. Switch the four elevation presets to an `OrthographicCamera`, and expose a focal length in mm defaulting long (~85 mm eq.) for the hero and iso shots.
File: `three/Scene3D.tsx:444-471, 1407` · **Effort M** · **Payoff medium**
Why it is next: two payoffs from one change — the elevations become drawings an engineer can hand over, and a long lens is what makes a render stop looking like a video game. ResLink does not ship this either (their shared viewer is 3D ↔ top only), but PVcase and SolarEdge do, so this is us doing a market-standard thing worse than not doing it.

**20. Camera and view persistence, plus named saved views**
What it does: nothing is remembered. `three/Scene3D.tsx:444-455` recomputes every preset from the bounding sphere on **every mount**, so: frame a roof carefully → go back to 2D to move one obstruction → reopen 3D → you are at the default iso again, and you re-orbit by hand. Every time. Persist the last camera pose per project and add named saved views ("the angle I show customers").
File: `three/Scene3D.tsx:444-455, 756, 830` · **Effort M** · **Payoff medium**
Why it is next: **store the pose outside the fingerprint** — `lib/structure-view.ts:8-10` already states the rule that view state must never key `designFp`/`layoutFp`. ResLink has no persistence either, but they ship `Share 3D view` (copy link), so their captured angle is at least reproducible.

**21. Satellite-overlay toggle and a global mounting-frame toggle**
What it does: two booleans, both currently missing. (a) The aerial photo carries the site's **own** sun, its own shadows and often its own existing panels; an engineer checking our shadow simulation must be able to take it off without losing the sky and the ground. Today the only way is switching to Mesh view (`three/Scene3D.tsx:1181-1187`), which swaps the sky, the ground and the lighting rig. (b) To see the steel under an array you must select one table and enter structure edit — there is no "show the frame under the whole array", though `lib/structure-view.ts:60-80 partitionPanels` already handles ghosting everything.
File: `three/Scene3D.tsx:1141-1187, 3144-3150, 3768-3796` · **Effort S** · **Payoff medium**
Why it is next: ResLink ships both, plus three more, in one panel — `Hide/Show satellite overlay` · `Hide/Show external buildings` · `Hide/Show annotations` · `Hide/Show sun path` · `Show/Hide mounting frame` (inventory §5). Ours is a billed engineering model behind a selection; theirs is a decoration behind a switch, and the switch is what the user sees.

**22. WebGL context-loss recovery and scene telemetry**
What it does: `grep -rn 'webglcontextlost|contextrestored' src/` returns **zero**; `grep -rn 'drawCalls|renderer.info|gl.info|WEBGL_debug_renderer' src/` returns **zero**. Today an Android context loss turns the 3D view permanently black with no message, and the only escape is a reload that re-downloads the GLBs. ResLink handles `webglcontextlost` / `webglcontextrestored` and shows `"Recovering 3D view..."`, and samples GPU renderer/vendor string, dpr, fps, worst frame gap, draw calls, triangles and texture count every 2 s (inventory §0, line 50).
File: `three/Scene3D.tsx:1400-1412` · **Effort S** · **Payoff high (not visual)**
Why it is next: **every quality decision above is currently a guess.** ResLink can answer "what does a Redmi Note render at" from data; we cannot. Ship the same four counters and the rest of this list becomes measurable instead of argued — and context loss is a routine event in exactly the ~100 MB state item #11 describes.

**23. Instance the rooftop furniture**
What it does: a 30 m safety rail renders as 2 bars + 21 posts = **23 separate meshes**, each with its own inline geometry and inline material (`three/Scene3D.tsx:3367-3396`, `posts = Math.max(2, Math.round(len / 1.5) + 1)`). A C&I roof with 10 rails and 20 walkway runs is ~250 extra draw calls and ~250 material objects for decoration alone. `Scene3D.tsx` carries 27 inline material tags and 24 inline geometry tags; `ObstructionMesh.tsx` another 18. Hoist three module-level shared materials and batch the posts into one InstancedMesh per rail.
File: `three/Scene3D.tsx:3353-3364, 3367-3396` · **Effort S** · **Payoff low visual / medium perf**
Why it is next: independent of the 169 MB — item #11 does not touch it — and it lands on the C&I roofs that are half the declared scope.
**Rails done 2026-09-09** (`three/RailsInstanced.tsx`, two instanced meshes for the whole project, gate test against the engine's casters). Walkways still one mesh each — open.

**24. Ground normal map, and one photo-albedo material factory**
What it does: the four ground planes are flat photographs at `roughness: 1` with no normal map (`three/Scene3D.tsx:2874-2905`, `three/SurroundRelief.tsx:56-73`), so the site sits on a mirror-flat plate out to the horizon. Behind it, **five separate magic brightness constants** govern photo exposure — `0.46` (`three/Scene3D.tsx:3792`), `0.66/0.64/0.60` walls (`:3793`), `WALL_SHADE = 0.55` (`three/SurroundRelief.tsx:54`), plus `PHOTO_TINT` — implemented by three bespoke `onBeforeCompile` patches doing the same thing three ways. Replace with one factory, one documented exposure constant and a triplanar helper; soften the DSM relief silhouettes.
File: `three/Scene3D.tsx:2874-2905, 3768-3796`, `three/SurroundRelief.tsx:56-125` · **Effort M** · **Payoff low-medium**
Why it is next: mostly a maintenance win, but it is the prerequisite for #12 not adding a sixth magic number.

**25. Sky variability, clouds, and DOF on hero renders only**
What it does: drei `<Cloud>` and turbidity varying by season on the existing `<Sky>` (`three/Scene3D.tsx:2801-2811`), plus a depth-of-field pass enabled only inside the offscreen capture from #8.
File: `three/Scene3D.tsx:2804-2811`, `three/ScenePost.tsx` · **Effort M / S** · **Payoff low**
Why it is next: last. It is polish on top of a pipeline that must first be correct. DOF on the live canvas costs frames on the phone for nothing; in the hero render it is free.
