### The three that cost nothing and show the most

| # | Do this | The whole change | File | Time |
|---|---------|------------------|------|------|
| 1 | **Sky-as-IBL, then glass and frame back to `envMapIntensity: 1.0`** | Move `<Sky>` + sun + ground inside `<Environment>`, raise `resolution` 128 → 256, delete the three Lightformer rects, revert the `0.2` workaround | `three/Scene3D.tsx:2746-2770` · `three/textures.ts:200, 214-224` | **~2 h** |
| 2 | **`<SoftShadows/>` plus `normalBias` 0.03 → 0.007** | One drei component inside the Canvas, two numbers changed in two places, re-tuned together | `three/Scene3D.tsx:1401, 2794-2795, 2845-2846` | **~2 h** |
| 3 | **Offscreen hero render at 2500 px** | Swap `toDataURL` on the live canvas for a `WebGLRenderTarget` pass with the shadow map raised, JPEG 0.95 | `three/Scene3D.tsx:1125-1129` | **~2 h** |

Six hours. **#1 and #2 fix what the frame looks like; #3 stops us throwing that away at 585 px in the one image the customer actually sees.** Add bloom (`three/ScenePost.tsx:40-46`, ~30 minutes) if the day has an hour left in it.


---

## The plan — what to build, in order

Ranked by what it costs, not by which section of this report it came from. Effort: **S** under a
day · **M** a few days · **L** a week or more.

### Week 1 — stop the bleeding

Every item here is a wrong number on a document that leaves the building, a design that can be
lost, or a control the phone cannot reach. Almost all are one to a few lines. If the week runs
short, 1–14 are the ones that must ship.

1. **Add `pricing.discount` to the design fingerprint** — `lib/fingerprints.ts:183` serialises
   `marginPct` only, so a negotiated discount never reaches the price; reproduced live on the
   8-panel fixture as **₹27,688 over-quoted**. **S**
2. **Derive the AC cable instead of hardcoding it** — `lib/sld.ts:52`
   (`inv.phases === 3 ? 10 : 6`) should call `sizeAcCable` like the BOM does; wrong on **24 of 24
   three-phase** catalogue combinations, printed on the DISCOM sheet (`Step8Sld.tsx:496,502`) and
   the customer spec table (`ProposalView.tsx:499`). **S**
3. **Wire `review.issuable` to the Generate button** — `lib/review.ts:161` is consumed only by
   tests while `screens/Step7Proposal.tsx:366-376` has no `disabled`, so **"Not ready to issue"
   prints above an enabled button** that issues at the default 12% margin. **S**
4. **Close the side door to the commercial documents** — the wizard gate is real but
   `router.ts:49-51` exposes `/proposal` and `/bom` ungated and `screens/Dashboard.tsx:278`
   navigates straight there, so **the gate we advertise is bypassed from the project list**. **S**
5. **Score the budgeted fill instead of truncating it** — `lib/layout.ts:398` returns the first N
   panels in row-major order and `lib/auto-design.ts:182` passes the budget, so a target-kWp design
   **keeps the worst panels**: 58.7% measured access where the best eleven measured 100%. **M**
6. **Pass the surround grid into both shading calls** — `lib/solar-heatmap.ts:237` and
   `lib/auto-design.ts:90` call the engine with no options, so the **heatmap and the roof ranking
   ignore the neighbours** the rest of the app shades with. **S**
7. **Count parallel strings per MPPT in the validator** — `lib/stringing.ts:184-191` ignores
   `parallelPerMppt`, so a false `mppt_overflow` **permanently blocks the wizard on ordinary
   15–25 kW C&I designs** and the SLD becomes unreachable. **S**
8. **Give the 2D canvas touch gestures** — `components/SatCanvas.tsx:145-152,180-196,233`
   (`touchAction:'none'`, one-pointer `dragRef`, wheel-only zoom): **no pinch, no one-finger pan**
   on the surface the whole mobile pitch depends on. **M**
9. **Make the canvas cover the site** — `SatCanvas.tsx:104,236` + `lib/maps.ts:9,34` cap the
   imagery at ~90 m at `scale=1`, so **a C&I shed or a 1 MW field cannot be traced at all**; request
   `scale=2` and a zoom that covers the bounding box. **M**
10. **Get Next on screen** — `screens/Wizard.tsx:287-296` is a ten-control non-wrapping flex row;
    measured live at 390 px, **Next renders 240 px off the right edge on all ten steps**. Reduce to
    Back · title · Next. **S**
11. **Un-bury Delete Roof** — `screens/Step2Roof.tsx:1624-1633` and `:1700-1706` overlap by 27 px at
    the same `z-index: 30`, so **"Delete Roof 1" (25×15 px) is physically unreachable**. **S**
12. **Stop the roof from eating the pan gesture** — `screens/Step2Roof.tsx:2834-2842` captures the
    pointer on the selected roof body and `:790` auto-selects after a trace, so **a one-finger drag
    moves the building instead of the map**. **S**
13. **Let a tap start a manual string** — the guard at `screens/Step6Editor.tsx:805-809` refuses any
    module already in a string, i.e. **every module on every auto-strung design**, which is all of
    them; the 3D path has no such guard. **S**
14. **Ungate box-select from the Shift key** — `screens/Step6Editor.tsx:815,1359`,
    `three/MarqueeSelect.tsx`, `three/Scene3D.tsx:526`: on a phone **exactly one module can be
    selected in 3D**. **S**
15. **Put obstructions into the shadow-camera bounds** — `three/scene-bounds.ts:22-56` reads roofs
    and panels only, so a tall neighbour **casts no shadow in the picture the proposal ships while
    the engine still charges for it**. **S**
16. **Take the QR code and share link off the customer cover page** — `screens/ShareViewer.tsx:9,27`
    resolves from the *viewing* device's localStorage, so **the scannable code on the cover works
    for nobody**; hide it until a server mints the link (item 78). **S**
17. **Stop the share page leaking the financing model** — `components/EnergyReportSheet.tsx:23-26`
    calls `computeEnergyReport`/`computeFinancials` directly and gates on `isShadingFresh` alone, so
    **a prospect sees net cost, margin-derived savings, payback, EMI and PPA rate**; route through
    `deriveEnergy`/`deriveFinance` and `designFreshness(p).all`. **S**
18. **Fix the three headline numbers on the customer document** — payback prints the sentinel
    **"25 yrs"** as a result, the headline money is computed off the **rounded MWh**, and 25-yr
    savings is **rounded to whole lakhs** (`screens/ProposalView.tsx:280-290,346`). **S**
19. **Correct the financing base** — the cash column tells a buyer their day-one outlay is the
    **subsidy-net** figure and the loan down-payment and principal are computed on the same wrong
    base (`lib/financing.ts`, `ProposalView.tsx:358-399`); the PPA "over the term" figure ignores
    escalation and degradation, and escalation itself is a magic number in a function body. **S**
20. **Deduct or delete "Shading — electrical (strings)"** — it is printed in the loss waterfall on
    the internal report and the customer proposal and **never subtracted from the generation figure
    above it** (`lib/energy/report.ts`). **S**
21. **Refuse the over-count inverter placement** — `lib/ops/electrical-ops.ts:145-149` silently
    drops the oldest placement and shifts every index, **re-pointing existing strings at a different
    physical box** on the SLD, the DXF and the installation sheet. **S**
22. **Print the BOM the customer is charged for** — the proposal table renders excluded and
    zero-quantity lines and shows **design quantity while billing order quantity**
    (`lib/bom/registry.ts:140-173`, `ProposalView.tsx`). **S**
23. **Three BOM lines that are simply wrong money** — rail metres ignore module orientation so a
    landscape array is quoted **half the rail it needs**; end clamps are a hardcoded **8** for any
    number of loose panels; ground pedestals are billed with **rooftop wording at the excavation
    rate** (`lib/bom/emitters/mechanical.ts`, `emitters/civil.ts`). **S**
24. **Stop lying about saving** — `store/store.tsx:351-359,497` computes `persistStatus` and
    `retryPersist` and **nothing renders either**, while `screens/Wizard.tsx:363-372` toasts
    "Project saved" unconditionally; a quota failure loses a rooftop survey behind a green toast.
    **S**
25. **Add error boundaries** — `grep ErrorBoundary src` returns **zero**, so one throw on any step
    blanks the whole product; one boundary per wizard step with a retry. **S**
26. **Stop iOS zooming on every field** — inputs are under 16 px (`theme.css:72-75`), no
    `scrollIntoView` on focus, and `src/app/layout.tsx` never emits `viewport-fit=cover`, so **all
    four safe-area utilities are dead** and fixed chrome sits under the home indicator. **S**
27. **Survive a lost GL context** — no `webglcontextlost` handler anywhere against a
    `frameloop='always'` scene holding ~169 MB of GLBs; add the handler and a **plain-models
    toggle** (`three/ObstructionMesh.tsx:60-67`, `three/Scene3D.tsx:2387`). **S**
28. **One solar-access scale** — access can never read below **35%** while every legend claims
    0–100%, and `lib/shade-ramp.ts` vs `lib/solar-heatmap.ts:66-74` paint the worst cell amber and
    label it "Poor". **S**
29. **Stop the SLD contradicting the BOM on earth pits** — `screens/Step8Sld.tsx:565` hardcodes
    "3 pits" against the two the BOM derives, and Step 9's checklist says three again. **S**
30. **Model module maximum system voltage** — a 1000 V-class module strung to **1035 V cold Voc
    prints a green compliance box** on the DISCOM sheet (`lib/electrical/window.ts`,
    `data/panels.ts`). **S**
31. **Apply India's 2× sanctioned-load rule where capacity is chosen** — we collect
    `sanctionedLoadKw` at `Step1Setup.tsx:211-219` and warn at **1×, after auto-design**; the rule
    belongs on Step 4 at 2×. **S**
32. **Make the 3D agree with the survey** — a rectangular tank or dish is drawn as a **square
    footprint using the smaller dimension**, the GLB turbine vent spins its whole body at ~200°/s,
    a cleared size field writes 0 and silently stops the shading, and both tilted legs sit under the
    same edge (`three/ObstructionMesh.tsx`, `screens/Step3Obstructions.tsx:564-624`). **S**
33. **Give the proposal an identity** — no quote number, no issue date, no validity, no revision;
    page numbers are hand-typed literals footed **1, 2, 2, 3, 4, 4**; and every file saves as
    **"Solar App.pdf"** with our URL across each sheet. **S**

### Weeks 2–6 — reach parity

Ordered by payoff over effort. Everything here is something ResLink ships and we do not.

34. **Split the on-object card, then hang it off a 2D pick** — ~~mount `three/EntityLabel.tsx`~~
    **CORRECTED:** the card cannot be mounted in 2D (drei `<Html>` is r3f-only). Lift its body into
    `components/EntityCard.tsx`, keep `three/EntityLabel.tsx` as the `<Html>` wrapper so all ten 3D
    mounts compile untouched, and add `components/CanvasCard.tsx` as the SVG `<foreignObject>`
    anchor. Fix the sub-44 px buttons and 11.5 px labels while lifting. Then a long-press trigger
    (new code, §7.2) and a non-modal variant — our Step 3 `Sheet` dims the whole roof and covers
    76% of the viewport. **M · high**
35. **Make a table a first-class object in 2D** — a tap resolves to the module, not the segment, so
    nothing table-scoped can be reached by thumb. **Ships with the hit-test fix** (`lib/plan-pick.ts`)
    and closes the footgun where a one-module tap opens a sheet whose Delete kills all 40.
    **M · high**
36. **Table expand: panels-per-row** — ~~add a direction picker~~ **CORRECTED:** the direction
    picker already exists (axis pills, side pills, count stepper in the grow popover). What is
    missing is a span/offset on `growCandidates`; `lib/segment-ops.ts:262-272` always emits a full
    lattice row. Plus the three-line fix in `onTableDragEnd`, which commits without re-selecting, so
    an outward edge-drag unmounts all four handles mid-gesture. **M · high**
37. **Delete rows / columns with the rows marked and the count previewed** — the owner's named ask.
    **CORRECTED:** the mechanism already works (marquee a row → Delete → `cascadeDeletePanels` →
    `reindexSegment`); this item buys the **affordance and the number**, not the engine. Tappable
    bands on the lattice, ≥44 px tap zones, and `summarizeImpact` for the count. **M · high**
38. **Box and lasso selection, with a Tables/Panels mode and add/remove regions** — **CORRECTED:**
    Week 1 already added touch box-select in both views, so do not re-add a Shift escape; extend
    `addSelect` / `boxSelect` into a three-state mode. What is real: rectangle-only everywhere, no
    Tables target, no subtract, and 3D's hardcoded `additive: true` silently deselecting a whole
    block on a second box. **M · high**
39. **Multi-table settings** — `lib/segment-ops.ts:349-362` returns `{kind:'other'}` as soon as two
    panels differ, so **"set these twelve tables to 12°" is impossible**, not slow; make the sheet
    take `segmentIds: string[]` and render "–" for mixed. **L · high**
40. **The switches whose engine already exists** — portrait/landscape (five hardcoded `'portrait'`
    literals; `three/PanelsInstanced.tsx` already renders a landscape mesh nothing can produce),
    grouped/separate, and a live GCR/row-pitch slider over `segmentRespace`. **CORRECTED:** row gap
    and column gap are **one scalar**, not two — independent gaps need a new optional field on
    `ArraySegment`, kept lazy so `lib/fingerprints.ts` does not re-key every existing project. Also
    fix `lib/bom/emitters/mechanical.ts`, which computes rail metres from `spec.lengthMm` regardless
    of orientation: shipping landscape without it **under-bills half the rail on a customer
    document**. **S each · high**
41. **Fill a roof from inside the editor** — the auto-fill dialog is **dead code** (`confirmPlace`
    is never set true). **CORRECTED:** do not resurrect it — the op behind it (`layoutAutoDesign`)
    passes `avoidPanels: []` and **replaces the whole project**, destroying every hand-placed
    module. Build on the Table tool's existing additive, collision-aware per-area fill instead, and
    add a per-roof clear so "Clear all panels" stops wiping every roof and every string. **M · high**
42. **The overlap resolver** — **CORRECTED:** keepouts are already caught by a `panel_in_keepout`
    hard error in `lib/drc.ts`; **walkways and rails** are the silent ones (`lib/ops/site-ops.ts`
    appends and never touches `panels`), and the buried modules keep counting toward kWp, energy,
    the BOM and the quote — which also double-bills the walkway area. Ship the walkway resolver;
    **rails are a separate item**, because `lib/scene-model.ts` has no rail caster at all.
    **M · high**
43. **Remove-shaded-panels as a threshold slider with a live count and kW cost** — we stop at a
    fixed 70% warning (`lib/drc.ts:25`) and hand the user a marquee. **CORRECTED:** unscored panels
    are not `undefined` — every creation site stamps an optimistic **1.0**, so a just-placed module
    reads as fully sunlit. Count them honestly and disable removal while shading is stale. Three
    shade thresholds already coexist (drc's 0.7, and 0.95/0.85 hardcoded twice more in the fills) —
    **do not add a fourth.** **M · high**
44. **A real PDF file, named after the project** — `window.print()` at `ProposalView.tsx:144` is the
    whole export; the PDF is the prerequisite for everything below it. **M · high**
45. **WhatsApp delivery from inside the product** — zero `wa.me` hits in the tree, while
    `customerPhone` is already captured; this is how an Indian EPC closes. **S · high**
46. **Excel BOM under a project header block** — `lib/bom.ts:159-244` writes 17 columns of CSV with
    **no header block at all**, so nothing in the file names the job. **M · high**
47. **The missing Layout Drawings outputs** — an **Earthing & LA sheet** (nothing emits
    `earth_conductor`, which is declared and DXF-mapped), a **DC String Route sheet that draws the
    routed cable** rather than the series order, five sheets that stop being numbered **"OF 2"** on
    three contradictory paper sizes, a structure detail drawn from the real design instead of a
    hardcoded one, one title block instead of two, and a legend that stops advertising symbols the
    sheet never draws. **L · high**
48. **Export the SLD as DXF and PDF, and All Sheets as one PDF** — today the DXF chip exports the
    array layout, not the sheet you are looking at (`Step8Sld.tsx:169-194`). **M · high**
49. **The SLD parameters an Indian sheet needs** — editable earth pits, **SPD type**, LA flag, free
    notes, a grid/standards block (voltage, frequency, wiring and earthing codes), an `mfm` symbol,
    and inline justification beside each override (Isc beside cable, minimum beside fuse, full-load
    amps beside the breaker). **S–M · high**
50. **1φ/3φ templates and a phase filter on the inverter picker** — a three-phase inverter can be
    chosen on a single-phase service and **the SLD prints "3φ" and "(1φ)" on the same sheet**.
    **S · high**
51. **Hand-stringing ergonomics** — undo-last-panel, clear-all, un-tap in 2D, live
    unassigned/string counts, `stringRemove` as a 2D action, keep the tapped string when a save is
    refused, and surface the pruning message the sync currently discards
    (`store/useElectricalSync.ts:29-30`). **S · high**
52. **Obstruction bulk tools** — sticky placement mode (one line:
    `Step3Obstructions.tsx:171`), box + lasso select, **array (rows × columns)**, **mirror across an
    edge**, align/space, and bulk move/copy/delete with an undo toast; eight vents on a purlin line
    is ~64 taps today. **M · high**
53. **Six flat obstruction types plus skylight** — AC unit, keep-out zone, light pole, transformer
    pad, fence, lightning arrester against their 17 types, and skylight with its four sub-shapes;
    the palette edit is `OBSTRUCTION_PRESETS` at `lib/roof-factory.ts:196-211`. **S–M · high**
54. **The missing meshes** — of 11 `ObstructionType` values **6 have a GLB**, `windmill`'s folder
    holds only `.gitkeep`, and `elevated`/`ladder`/`other` fall back to a grey box
    (`three/ObstructionMesh.tsx:255-270`); ship the props and decimate the existing ones (turbine
    vent alone is **39.2 MB / 1.13 M triangles**). **M · high**
55. **Show the design on the auto-placement step** — `Step5AutoDesign.tsx:83-167` is prose and two
    buttons with no canvas; add per-surface result cards with a graded access band, a Target-kWp
    Met/Exceeded chip, monthly bars, roof-priority reorder, orientation and row-spacing controls,
    and a progress state (the run is synchronous and can freeze a mid-range Android for 1–3 s).
    **M · high**
56. **Inverter placement inside the flow** — it lives four taps into a radial menu on the *next*
    step, the 3D tool disarms after one placement, and until an inverter exists there are **no
    routed home runs and the DC cable line falls back to an assumption**. **M · high**
57. **Reorder the wizard so pricing precedes the proposal** — `Wizard.tsx:22-33`; today the BOM is
    priced two steps after a GST-inclusive quote can leave. **M · high**
58. **A tappable step strip with done / skipped / blocked state** — ten steps, no way to jump, and
    nothing marks steps 3, 5, 7, 8 and 9 as the optional steps they already are. **S · high**
59. **Ground mount, made usable** — a Ground draw tool that does not start as a roof (the Step 1
    card is a dead `div`), zones over `project.segments`, earth pits that scale with the plant
    (`data/rules/india.ts:376-377` is a flat 2), an **8 m LA mast** instead of a 2 m finial sold as
    a 60 m radius, the fence drawn where it is billed, table boundaries, and central inverters in
    the catalogue (1 MW is **34 boxes placed by hand** today). **M–L · high**
60. **Carport as a roof type** — `RoofType` has four members and no carport; the structure is the
    product and the customer buys the parking count. **L · high**
61. **The datasheet-PDF importer and manual spec entry** — `Step4Components.tsx:792-806` is a
    **non-interactive `div`** reading "mocked in POC"; with 15 panels and 12 inverters in the
    catalogue an EPC whose SKU is missing **cannot design at all**. **L · high**
62. **A multi-inverter design** — `Components` holds one `inverter` plus a count, so **1×20 kW +
    1×10 kW is unrepresentable** on a routine 60–80 kW C&I roof. **L · high**
63. **A per-loss editor on the energy report** — every loss is a module constant; an EPC who knows
    his soiling is 7% cannot say so, and his lender will ask. **M · high**
64. **Move the roof heatmap into the worker with a progress model** — ~540,000 raycasts run on the
    main thread and are thrown away when a module moves a centimetre. **M · high**
65. **Sun on the step where the shadow is caused** — Step 3 opens in `mesh` mode
    (`Step3Obstructions.tsx:295`, one word) which strips the sun, the surround and the gizmo; add a
    2D shadow footprint and month-by-month access with an **annual** figure. **S–M · high**
66. **Roof-step parity** — ridge height as an input, per-edge lock plus a visible snap-to-90,
    type-a-length-then-tap placement, parapet validation and **per-edge parapet height**, a live
    area readout while tracing, and a unit-aware edge editor (**an imperial user types feet and gets
    metres** today). **M · high**
67. **Reference-drawing underlay** — import a DXF or PNG/JPG site plan and scale it by two tapped
    points; the only feature ResLink charges Pro for, and the only answer on a tier-2 site where the
    imagery is years stale. **L · medium**
68. **Roof openings** — `Roof.polygon` is a single ring, so a courtyard or stairwell void can only
    be faked as an obstruction with the wrong keepout. **L · medium**
69. **In-product support and onboarding** — per-step Help that reaches a human, WhatsApp pre-filled
    with step and project id, a guided tour with "don't show again", and a real tutorial video (Step
    1 currently ships **ResLink's own string on a gradient rectangle**). **S · high**
70. **Offline operation with a visible save/sync state** — no service worker, no manifest, no
    `navigator.onLine`; we are already local-first and tell the user nothing. **M · high**
71. **Loading, empty, error and offline states across all 18 screens** — N10 is unmet everywhere,
    and `StudioClientLayout.tsx:143` renders a blank first paint on every route. **M · high**
72. **Hindi** — no i18n framework, no Devanagari face, against their four shipped languages; do it
    **after** the mobile layout, not before. **L · high**
73. **The BOM screen on a phone** — six 1060 px tables, 234 controls and 197 live inputs at 390 px;
    DESIGN-SYSTEM §6.3 already prescribes the card list. Include the **derivation button that has no
    `onClick`** (`Step9Bom/BomRow.tsx:262-268`) — the provenance story is currently unopenable on
    touch. **M · high**
74. **The proposal on a phone** — fixed three-column grids, a five-column payment table and 15
    sub-12 px sizes on the one screen the customer definitely opens on a phone. **M · high**
75. **Project list and pipeline** — sort by kWp, four statuses instead of two, employee filter,
    ground-mount badge, Export 3D from the row menu, and remembered banner dismissals. **M ·
    medium**
76. **Step 0 gaps** — **use my current location** and typed lat/long (zero `geolocation` hits in the
    repo), a fullscreen satellite preview, and the **monthly GHI + temperature table** we already
    fetch and store (`types.ts:77`, `lib/pvgis.ts:102,127`) and render nowhere. **S · medium**
77. **Sixteen missing BOM lines** — cleaning kit, inverter mounting kit and shed, DC/AC glands and
    lugs, itemised isolators/MCBs/RCCB and a standalone SPD, module earthing clips, fire kit,
    monitoring (logger/SIM/pyranometer — **a C&I bid without it is non-responsive**), spares and
    ferrules, O&M/AMC, waterproofing and parapet sealing, splice plates and end caps, fasteners by
    joint size, plus `supplier` and `notes` fields on every line. **S each · high**
78. **A server-minted, revocable, expiring share link** — needs the backend the POC does not have;
    it is the prerequisite for item 16 becoming a feature rather than a deletion. **L · high**
79. **The HT side of the SLD** — transformer kVA, ratio, VCB, HT cable, CT/PT ratios; **zero hits**
    across the feature, and every Indian plant above ~100 kW is rejected without them. **L · high**
80. **Camera and view persistence** — `three/Scene3D.tsx:444-455` recomputes every preset from the
    bounding sphere on each mount, so **you never return to the angle you left**; add saved named
    views and make the four "elevations" actually orthographic. **M · medium**
81. **GLB export with per-category toggles** — `GLTFExporter` ships in the `three` we already
    depend on; it is what a C&I client's architect asks for. **M · medium**
82. **Capture All, Retake and pre-capture toggles on Step 7** — four sequential full-screen 3D
    mounts switched by 22 px dots, with **no winter shadow frame in the set** and captions that
    describe a time the scene did not render. **S · high**
83. **Company logo as a company-level asset** — `logoDataUrl` is per project, so the EPC re-uploads
    on every job and the customer PDF is otherwise branded with **our** product name. **S · high**

### Beyond — overtake

Where our physics, provenance and structure engine let us pass them rather than catch up.

84. **Sell the per-caster sentence** — `lib/energy/report.ts:341-361` can already say *"that neem
    tree costs you 1,240 kWh a year"*; put it on the object, under an animated shadow, and the
    sun-path stops being an engineer's chart and becomes the closing tool ResLink only claims to
    have. **M · high**
85. **Make provenance the product, and make it true** — the four-tier system on every BOM line is
    real and they have nothing like it, but `lib/roof-factory.ts:131-153` never sets `heightSource`,
    `lib/roof-map-fit.ts:100-102` stamps a median as `measured`, and
    `lib/roof-ai/gemini-client.ts:82-85` puts a confidence on a preset height; fix the three
    mis-stamps, then print a **provenance ledger** on the quote. **M · high**
86. **Turn the structure engine into fabricator deliverables** — steel by profile, a cut list,
    splice and joiner counts, per-joint fastener sizes, and a monorail work order (`lib/installation.ts`
    filters rooftop tables only, so **the metal-shed path emits no rails and no standoffs** while the
    BOM bills both). **M · high**
87. **Ship the comparison matrix as a customer page** — `lib/comparison.ts` runs six candidates
    through fill → string → 8760-hour energy → BOM → finance; nobody else in this market puts three
    priced options in front of a buyer. **M · high**
88. **Make the 8760-hour engine bankable** — one engine instead of two silently chosen by whether a
    fetch succeeded (`store/useTmySync.ts:15-16` pins a whole session to the weaker one with **no
    retry and no marker on the document**), then P50/P90 and a signed loss table. **M · high**
89. **Hyperrealism, in this order** — PCSS/varied shadow blur and a 0.005 normal bias, a sun colour
    and altitude ramp with real twilight, an IBL rendered from our own sky, bloom, per-module soiling
    and roughness jitter, cascaded shadow maps for C&I sheds, tree variety with LOD, the 226 lines of
    roof-covering materials made reachable again, and ground normal maps. **S–L · high perceptual,
    zero numerical** — it changes no number and blocks no user, which is why it is here and not in
    Week 1.
90. **Measure the phone instead of guessing** — a 30-frame GPU probe replacing the User-Agent sniff
    at `three/ScenePost.tsx:20-25`, plus scene telemetry; every quality decision above is currently
    unmeasured on the device class we sell to. **M · medium**
91. **Own the field survey** — two-tap ladder capture, measure-to-edge with a typeable distance,
    parent-surface override, and per-object view toggles; our anchor resolver and measure tool are
    already most of the way there. **M · medium**
92. **The AI planner** — the ops kernel with typed refusals and previews (`lib/ops/`) is the only
    safe substrate for it in this market; `opById`/`listOps` are already parked for exactly this.
    **L · medium**

### What NOT to do yet

- **Hyperrealism before Week 1.** It changes no number and blocks no user. Item 89, not item 1.
- **Terrain and land contours.** `lib/roof-factory.ts:162` documents the flat-terrain limit
  honestly; a wrong slope model is worse than a stated one. **L**, defer.
- **Plan entitlements and read-only tiers.** There is no backend, no checkout and no pricing
  decision — and pricing is owner-confirmed only. Stop advertising the PRO tier and the ground-mount
  card that do nothing (`Step1Setup.tsx:153-177`) rather than building the tier.
- **Telemetry replay routes and session recording.** Take the cheap performance probe (item 90)
  first; the replay product is theirs to fund, not ours.
- **Carport before the canvas can hold a site.** Item 60 is unusable until item 9 lands.
- **Hindi before the mobile layout.** Translating 8.5 px text that renders 255 px off-screen buys
  nothing.
- **The HT SLD before the LT sheet is right.** Item 79 is worthless while item 2 prints 10 mm² on a
  1 MW plant.
- **A new on-canvas card.** `three/EntityLabel.tsx` already is one — mount it, do not rebuild it.
- **Their catalogue, copied.** Seed from manufacturer datasheets and the importer (item 61); a
  scraped competitor database is not a defensible asset.
- **Any `.dependency-cruiser.cjs` rule downgraded to land an import.** Move the code instead —
  `npm run cycles` is a hard gate, and four gates stay green before every commit.


---

## What ResLink does NOT have

Two tables. The first is the list their **own bundle proves** — the inventory reached these by
absence of any string in a 4.71 MB public bundle, so they are as hard as competitive evidence
gets. The second is everything else the thirteen diffs turned up (142 `weHaveTheyDont` entries,
de-duplicated).

`[not built]` means we do not have it either — it is an opening, not an asset. Effort is
S (under a day) / M (a few days) / L (a week or more).

### A. The absences their own bundle proves

Source for every "they lack" cell: `reslink-feature-inventory.md` §"Things the bundle proves
they DON'T have (absence of any string)", plus §0 for the commercial rows.

| What they lack | What we have or could have (file:line) | Why it wins a deal in India |
|---|---|---|
| **Battery / storage — nothing, anywhere** | **Shipped.** Bank selection with DC-hybrid vs AC-coupled at `screens/Step4Components.tsx:299-320` + `screens/Step4Battery.tsx` + `data/batteries.ts`; `types.ts:435-440`; a whole BOM category — bank, DC leads routed off the placed cabinets, isolator+fuse per cabinet, AC-coupled interface — at `lib/bom/emitters/battery.ts:12-72`; cabinets are placeable objects on the plan (`screens/Step6Editor.tsx:768-779`) | Every C&I enquiry in a load-shedding state and every premium villa now opens with "and backup?". A tool that cannot quote a battery cannot quote the job. ResLink's answer is a spreadsheet outside their product |
| **EV charger and heat pump** | `[not built]` — grep for `ev.?charger\|heat.?pump\|evse` across `src/features/solar-studio` returns **0**. Effort **M**, payoff **medium**: an EV charger is a load line and an AC breaker, not new physics | The residential upsell that closes the "why now" objection. Neither of us has it, so it is a clean land-grab |
| **Optimisers and micro-inverters — "only string vs central"** | **Optimisers shipped**, and they change the engineering, not just the BOM: `types.ts:419-424 MlpeKind`, chip at `screens/Step4Components.tsx:282-295`, the MLPE mega-group that relaxes the same-orientation/tilt/shade constraint in `lib/electrical/grouping.ts`, one `elec.optimizer` line per enabled module at `lib/bom/emitters/electrical.ts:216-229`. **Micro-inverters `[not built]`** — `MlpeKind` is `'none' \| 'optimizer'` only (`types.ts:424`). Effort **M**, payoff **medium** | A multi-face hip roof in a dense colony strands a whole face on plain string wiring. Optimisers are how that house gets sold. We can show the extra kWh; they cannot model the part |
| **Bifacial** | **Shipped, with real rear-side physics.** `types.ts:306-314 bifacialityPct` (honest documented default), populated on Waaree Bi-55-550 and Vikram HYPERSOL 555 (`data/panels.ts:12,14`); rear irradiance solved from actual mounting geometry at `lib/energy/bifacial.ts:275-338` and `lib/energy/hourly.ts:304-333`; surface-specific albedo at `lib/energy/bifacial.ts:60-72` (grass 0.20 / weathered concrete 0.25 / weathered sheet 0.30 / tile 0.15, with the reasoning that a fresh galvanised sheet reads 0.5 and quoting it would inflate every yield); PVsyst's own rear-loss defaults at `:81-83`; shown as a **gain above the losses**, not a negative loss (`components/EnergyReportSheet.tsx:148-171`) | Bifacial is now the default module on Indian ground mount. A quote that cannot show the rear gain loses to one that can — and one that inflates it loses the second job |
| **Tracker** | **Shipped.** HSAT pose as a function of time with true backtracking from GCR at `lib/energy/tracker.ts:1-60`; the same pose feeds scene, shading and transposition (`lib/panel-pose.ts:39,100-118`) so the array visibly rolls and every sun sample is cast off that moment's geometry; offered only on ground (`screens/Step6Editor.tsx:2116`) and refused on a roof with "A tracker needs open ground, not a roof" (`lib/ops/layout-ops.ts:195`); rotation-limit slider at `:2126-2145`; hardware in the BOM — torque-tube metres, a bearing per post, drive units per row (`lib/bom/emitters/mechanical.ts:548-625`) | Open-access and captive C&I ground mount is the highest-ticket work in the market. No tracker means no bid |
| **LIDAR / photogrammetry import** | **We do better than import — we fetch it.** Google's Solar DSM at 100 m radius, downsampled 0.1 m → 0.5 m, site roofs cut out, stored grade-relative (`lib/surround.ts:20-27`), turned into a BVH caster the shading engine raycasts like any other geometry (`lib/scene-model.ts:305-323`); 3D Tiles photomesh at `three/RealSurround.tsx:274-297`; DSM relief at `three/SurroundRelief.tsx:56-125`. **File import (LAS/E57/drone mesh) `[not built]`** — effort **L**, payoff **low** for now: India's 3D Tiles carry no buildings, so the DSM path is the live one anyway | The neighbour's terrace is the number-one shading argument in an Indian residential survey. We answer it from data in seconds; they hand-draw a box |
| **AI roof detection** — "every obstruction is hand-placed", no roof detection at all | **Shipped, with measured height/pitch/azimuth.** `lib/roof-ai/pipeline.ts` + `lib/roof-ai/plane-fit.ts`; the review UI at `screens/Step2Roof.tsx:1447-1533` shows accepted/total, imagery quality and date, detector confidence, per-shape accept, a ±0.5 m nudge and warnings — **nothing enters the project unreviewed**. Three taps: Halo → Detect → Add selected | A 20-roof C&I shed complex traced by hand is an hour. Detected and reviewed, it is minutes. That is the demo that ends the meeting |
| **AI obstruction detection** | **Built, currently unreachable.** `lib/ops/roof-ops.ts:32-69 obstructionsAddFromMap` builds each object through the same factory as a hand placement but with **real DSM dimensions** and `provenance: { source: 'dataLayers', confidence: min(1, cells/16) }`; classifier at `lib/roof-map-fit.ts:310-316`. **Two caveats:** the action is not wired into Step 3 today, and the classifier returns only `elevated / tank / other`. Effort **S** to wire, **M** to widen the classes; payoff **high** | Same argument as roof detection, and the shading argument on top. It is already written — it just needs a button |
| **P50 / P75 / P90** | **Shipped, bank-grade.** `lib/energy/report.ts:38-78` decomposes sigma into the site's own measured year-to-year GHI spread and the model's own uncertainty (`MODEL_SIGMA_PCT = 5`, quadrature ≈4.7), cites the number of years of record, and prints "(assumed — record not kept yet)" when the record is short (`components/EnergyReportSheet.tsx:88-96`) | No Indian bank or NBFC funds a C&I plant off a P50. This is the single line that moves us from "design tool" to "financeable document" |
| **Sub-module / cell-string shading** | `[not built]` for both — but ours is one level deeper than theirs. `lib/string-shade.ts:37-47` models the **bypass diode at module level inside the string** (`BYPASS_BELOW 0.5`) and reports linear vs electrical vs worst-module access (`:49-98`). True per-cell-string with the OpenSolar six-dot display is effort **M**, payoff **high** (`our-audit-critique.md §F`) | The EPC's own engineer asks "what does that parapet actually cost me?". Per-module bypass already answers better than ResLink; six dots per panel makes it visible |
| **Horizon profile import** | **We measure the horizon instead of importing it.** Near skyline from the DSM plus the project's own roofs and obstructions, far hills from PVGIS: `lib/sun-chart.ts:139-249 horizonProfile`, `lib/far-horizon.ts` (`/api/site/horizon`, one fetch per pin), drawn under the sun path at `three/SunChart.tsx:158-268` for **both** the array centre and its worst corner, with shade windows converted to clock time and written as English sentences (`lib/sun-chart.ts:257-269`). **Importing a surveyed horizon file `[not built]`** — effort **S**, payoff **low** | Ghats, hill towns and mill compounds. "You lose sun after 15:40 in December on the west corner" is a sentence a customer remembers |
| **IRR / payback inside the design tool** — their financials live in the separate Proposals product | **Payback shipped, IRR not.** `lib/finance.ts:45-77` gives payback against an escalating tariff, 25-year savings, subsidy, net cost and a 5-yr 9.5 % EMI; ROI % and payback per candidate at `lib/comparison.ts:58-61`; **four financing modes reconciled to one quote total** (Cash / Loan / Lease / PPA off the same `fin.systemCostInr`) at `lib/financing.ts:50-121`, printed as a HOW TO PAY table at `screens/ProposalView.tsx:358-399`. **IRR and NPV `[not built]`** — grep returns 0. Effort **S** (two functions over the existing 25-year cashflow), payoff **high** for C&I | A C&I CFO asks for IRR, not payback. It is a one-day job on a cashflow we already build — and it lands in the same screen as the design, which ResLink structurally cannot do |
| **CAD import beyond DXF-as-tracing-underlay** | `[not built]` for us too — and **worse**: we have no DXF import at all (`grep importDxf\|parseDxf` → 0); we only export (`lib/export-dxf.ts:17-63`). Effort **M**, payoff **medium** | The C&I customer emails a shed GA drawing. They can at least trace over it; today we cannot even open it. **This one is behind, not an opening** — listed here so it is not mistaken for a win |
| **Teams / roles inside the design tool** (they have an employee filter only) | `[not built]` for us — no roles, no employee filter, no multi-user anything. `lib/persistence/repository.ts` is local-first single-user. Effort **L**, payoff **high** at company scale | An EPC with six surveyors cannot run a business on one browser's local storage. Their employee filter is thin, but it exists and ours does not |
| **Self-serve checkout — every upgrade is a `wa.me` deeplink to a salesperson** (`our-audit-critique.md §F`; §0 of the inventory documents the plan gating and the pre-filled WhatsApp message carrying step, project name, project id, phone and user id) | `[not built]` — no billing of any kind in this repo (`grep billing\|checkout\|razorpay\|stripe` → 0). Effort **M**, payoff **high** | Their funnel is sales-assisted and therefore slow and expensive. A ₹2,999/month card-swipe signup takes the long tail of two-man EPCs they will never call back |
| **Any provenance or derivation on a number** — nothing in their bundle labels the trust of a figure | **The system exists and is strong on the BOM; it is not blanket.** Four tiers (measured / derived / estimated / assumed) declared at `types.ts:917-939`, rolled to a worst-tier quote badge with a named verify-on-site list at `lib/bom.ts:87-118`, rendered as a dot with a full screen-reader label at `screens/Step9Bom/BomRow.tsx:17-29`, **exported as its own CSV column** at `lib/bom.ts:212`. A human-readable `formula` is **required on every constructed line** (`lib/bom/line.ts:15-33`; non-optional at `types.ts:926`) and exported too (`lib/bom.ts:187,213`). Also: catalogue provenance printed to the user (`data/catalog.ts:21-61` → `screens/Step4Components.tsx:403-405`, "representative data — not confirmed vendor quotes"). **Caveat, per `final-critique.md §3.2`: roof provenance mis-stamps in three places** — `lib/roof-factory.ts:131-153` never sets `heightSource`, `lib/roof-map-fit.ts:100-102` stamps a median as `aerial_map`, `lib/roof-ai/gemini-client.ts:82-85` puts `confidence` on a preset height. Claim the BOM, fix the roof. Effort **S** each | "Which of these numbers did you measure and which did you assume?" is the question that kills a quote in a C&I technical review. We are the only one of the two that can answer it line by line |
| **A dismissable staleness warning that stays dismissed** — theirs remembers (`dismissedBanners`) | Ours does not: `screens/Step9Bom/StaleBanner.tsx` / `OrphanBanner.tsx` have no dismissal memory, so the same warning re-fires on every open. **Behind, not an opening.** Effort **S** | Listed for completeness — an irritation, not a differentiator |

### B. Further openings, from the thirteen dimension diffs

Every row below is a `weHaveTheyDont` entry whose "theirs" side is an absence in the inventory
rather than a weaker implementation. Grouped by where a salesperson meets it.

**Decision support and sizing**

| What they lack | What we have or could have (file:line) | Why it wins a deal in India |
|---|---|---|
| **A costed option set.** Their Step 3 sizing help is a capacity number | Six candidate panels each run through the **full** pipeline — roof fill → auto-string → 8760-hour energy → BOM → finance — reporting annual kWh, net cost after subsidy, payback, 25-yr savings, ROI %, DC/AC ratio, string feasibility **with the actual refusal message**, array weight in kg, install complexity and both warranties: `lib/comparison.ts` (shortlist :206-227, build :93-133, compare :234-330, ranking :328-340), rendered `screens/Step4Components.tsx:382-559` | The customer asks "why this panel?" once. Answering with six costed rows and a written reason ends the price shopping |
| **Sizing from the electricity bill** — they size from roof area and sanctioned load only | One tap from ₹/month + tariff + peak sun hours to a kWp suggestion: `lib/solar.ts:83-91 suggestKwpFromBill`, banner `screens/Step4Components.tsx:101-108,216-233` | The homeowner does not know their sanctioned load. They know their bill. This is the first thirty seconds of every residential call |
| **DC/AC ratio as a named check with a band** — their string is `inverter ≈ {{kw}} kW AC`, no ratio, no clipping language | Live band + diagnosis on the picker and every comparison row: `screens/Step4Components.tsx:833,901-925` (`>1.35 high (clipping risk)`, `<0.9 low (oversized)`, else `healthy (0.90–1.35)`), same band in `lib/comparison.ts:142-154,272-275`, per-inverter Pnom warnings `lib/stringing.ts:139-165` | Over-sized DC is the most common Indian design error and the most common warranty fight. Catching it on screen is a credibility moment |
| **A second opinion on roof capacity** | Google Solar's `maxPanels` shown beside our own estimate, with the wattage mismatch flagged honestly: `screens/Step4Components.tsx:201-215` | The customer has already read a "your roof fits 12 kW" number off a website. Showing both and explaining the difference disarms it |
| **A labelled catalogue** | Versioned envelope with `catalogVersion`, `effectiveFrom`, `provenance` and per-entry overrides: `data/catalog.ts:21-61` | Prices move monthly here. A quote that names its price date survives the argument |
| **A panel swap that re-lays the array** | A swap is a first-class undoable op that re-spaces every segment to the new module's lattice and re-keys the layout fingerprint so stored captures go honestly stale: `lib/ops/components-ops.ts:23-64` ("A panel swap is not a field write") | Nothing silently disagrees with the spec sheet after a last-minute module change — which is when the mistakes happen |

**Roof, survey and site**

| What they lack | What we have or could have (file:line) | Why it wins a deal in India |
|---|---|---|
| **Per-edge setbacks** — theirs is one uniform `Edge Setback (m)` | `types.ts:198 perEdgeSetbacksM`, UI `screens/Step2Roof.tsx:2172-2222` with a "Reset to uniform" escape, drawn as a **true polygon-clipping inset** (`insetPolygonRobust`, cached, `:2792-2801,2846-2855`) — not a circle approximation | Indian terraces have a parapet on two sides and a neighbour's wall on the third. One uniform setback throws away real kWp |
| **Site calibration from a known distance, plus north offset** — theirs exists only inside the reference-drawing overlay | Two-click measure `screens/Step2Roof.tsx:1537-1564`, `CalibrateDialog` `:3203-3290`, applied as **one undoable patch that rescales all geometry** `:1572-1587`, carried through the canvas at `components/SatCanvas.tsx:105` | Satellite scale is wrong often enough that a surveyor's tape measure is the tie-breaker. One dimension fixes the whole site |
| **Exact vertex coordinates** — they offer edge lengths only | `RoofVertexInspector` in east/north metres, commit on blur/Enter: `screens/Step2Roof.tsx:2602-2690` | The C&I customer has the shed's setting-out drawing. Typing the coordinates beats tracing them |
| **A destructive-change impact dialog** | "This roof change affects N items placed in a later design step", itemised as panels / obstructions / walkways / rails / arresters / inverter placements: `screens/Step2Roof.tsx:574+` and `:1855-1883` | Losing two hours of placement to one dragged vertex is how a tool gets abandoned |
| **Covering and shape as separate facts** — their seven "roof types" conflate covering (RCC Flat, Metal Shed), shape (Gable, Hip) and structure (Carport, Ground Mount) in one list | `types.ts:140-158` documents the split; `roofType` threaded through `lib/roof-factory.ts:129` → `lib/roof-gable.ts:112`, `lib/roof-hip.ts`, `lib/roof-skeleton.ts:281` | Convert a metal shed to a gable in their tool and it silently re-prices from clamps to hooks. Ours does not |
| **Mangalore / clay tile as a covering** — absent from their seven types entirely | `types.ts:158 'tile'`, card at `screens/Step2Roof.tsx:1935-1949` ("panels flush-mounted on adjustable tile hooks"), and its own hardware line — tile hooks + lead/EPDM flashing vs L-feet + PU sealant — at `lib/bom/emitters/mechanical.ts:73-112,378-419` | Tile is a very large share of Indian residential — coastal Karnataka, Kerala, Goa, Maharashtra. They cannot quote it correctly at all |
| **Reflex-corner roofs without a paywall** — they sell "Auto Roof (any shape)" as PRO | A real straight-skeleton wavefront with edge **and split** events: `lib/skeleton-wavefront.ts:1-14`, gated on a 1 % area-tiling residual that **refuses with a reason** rather than shipping a wrong roof (`lib/roof-skeleton.ts:298-306`); L, T, plus/cross and U proven in `lib/__tests__/roof-skeleton.test.ts:112,217,235,255` | L-shaped and U-shaped terraces are the Indian norm. Free for us, an upsell for them |
| **Face-group coupling** | Eave height and pitch propagate across sibling faces so the ridge stays level, azimuth stays per-face, and an unbuildable split is refused with the measured error — "The two halves would meet the ridge 0.16 m apart", naming Hip as the fix: `types.ts:201-206`, `lib/roof-face-group.ts`, `lib/roof-gable.ts:136-146` | Nobody builds a roof whose ridge is 160 mm out. Refusing with the number is what an engineer trusts |
| **Auto-recognised rooftop structures** | A roof drawn fully inside another becomes a mumty/stair room stacked above its parent at `parent.heightM + 2.2` (`screens/Step2Roof.tsx:764-770`, `lib/roof-factory.ts:107-111,138`), and the child is refused its own DSM height and re-lifted by its original rise (`lib/roof-map-fit.ts:206-215`) | Every Indian terrace has a mumty and a tank platform. Getting them stacked right is the difference between a shading study and a picture |

**Obstructions and shading**

| What they lack | What we have or could have (file:line) | Why it wins a deal in India |
|---|---|---|
| **What one obstruction costs the plant** | `casterCost` returns modules affected, kWh/yr lost and % of annual yield for a single caster (`lib/energy/report.ts:341-362`), printed **on the object itself**: "shades N modules · costs ≈ X kWh/yr (Y%) — remove it and the plant gains that" (`three/Scene3D.tsx:3105-3116`) | The most persuasive sentence in the product. "Move the water tank and you gain 1,400 units a year" sells the AMC, the resiting and the trust |
| **A capability model** — their physics is binary; their own copy says trees "only cast shadows. They don't block panel placement" | Six capabilities per obstruction (`types.ts:259-281`: `panelsMayCross`, `mustRemainOpenToSky`, `minVerticalClearanceM`, `minHorizontalClearanceM`, `supportsStructuralLoad`, `requiresEngineerConfirmation`), honoured by the fill (`lib/layout.ts:127-133 isBridgedAt`) and by DRC (`lib/structure-edit.ts:232-245`); the bridge is logged with its clearance arithmetic and an engineer flag (`lib/auto-design.ts:192-223`) | Panels **bridging over** a pipe run or a low duct is standard practice on an Indian factory roof. Binary blocking throws that kWp away |
| **Turning an obstruction into a rooftop platform** | `obstructionToPlatform` (`lib/roof-factory.ts`, wired `screens/Step3Obstructions.tsx:700-728`) makes the object a real Roof at its own top height, so stacking, shading, structure and BOM all flow normally | The tank platform *is* the best south-facing surface on half of Indian terraces |
| **A parent surface re-resolved from position on every move** — they ask the user to pick the parent | One `update()` re-runs `resolveAnchorRoofId` on every move (`screens/Step3Obstructions.tsx:130-146` → `lib/ground.ts:17-19` → `lib/roof-topology.ts:123-136`), with the bug it prevents written into the comment | An anchor cannot go stale, so vents cannot end up levitating over a pitched roof in front of a customer |
| **Live reconcile on placement** | Placing or moving an obstruction switches off the modules under it **in the same undoable patch** (`screens/Step3Obstructions.tsx:145,169,187,194` via `withObstructions`) | No "re-run the fill" step, no forgetting to |
| **A model-vs-map height cross-check in words** | `lib/surround-check.ts:24-45` emits `roof_height_vs_map` when model and DSM disagree by >1.5 m, naming the direction and the consequence ("it sets the shade, the cable drops and the structure"); repeated on the 3D card (`three/Scene3D.tsx:2976-2985`) | Catches the single most damaging survey typo before it reaches the quote |
| **Per-module shade attribution** — no equivalent string anywhere in their bundle | Every caster that takes beam from a module, named, ranked, with its % cost and **clickable to fly the camera at it**: `lib/shading.ts:361-439`, `components/PanelYieldCard.tsx:120-144` ("Sun lost to — click to look") | Turns a shading complaint into a two-second demonstration |
| **String-level electrical shading with bypass diodes** — PVsyst's "electrical shading according to strings"; they model per-panel only | `lib/string-shade.ts:37-47,49-78,85-98` | The gap between a linear shading loss and an electrical one is often 3–5 % of annual yield. That is the whole margin on a residential job |
| **A sun-path chart over the site's own measured horizon** | Near skyline from the DSM + the project's own roofs and obstructions, far hills from PVGIS, **centre and worst corner**, shade windows in clock time as English sentences: `three/SunChart.tsx:158-268`, `lib/sun-chart.ts:139-249,257-269` | The one screen a technically-minded customer stares at |
| **Modules shading modules, measured** | Inter-row self-shading raycast at three sample points along module depth so a row shadow resolves as a fraction, not a step: `lib/shading.ts:128-142,205-211`; the Tier-1 GCR derate was **removed** because of it (`lib/energy/report.ts:191-196`) | Analytical GCR curves under-count exactly where money is lost |
| **Twelve months, 04:00–20:00** | `lib/shading.ts:54-74` — all 12 months at 30-minute steps, off the main thread (`lib/analysis-client.ts:30-34`); the comment records that the old 08:00–17:00 window made sunrise and sunset invisible, "and that blind spot is precisely where inter-row shading lives" | Long low-sun shadows are the ground-mount loss. A narrow window systematically under-counts them, and the customer finds out in year one |
| **Tracker-aware shading** — the sun as the outer loop, so every tracker plate is re-posed before any ray is cast | `lib/shading.ts:250-268` + `lib/energy/tracker.ts` | Without it a tracker yield figure is fiction |
| **Local mean solar time from the site's own longitude**, with an offline IANA table | `lib/sim-time.ts:16-18`, `lib/sun.ts:59-67`, `lib/sun-chart.ts:17-76` | A Guwahati project and a Pune project are both right, and a design opened from another timezone does not shift its shadows |
| **A received-irradiance layer with a provenance badge** | Absolute kWh/m²·month on the heatmap carrying "Real · PVGIS", kept separate from the geometric access metric: `lib/solar-heatmap.ts:290-296`, `three/Scene3D.tsx:2088-2103` | Two different questions, two honestly separated answers |

**Layout, structure and the editor**

| What they lack | What we have or could have (file:line) | Why it wins a deal in India |
|---|---|---|
| **Row pitch solved from the sun** — theirs is a typed Row Pitch, graded afterwards, or a checkbox captioned "Prevents shading on Dec 21" | `lib/spacing.ts:23-51 shadowFreePitchM` sweeps the true sun position at half-hour steps across the winter-solstice 09:00–15:00 window at the project's own lat/lng, hemisphere-correct (`:39`), and `lib/layout.ts:246-263` makes it the **default** for every tilted flat or ground fill; Recommended pitch, GCR and **"Rows as built"** with its measured GCR at `screens/Step6Editor.tsx:2204-2222` | Row pitch is where an Indian EPC loses either kWp or December yield. Solving it removes the argument |
| **Roof ranking by real expected yield** — theirs is a drag-to-prioritise list, i.e. a human guess | `lib/auto-design.ts:64-115 rankRoofs` runs up to 12 candidate panels per roof through the raycast shading engine **and** transposes tilt/azimuth through `poaFactor`, then sorts by `access × poa`; the comment at `:56-60` names the failure it fixes — an unshaded north face reads ~100 % access and yields far less than a south one | On a multi-shed site this is several percent of annual yield, decided automatically and correctly |
| **A machine-generated decision log** — they narrate progress, never a decision | `lib/auto-design.ts:139-271` emits a `DesignDecision` per choice with its numeric `inputs` (`capacityKwp=…`, `beamAccess=…`, `orientationPoa=…`, `tilt=…°`, `latitude=…`), rendered verbatim at `screens/Step5AutoDesign.tsx:148-166` and in Step 6's "Why this layout?" (`screens/Step6Editor.tsx:1911-1943`) | "Why did it do that?" is the question that decides whether the engineer trusts the auto button |
| **Auto-string and auto-route in the same undo step** — they require a separate manual `Confirm & String` | `lib/ops/run.ts:20-24` folds `syncElectrical` into the op's own patch, so one run produces panels + segments + strings + `cableRoutes` as one undoable step (`lib/derive/electrical-sync.ts:35-56`) | Fewer steps, and no half-designed state to quote from |
| **Live previews that can refuse** | Every 3D manipulation is a preview of a typed operation: ghost + `summarizeImpact` readout, or **`Refused — {reason}`** held on screen for 2.2 s so the table does not just snap back unexplained (`three/Gizmos.tsx:404-408,479-500,270-300,334-339`); the 2D drag ghost paints the exact modules that will land, already avoiding obstructions/walkways/keepouts/setbacks, with a `rows × cols × modules × kWp` readout (`screens/Step6Editor.tsx:3012-3026,3702-3739`) | Direct manipulation that explains itself is what "easy" actually means on a touchscreen |
| **A parametric member model in the editor** | Leg / rafter / purlin / brace counts and metres, steel kg by section, structural DRC and a standing engineer disclaimer: `screens/Step6Editor.tsx:2269-2344`, profiles carrying `{kgPerM}` at `:2264`; per-table **leg-plan editor** at `three/StructEditPanel.tsx:519-552` → `three/LegPlanEditor.tsx` | Their "Show/Hide mounting frame" is a decoration toggle. Ours is the thing that gets fabricated |
| **Foundations as buildable assemblies** | `lib/foundation.ts` — pedestal / ballast / pile / chemical anchor built as parts (body, 12 mm grout bed, base plate, four cast-in M12 bolts) in the leg's local frame; a square-vs-circular volume branch that matters by 21 %; `foundationTooTall` as a DRC failure (`:98-106`); the D15 height contract (`:13-25`) keeps the module plane fixed when the footing kind changes, so switching foundations never silently moves shading, energy and every stored capture | Their equivalent BOM line is "Concrete + Chemical Anchor". Ours prints concrete m³ **and** the kilograms of dead load added to the roof |
| **Loose modules promoted into a real table** | `screens/Step6Editor.tsx:566-583` → `lib/ops/layout-ops.ts:114-135` → `lib/segment-ops.ts:47 groupIntoTable` | Hand-placed panels become billable racking instead of orphans |
| **Hand-editable DC routes on the plan** | Grab a corner, or press on the run itself to create a corner and drag it in one gesture, committed as a single undo step, then marked hand-routed so auto-stringing leaves it alone: `screens/Step6Editor.tsx:909-947,3267-3311` | The cable run the site actually allows, not the one the algorithm imagined |
| **A layout lock** | `MUTATING_TOOLS` + a `locked` guard on all fourteen mutating handlers, with one explanation: `screens/Step6Editor.tsx:182-190,432-434,449-458` | Approved designs stop drifting between the sale and the site |
| **Placeable enclosures** | Battery cabinets and DCDB/ACDB are physical objects, drawn on the plan and draggable along walls in 3D: `screens/Step6Editor.tsx:1695-1717,768-779,3370-3386,3510-3523`, `three/Gizmos.tsx:591-760 WallGizmo`. Their DCDB/ACDB exist only as BOM lines | Where the boxes go decides the cable metres, which decides the price |
| **Measured cable metres from the placement** — they ask the user for `dcCableRunM` and `acCableRunM` as "smart answers" | `lib/routing.ts:46-53` derives DC and AC vertical drops from the roof height and the placed inverter's own mounting height; `:480-497` routes each home run to that inverter and stamps `assumedTarget: true` when no placement exists, which **downgrades the BOM line to `estimated`** (`lib/bom/emitters/electrical.ts:95-96`) | A typed guess is where the cable cost overrun comes from. We measure geometry, and say so when we cannot |
| **Inverter placement beyond a wall** | Wall, free-standing on a roof stand, or ground level, decided by distance (`screens/Step6Editor.tsx:761-766`, `three/Scene3D.tsx:2935-2941`); the same grammar places DCDB, ACDB, battery and meter (`types.ts:687-699`) | Indian C&I inverters mostly stand on a plinth, not a wall |
| **A validation banner that is an index into the design** | Tap an issue and the offending modules are selected and centred: `screens/Step6Editor.tsx:1247-1275,2377-2399`, over DRC codes `panel_overlap`, `setback_breach`, `panel_in_keepout`, `panel_over_obstruction`, `bridge_clearance`, `foundation_clash`, `foundation_too_tall` | Errors you can reach are errors that get fixed |

**Electrical and drawings**

| What they lack | What we have or could have (file:line) | Why it wins a deal in India |
|---|---|---|
| **A cold-Voc max-system-voltage check on the sheet** | Derived from the **longest real string** × `vocAt(panel, temps.minCellC)` (`lib/sld.ts:26-34`), drawn green/red with "OVER LIMIT — shorten the string" (`screens/Step8Sld.tsx:436-457`) | The one number a DISCOM inspector and an insurer both look at |
| **Naming an impossible panel+inverter pair** | `lib/electrical/window.ts:64-71` sets `impossible` with the full sentence — proved live on Adani ELAN Shine 540 + Amaze AN HKVA 30 KVA-360V: 16 in series when hot, only 14 fit when cold, so all 185 modules stay unstrung **and it says why** | Better an honest refusal than a design that cannot be built, discovered on site |
| **Grouping rules for what may share a series circuit** — theirs is an opaque "Auto String" | Roof **plane** (co-planar union-find, not roofId), azimuth ±5°, tilt ±3°, shade tier, plus an MLPE mega-group; serpentine ordering in the roof's own grid frame so the string reads the way the cable runs: `lib/electrical/grouping.ts:1-20,312-340` | Mixed-orientation Indian roofs. Getting this wrong costs yield forever |
| **PVsyst-style MPPT assignment** | Largest-string-first, one tracker per string while trackers are free, identical strings only in parallel, lowest-DC-load inverter first: `lib/electrical/balance.ts:1-22` | The design an experienced engineer would have drawn by hand |
| **Cables sized by physics, not a lookup** — theirs is `DC mm² by kWp: ≤10 → 4 · ≤50 → 6 · else 10` and `AC 3-phase: ≤15 → 10 · ≤30 → 16 · ≤60 → 25 · ≤120 → 35 · else 50` | DC: fuse from `iscA × fuseFactor` up a standard ladder, size from the larger of ampacity and **drop over the real routed loop**, `governedBy` named, one BOM line per size (`lib/electrical-sizing.ts:16-60,70-86`, `lib/electrical/dc-cable.ts:67-93`). AC: breaker coordination **and** drop over the routed run (`lib/electrical-sizing.ts:194-216`) | Copper is the second-biggest line in an Indian BOM. A lookup either over-buys it or fails the drop limit |
| **A string-combiner model drawn on the sheet** — theirs is the note "Strings will be grouped into SCBs (20 strings/box)" | Evenly-loaded SCBs with a real gPV fuse per string and a hard reconciliation gate (Σ inputs === totalStrings): `lib/electrical/combiner.ts:33-69`, drawn `screens/Step8Sld.tsx:364,413-436`, priced `lib/bom/emitters/electrical.ts:232` | C&I central-inverter topology is where the money is |
| **A 3-line diagram toggle** — theirs is single-line only | Every conductor drawn and named (L1/L2/L3/N/PE vs L/N/PE) from one phase rule: `lib/sld.ts:112-118`, `screens/Step8Sld.tsx:483-500` | Some DISCOM submissions ask for it |
| **A Cable Schedule sheet** | Every routed run as a buyable line — tag, ends, conductor, roof metres, wall drop, slack, metres to buy — with honest truncation at 34 rows while the totals still sum every run: `screens/CableScheduleSheet.tsx:1-4,111-175` | The purchase order writes itself |
| **A Structure sheet with the foundation in section** | A3 sheet, isometric per table, footings at the leg bases, and a **typical section of one leg pair** with the resolved `foundationAssembly` drawn in section plus a title block naming the foundation and its shape: `components/drawing/StructureSheet.tsx:53-61,64-93,95-102,133-138` | Their equivalent is a "Typical Table Arrangement" detail block |
| **A layout DXF that carries setting-out data** — their documented 14-layer DXF is on the SLD; their layout DXF layer set is not evidenced | 13 named layers in true metres including `STRUCT-MEMBERS`, `STRUCT-LEGS` ("setting-out points"), `STRUCT-FOOTINGS` ("what gets cast/placed"), walkways and arresters/earth electrodes: `lib/export-dxf.ts:17-63,96-98,131-134`; it draws **both** the series order and the routed conductor polylines (`:102-129`), where the printed sheet draws only the series order | The fabricator and the site engineer work from this file |
| **SLD parameters stored as a diff against live defaults** | `diffSldOverrides` stores only what differs, so untouched ratings keep tracking a component change; the header shows the override count: `lib/sld.ts:1-6,87-100`, `screens/Step8Sld.tsx:99-121`. Their evidenced control is "Reset to calculated values" | Change the inverter and the drawing follows, except where a human deliberately overrode it |
| **An on-object string card** — theirs is `String {{num}}` + `INV {{inv}} · MPPT {{mppt}}` | Modules, INV/MPPT, Imp, cold Voc vs the inverter limit, hot Vmp vs the MPPT window, OK/PROBLEM with the reason, routed home-run metres + mm² + drop % + over-limit flag, live Pnom ratio, and the string's shade split (linear / worst module / electrical) — with actions Wire by hand · Cable run · Re-plan all strings · Un-wire: `three/Electrical.tsx:457-524` | Every question an engineer has about a string, on the string |
| **An inverter Bay view** | Where every string landed and where the next goes — MPPT pips, DC kWp vs AC rating, string count, and a `~` flag when the box is not physically placed (because an unplaced inverter cannot measure its cable run): `components/InverterBay.tsx:1-11,95-135` | Makes a 60-string C&I job legible |

**BOM, quote and the document that leaves the building**

| What they lack | What we have or could have (file:line) | Why it wins a deal in India |
|---|---|---|
| **A derivation on every line** | `formula` is required on every constructed line (`lib/bom/line.ts:15-33`) and exported as a CSV column (`lib/bom.ts:187,213`). Real examples from the golden snapshot: "Member model: A1: 12 legs 4.2m, 6 rafters 13.7m, 4 purlins 14m, 4 braces 7m" and "Routed home runs 41 m + 12 m inter-row hops (incl. 10% slack, 1.5 m drop/run) · Conductor 6 sq.mm for 3 strings: governed by VOLTAGE DROP on 2 of them" | The procurement team stops phoning to ask where a number came from |
| **Live re-derivation** — their whole `...AtBurn` stamp mechanism (`stringsAtBurn`, `kwpAtBurn`, `panelCountAtBurn`) exists to solve staleness | `lib/bom.ts:53-58 deriveBom` re-runs the seven emitters over a fresh context on every read, memoised on the design fingerprint rather than a persisted document (`lib/derive/outputs.ts:34`). **A non-overridden line cannot be stale** | Their architecture has a bug class ours does not have |
| **Three-number staleness** — theirs re-takes the computed value behind an `hasAutoRefresh` badge | What you typed, what the engine said when you typed it, and what it says now — with a per-field "Take {value}": `lib/bom/merge.ts:172-181`, `types.ts:970-978`, `screens/Step9Bom/StaleBanner.tsx:84-103` | The estimator keeps their override on purpose, not by accident |
| **Orphan rescue** | An edit whose line the design no longer produces is surfaced with keep-as-custom / discard rather than silently dropped: `lib/bom/merge.ts:43-60,144-168`, `screens/Step9Bom/OrphanBanner.tsx` | A hand-priced special does not vanish when the layout changes |
| **Per-line GST with pro-rata discount apportionment** — theirs defaults to a flat 5 % on every line | `lib/bom/money.ts:1-24` documents why margin sits **below** GST (same total, different declared taxable value); `:136-187` apportions one project discount across the 5 % and 18 % buckets pro-rata; `:117` and `screens/Step9Bom/index.tsx:292-302` warn on a below-cost discount | Modules are 5 %, most BOS is 18 %. A flat rate is wrong on the invoice, and the invoice is the legal document |
| **Design quantity vs order quantity** | Two distinct columns with discrete units ceiled (`lib/bom/registry.ts:140-173`, `lib/bom/money.ts:36-40`); the comment at `registry.ts:143` names the failure: "The reference tool prints 116.15 Nos" | Nobody buys 116.15 clamps |
| **A real steel member model behind the MMS lines** — theirs is two bundled items, "MMS — RCC Flat" and "MMS — Metal Shed" | One line per structural profile, spec'd "HDG C-Channel · 80 × 40 × 8 × 1.6 · 2.2 kg/m · IS 2062 · HDG ≥ 80 µm", with a per-segment member breakdown in the formula: `lib/bom/emitters/mechanical.ts:196-228` | Steel is the largest non-module line. Two bundled items is a guess; kilograms by profile is a purchase order |
| **Metal-shed penetration accounting** | Standoffs counted off the node graph and exactly one EPDM sealing washer per hole — "Every fixing is a hole in the roof": `lib/bom/emitters/mechanical.ts:473-499` | Roof leaks after a monsoon are the number-one after-sales complaint on shed jobs |
| **Free-field site works from real geometry** | Perimeter fencing at the **measured** boundary, a gate per area and a buried earthing ring at the same perimeter, all stamped `assumed` because fence type is a client decision: `lib/bom/emitters/safety.ts:126-182`; walkways in m² and rails in m from drawn geometry at `:10-39`. They draw walkways, rails and lifelines on the roof at step 5 but **none of the three appears in their 10 BOM categories** | Fencing on a leased ground-mount site is lakhs. Leaving it out is how a job goes underwater |
| **Site-dependent scope emitted at qty 0 and excluded** | Crane/hydra, scaffolding, civil works and trenching ship as zero-quantity excluded prompts (`lib/bom/emitters/civil.ts:34-82`), with the reasoning in the file: "Omitting them silently is how they end up missing from the quote and eaten as margin" | The estimator sees the cost they forgot instead of discovering it on site |
| **A customer narrative with per-sentence traceability** | Every beat in `lib/proposal-narrative.ts` carries `facts: (string\|number)[]` — the raw project values it was built from — and sections with no real data are **omitted rather than padded** (`:31`); rendered as "YOUR SOLAR STORY" (`screens/ProposalView.tsx:218-236`) | A proposal with no invented sentences survives a technical review |
| **A "PROVISIONAL — not for issue" stamp that survives Print** | `components/FreshnessBanner.tsx:24-46` — under `print` the banner keeps its class instead of `.no-print`, and it is mounted twice on the proposal (`screens/ProposalView.tsx:102` and again inside page 1 at `:164`) so the physical first sheet carries it | The single strongest trust signal in the product, and it is on paper |
| **A pre-issue readiness review with jump-to-step** | Four signals — electrical / design review / quantity confidence / imagery — each carrying its `step`, with a per-item jump button: `lib/review.ts`, `screens/Step7Proposal.tsx:187-229` | Catches the half-finished quote before it is emailed |
| **Capture staleness with "Outdated — retake"** — they ship `Retake` but no staleness detection | Each capture is stamped with the layout fingerprint it was taken against (`lib/fingerprints.ts:334-343`); a warm badge appears on any capture the design has moved past, and the user promotes any capture to Cover rather than accepting the first one saved (`screens/Step7Proposal.tsx:262-289,320-348`) | The cover image matches the design being quoted |
| **A customer document and an engineering document** | The engineering doc prints the full chain — cost + margin % → taxable-before-discount − discount → taxable + GST = system cost (`screens/ProposalView.tsx:433-457`, with the in-file note that the short version was arithmetically false once GST went per-line); the customer doc prints the **one** final price (`:460`) | Two audiences, one arithmetic, no contradiction |
| **Losses, monsoon-split monthly chart and stated irradiance provenance on the customer document** | Monthly bars coloured by `r.monsoonMonths`, printing either "Monthly shape from measured PVGIS climate (real monsoon variation)" or "built-in seasonal model (estimate, ±10%)": `screens/ProposalView.tsx:291-321,305-309` | The monsoon dip is the first thing an Indian customer asks about. Showing it measured pre-empts the "you over-promised" conversation in year one |
| **Structural honesty on the customer document** — they sell "Detailed Structure Analysis" as a paid tier with no disclaimer travelling with the output | Engineering status label, `STRUCTURE_DISCLAIMER`, the state's wind-zone note and the engineer's notes, printed on the pricing page (`screens/ProposalView.tsx:400-409`); recorded sign-off state `pending` / `engineer_approved` at `screens/Step8Sld.tsx:126-147`; `drawingFootnote(project)` stamped on every sheet (`:617,746,808`) | Protects the EPC legally and reads as competence, not weakness |
| **An electrical page folded into the proposal** | `screens/ProposalView.tsx:468-513`, guarded at `:92-93`, reading the same `effectiveSld(project)` as the Step-8 sheet so the two can never print different ratings | One document to the DISCOM and the customer |
| **An Installation Plan handed over at Final** — their Step 10 is "Save, generate PDF, Done & Return to Proposal" | `screens/Step10Done.tsx:67-69` → `screens/InstallationSheet.tsx` from `lib/installation`. **Caveat (`final-critique.md §3.4`): rooftop tables only** — `lib/installation.ts` filters `roof_anchor`, `front_leg`, `back_leg`, `rafter`, `purlin`, `brace` (lines 97, 114, 130, 145) and emits **no `rail`, no `sheet_standoff`**, so a metal-shed monorail work order never mentions fixing the rails or drilling the roof while the BOM bills both. Effort **S**, payoff **high** | The site team gets a work order instead of a WhatsApp voice note — once the monorail path is fixed |
| **Undo/Redo in the wizard shell on all ten steps**, labelled with the operation to be undone | `screens/Wizard.tsx:145-183` + Cmd/Ctrl+Z at `:219-237`. Theirs is per-action toasts inside Step 2 and Undo/Redo inside Step 5 only | Confidence to experiment, on every screen |
| **A Design Health score with a band and an explanation sheet** | `screens/Wizard.tsx:196-203,320-358` over `memoizedHealth` / `describeHealthCode` | One glance tells the salesperson whether this design can be sent |
| **Per-step help with operating content** — theirs is `aboutStep: "About: {{step}}"` and a YouTube link | `screens/Wizard.tsx:37-108 STEP_HELP` gives every step a purpose paragraph plus concrete tips, including the full Step 6 shortcut set | Onboards a new surveyor without a video call |
| **Duplicate-project, and an honest quarantine banner for unreadable projects** | `screens/Dashboard.tsx:78-86` (name de-duplication) and `:103-119` (quarantine alert naming the recovery keys); per-project quarantine, quota failures reported not swallowed, a versioned v1→v2 migration keeping the v1 payload, base64→IndexedDB hoisting and cross-tab merge at `lib/persistence/repository.ts:1-7`. Theirs shows only `reslink_plan_cache_v1` and "Loaded from cache (offline)" | One corrupt project never wipes its siblings. Add a service worker on top and we beat them on offline too |
| **m/ft on every step** — theirs is evidenced only inside Step 1 drawing aids | `screens/Wizard.tsx:359-362 UnitToggle` in the shell | Procurement stays metric regardless, per house rule |

**3D and the shared viewer**

| What they lack | What we have or could have (file:line) | Why it wins a deal in India |
|---|---|---|
| **Any physically-based lighting** — "No tone mapping, no env map / IBL, no PMREM, no post-processing stack found in the app bundle" | ACES filmic tone mapping, HDR half-float buffer, N8AO ambient occlusion and SMAA (`three/ScenePost.tsx:33-45`, `three/Scene3D.tsx:1409-1411`); a drei `<Environment>` with three Lightformer rects so every PBR surface has something to reflect (`:2746-2770`); a live sky driven by the real sun direction, exponential haze and stars below the horizon (`:2801-2811`, `:177-194`) | Their realism is props plus a satellite texture. Ours is light. Side by side on a laptop, this is the demo |
| **Physically correct module glass** — theirs is `meshStandardMaterial` with glass at `roughness .12 / metalness .55` (glass is not 55 % metal) | `MeshPhysicalMaterial({ metalness: 0, roughness: 0.08, clearcoat: 0.5, clearcoatRoughness: 0.06, envMapIntensity: 0.2 })` at `three/textures.ts:214-224` | The panels look like panels |
| **A module face drawn from the datasheet** | Cell count from module length, cell colour and busbar count from technology, half-cut centre gap, per-cell tone wobble, fingers and ribbons at true scale, per orientation, cached per spec: `three/textures.ts:40-42,97-158,161-171`. Swap a 540 W Adani for a 585 W TOPCon and **the picture changes correctly** | The customer recognises the module they were quoted |
| **A shadow map above 2048** — theirs is `shadowMapSize: 2048` | 4096 (`three/Scene3D.tsx:2838-2839`), frustum fitted at `:2719`. **Caveat (`final-critique.md §3.3`): `three/scene-bounds.ts:22-56` fits to roofs and panels only — obstructions never enter the bounds**, which the 3D diff ranks P-ORDER 2 critical. Fix that before this is a clean claim. Effort **S**, payoff **high** | Four times the texels on the one thing the customer looks at — once the bounds include the objects casting the shadows |
| **Instanced modules** | One InstancedMesh per orientation for glass, frames and legs, with per-instance colour writes for selection/hover and no rebuild: `three/PanelsInstanced.tsx:131-152,216-227`. A 500-module C&I array is 3–4 draw calls | It stays smooth on the mid-range Android a surveyor actually carries |
| **Camera control in the shared viewer** — theirs offers "switch 3D ↔ top view" | Top / Iso / Front / Back / Left / Right presets plus Fly, Isolate, Walk and Fullscreen, with distance / angle / area / elevation measurement: `three/Scene3D.tsx:1252-1341`, `three/Measure.tsx`. **Caveat: presets recompute from the bounding sphere on every mount (`three/Scene3D.tsx:444-455`), so there is no camera persistence and no saved/named views** — "the angle I show customers" cannot be stored. Effort **S**, payoff **medium** | The customer explores the design themselves instead of asking for another render |
| **On-object contextual cards across nine pickable kinds** | `three/EntityLabel.tsx` (90 lines) mounted at 10 sites covering all nine `ScenePick` kinds (`three/scene-pick.ts:10-21`). **This already is the card the owner asked for** — the remaining work is mounting it in the 2D SVG canvases and choosing a trigger (`DESIGN-SYSTEM §7.2` already specifies long-press for touch), not building a component. Effort **M**, payoff **high** | Their contextual handles ride the object in **2D**; ours ride it in 3D. Mounting ours in 2D takes the lead outright — and takes the `casterCost` sentence to the users who never open 3D |
| **A structure that is always drawn from a real model** — theirs is a `Show/Hide mounting frame` toggle | `<StructureInstanced>` + `<StructureNodesInstanced>` render unconditionally whenever a panel spec exists (`three/Scene3D.tsx:3144-3150`), editable for foundation, shuttering, purlins, rafters, overhang, bracing and leg plan | A switch on a decoration versus a billed engineering model |
| **A written internal design standard** | `docs/DESIGN-SYSTEM.md` (562 lines): N1–N10 non-negotiables at `:36-45`, the touch/pointer parity contract §7.1-7.4 at `:291-341`, density doctrine §6, accessibility contract §9, anti-patterns §11, definition of done §13. Plus focus-trap / Escape / focus-restore on every modal (`components/ui.tsx:60-67,83,120`), 8 `aria-live` regions, and reduced-motion honoured globally including on legacy screens (`src/design/index.css:230-236`) | Not a sales line — the reason we can fix the mobile gap fast and they cannot audit theirs at all |

**One count the owner will ask for:** of 11 `ObstructionType` values, **6 have a GLB**, one of those six (`windmill`) points at a folder holding only `.gitkeep`, and `elevated`, `ladder` and `other` fall back to a grey box (`three/ObstructionMesh.tsx:255-270`). ResLink ships **17 types plus 4 skylight sub-shapes** with real GLBs preloaded from `/design/models/`. On 3D object *coverage* we are behind; on 3D *rendering* we are a generation ahead. Effort **M** to close (asset work, not code), payoff **high**.
