## The ten things we already do better

Read these aloud. Every "Theirs" cell is a quoted string or a proven absence from their public
bundle.

| What | Ours (file:line) | Theirs |
|---|---|---|
| **1. We hand the customer six costed options, not one opinion.** Six candidate panels, each run through the whole pipeline — roof fill, auto-string, 8,760-hour energy, BOM, finance — and each row shows annual kWh, net cost after subsidy, payback, 25-year savings, ROI %, DC/AC ratio, whether it can even be strung, array weight and both warranties, with a written reason for the winner | `lib/comparison.ts` (439 lines) — shortlist `:206-227`, build `:93-133`, compare `:234-330` (energy + finance + `validateSystem` per row), ranking `:328-340`; rendered `screens/Step4Components.tsx:382-559` | Nothing of the kind at their Step 3. Their sizing help is a capacity number and `inverter ≈ {{kw}} kW AC` |
| **2. Our energy number is a simulation, not seven percentages.** A real 8,760-hour typical-meteorological-year run: Perez anisotropic sky, Kasten-Young air mass, ASHRAE incidence-angle losses, Huld low-light efficiency, Faiman module temperature from air temp and wind, a real inverter efficiency curve with clipping counted in hours **and** kWh — and it comes out as **P50 / P75 / P90 / P99** with the uncertainty decomposed into the site's own measured year-to-year spread and the model's own error | `lib/energy/hourly.ts:108-146,183-415,291-303,335-339,360-370`; `lib/energy/report.ts:38-78`; `components/EnergyReportSheet.tsx:88-96` | Their loss table is **seven editable percentages**. The bundle proves **no P50/P75/P90** |
| **3. We tell the customer what their water tank costs them, in units, on the tank.** Click the object and it reads: *"shades 6 modules · costs ≈ 1,410 kWh/yr (2.4%) — remove it and the plant gains that"* | `lib/energy/report.ts:341-362 casterCost`; printed on the 3D object card at `three/Scene3D.tsx:3105-3116`; per-module attribution ranked and clickable-to-fly at `lib/shading.ts:361-439` and `components/PanelYieldCard.tsx:120-144` | No equivalent string anywhere in their bundle. Their obstruction physics is binary — their own copy says trees *"only cast shadows. They don't block panel placement"* |
| **4. Every line in our BOM shows its working and says how much to trust it.** A human-readable formula is **required** on every line — *"Routed home runs 41 m + 12 m inter-row hops (incl. 10% slack, 1.5 m drop/run) · Conductor 6 sq.mm for 3 strings: governed by VOLTAGE DROP on 2 of them"* — and every line carries one of four tiers (measured / derived / estimated / assumed), rolled up to a quote badge with a named list of what still needs site verification, exported as its own CSV column | `lib/bom/line.ts:15-33` (`formula` non-optional, `types.ts:926`); tiers `types.ts:917-939`; roll-up `lib/bom.ts:87-118`; dot + screen-reader label `screens/Step9Bom/BomRow.tsx:17-29`; CSV `lib/bom.ts:187,212-213`. *(Scope: this is the BOM. Roof-height provenance still mis-stamps in three places — `lib/roof-factory.ts:131-153`, `lib/roof-map-fit.ts:100-102`, `lib/roof-ai/gemini-client.ts:82-85`.)* | Nothing in their bundle labels the trust of a figure. Their MMS is two bundled items, "MMS — RCC Flat" and "MMS — Metal Shed"; their GST defaults to a flat 5 % on every line |
| **5. We can quote four product categories they cannot quote at all.** Battery with DC-hybrid vs AC-coupled and its own BOM category. Bifacial with rear-side irradiance solved from the actual mounting geometry and surface-specific albedo. Single-axis tracker with true backtracking, offered only on ground and refused on a roof. DC optimisers that change what may share a string, not just the parts list | Battery `screens/Step4Battery.tsx`, `lib/bom/emitters/battery.ts:12-72`; bifacial `lib/energy/bifacial.ts:60-72,275-338`; tracker `lib/energy/tracker.ts:1-60`, `lib/ops/layout-ops.ts:195`, `lib/bom/emitters/mechanical.ts:548-625`; optimisers `types.ts:419-424`, `lib/electrical/grouping.ts`, `lib/bom/emitters/electrical.ts:216-229` | The bundle proves: *"No battery / storage. … No optimiser or micro-inverter modelling (only string vs central). No bifacial. No tracker."* |
| **6. Our roofs come off the aerial map already measured — and nothing enters the project unreviewed.** Detect from a pin, get height, pitch and azimuth measured from Google's height map, review them as ghosts with imagery quality, imagery date, detector confidence, a ±0.5 m alignment nudge and per-shape accept. Three taps from pin to placed roof. Plus per-edge setbacks drawn as a true clipping inset, tile as a first-class covering, and a straight-skeleton engine that tiles L, T, U and plus shapes exactly or **refuses with the reason** | `lib/roof-ai/pipeline.ts`, `lib/roof-ai/plane-fit.ts`, review UI `screens/Step2Roof.tsx:1447-1533`; setbacks `types.ts:198`, `screens/Step2Roof.tsx:2172-2222`; tile `types.ts:158`; skeleton `lib/skeleton-wavefront.ts:1-14`, `lib/roof-skeleton.ts:298-306`, proven `lib/__tests__/roof-skeleton.test.ts:112,217,235,255` | The bundle proves *"No AI roof detection"*. They sell "Auto Roof (any shape)" as a **PRO** upsell, offer **one uniform** `Edge Setback (m)`, and have **no tile** among their seven roof types |
| **7. The neighbours actually shade the design.** We fetch Google's Solar height map at 100 m, cut out the site's own roofs, and hand it to the shading engine as real geometry the sun rays hit — so the tall block next door costs the yield it really costs. One switch hides it **and** removes it from the numbers together, so the picture and the figure can never disagree | `lib/surround.ts:20-27`; caster `lib/scene-model.ts:305-323`; single toggle `three/Scene3D.tsx:1190-1198`; model-vs-map height cross-check in plain words `lib/surround-check.ts:24-45` | The bundle proves *"No LIDAR or photogrammetry import"* and *"every obstruction is hand-placed"*. Their neighbours are a hand-drawn "Nearby Building" box |
| **8. Our electrical refuses to be wrong, and says why.** It will not draw an SLD from unstrung panels — it offers "Try again" or "String manually" instead of fabricating an impossible all-panels-in-series drawing. It names a panel + inverter pair that has **no legal series length** rather than returning a nonsense window. It puts a cold-Voc max-system-voltage check on the sheet in green or red. And it sizes every cable per string against the fuse **and** the voltage drop over the real routed loop, naming which criterion governed | Gate `screens/Step8Sld.tsx:270-276`; impossible window `lib/electrical/window.ts:64-71` (proved live: Adani ELAN Shine 540 + Amaze 30 KVA-360V needs 16 in series hot, fits 14 cold); max system voltage `lib/sld.ts:26-34` → `screens/Step8Sld.tsx:436-457`; sizing `lib/electrical-sizing.ts:16-60,70-86,194-216` | Their cable rule is a lookup: *"DC cable mm² by kWp: ≤10 → 4 · ≤50 → 6 · else 10"* and *"3-phase: ≤15 → 10 · ≤30 → 16 · ≤60 → 25 · ≤120 → 35 · else 50"*. Their SLD step is explicitly skippable (*"or skip this step"*) and their BOM ships a `Skip this step` button |
| **9. A price we have not recomputed prints the words "PROVISIONAL — not for issue" on the paper.** The staleness banner keeps its class under print instead of hiding, and it is mounted twice on the proposal so the physical first sheet carries it. Behind it: freshness stamped across shading, strings and routes and composed into one "may this money be shown as final" answer, a four-signal pre-issue review with jump-to-the-step buttons, "Outdated — retake" on any capture the design has moved past, and the structural disclaimer travelling onto the customer's pricing page | `components/FreshnessBanner.tsx:24-46`; mounted `screens/ProposalView.tsx:102,164`; freshness `lib/derive/freshness.ts:112-135`; review `lib/review.ts` → `screens/Step7Proposal.tsx:187-229`; capture staleness `lib/fingerprints.ts:334-343` → `screens/Step7Proposal.tsx:262-289`; disclaimer `screens/ProposalView.tsx:400-409`. *(Related but weaker than it sounds: the wizard cannot advance past Step 6 until the design strings — `screens/Wizard.tsx:126-136`, `:208-216` — but `/proposal` and `/bom` are ungated top-level routes, `router.ts:49-51`, and `screens/Dashboard.tsx:278` navigates straight to `/proposal`. Half a win until that side door is shut.)* | They enforce shading staleness inside step 5, but the inventory shows **no provisional stamp on the proposal**, no readiness review, no capture-staleness detection and no structural disclaimer travelling with the output |
| **10. The 3D actually looks real, and the module in it is the module you quoted.** ACES filmic tone mapping, an HDR buffer, ambient occlusion and anti-aliasing; image-based lighting so every surface has something to reflect; physically correct clearcoat glass; a live sky, haze and stars driven by the real sun; and a module face **drawn from the datasheet** — cell count from module length, busbars and cell colour from the technology, half-cut centre gap, fingers and ribbons at true scale. Swap a 540 W Adani for a 585 W TOPCon and the picture changes correctly | `three/ScenePost.tsx:33-45`; `three/Scene3D.tsx:1409-1411,2746-2770,2801-2811,177-194`; glass `three/textures.ts:214-224`; module face `three/textures.ts:40-42,97-158,161-171`; instanced draw `three/PanelsInstanced.tsx:131-152`. *(Caveat: our 4096 shadow map — `three/Scene3D.tsx:2838-2839` vs their 2048 — is fitted to bounds that exclude obstructions, `three/scene-bounds.ts:22-56`. Fix before claiming the shadow.)* | *"No tone mapping, no env map / IBL, no PMREM, no post-processing stack found in the app bundle."* Their materials are `meshStandardMaterial` with glass at `roughness .12 / metalness .55` — glass is not 55 % metal. Their realism is props plus a satellite texture |
